\title{Dense minimum-cost flow in $\O(n^2\log n)$ word operations}
\date{November 2, 2026}

\begin{document}
\maketitle

\begin{abstract}
We give a randomized word-RAM algorithm that computes an exact minimum-cost
flow on a directed graph with $n$ vertices, at most one arc per ordered pair,
and polynomially bounded integer capacities, costs, and demands in
$\O(n^2\log n)$ word operations, where $\O(\cdot)$ hides factors of
$(\log\log n)^{O(1)}$.
The running time bound holds on every random tape.
The output is an exact integral flow with an integer dual certificate, or a
verified infeasibility certificate, with probability at least $1-n^{-c_0}$ for
any fixed constant $c_0$, and \textsc{Fail} otherwise.
Every returned answer is checked exactly before it is released.
Dense bipartite minimum-cost perfect matching is a special case.
The dense work per attempt is $\O(n^2)$ because maintained original-edge cores
and packed arithmetic answer the per-frame queries in time proportional to
the number of selected labels, and $O(\log n)$ attempts supply both progress
and confidence.
\end{abstract}

\begin{center}
\small

\end{center}


\section{Main result}
\label{sec:ram27-objective}

\begin{theorem}
\label{thm:ram27-main}
Let $C,c_0>0$ be constants, and let a directed graph on $n\ge2$ vertices be
given with
\begin{itemize}
\item at most one arc per ordered pair of vertices, and
\item integer lower capacities, upper capacities, costs, and demands of
absolute value at most $n^C$.
\end{itemize}
There is a randomized word-RAM algorithm with $O(\log n)$-bit words and
polynomial address space (\Cref{sec:ram27-model}) that uses at most
\[
\O\left(n^2\log n\right)
\]
word operations on every random tape, and returns, with probability at least
$1-n^{-c_0}$, either
\begin{itemize}
\item an exact minimum-cost feasible integral flow together with an integer
dual certificate, or
\item a verified infeasibility certificate, which is a bound inconsistency, a
nonzero total demand, or a violated capacity cut.
\end{itemize}
On the remaining outcomes the algorithm returns \textsc{Fail}.
\end{theorem}

Every returned mathematical answer is checked exactly before it is released,
so the algorithm never returns an incorrect flow, dual, or cut.

\begin{corollary}
\label{cor:ram27-matching}
Let $C,c_0>0$ be constants and let $n\ge2$ be even.
Given integer costs $\abs{\cc_e}\le n^C$ on the edges of $K_{n/2,n/2}$, a
randomized word-RAM algorithm returns, with probability at least
$1-n^{-c_0}$, an exact minimum-cost perfect matching together with an integer
assignment dual certificate, in
\[
\O\left(n^2\log n\right)
\]
word operations in the model of \Cref{sec:ram27-model}, with the same failure
convention as \Cref{thm:ram27-main}.
\end{corollary}

\subsection{Logarithmic factors and constants}
\label{sec:ram27-logs}

Throughout, $\O(\cdot)$ hides factors of $(\log\log n)^{O(1)}$ and nothing
else; every factor of $\log n$ is written out, and $\log m$ is identified
with $\log n$ since $m\le n^2$.
All constants may depend on the input exponent $C$ and the confidence
exponent $c_0$.
Words have $O(\log n)$ bits; the word length is written $\log n$ and its bit
length $\log\log n$, up to the integer rounding fixed once here: an integer
parameter defined by a real expression is that expression rounded up to the
next integer, and a parameter stated as a power of two is rounded up to the
next power of two.
Every such rounding changes constants only and is not written.
The tracking potential exponent is $p=512$, and the central potential
exponent $P$ is the least integer with $P\ge512$ and
\[
P\ge1+\log_2\bigl(3+\log_2(2+n)\bigr),
\]
so that $P=\Theta(\log\log n)$ and $P\ge p$.
Every bound of the form $O(mP^a(\log\log n)^b)$ with constants $a,b$ is
written $\O(m)$, and every $O(\log n\,\log\log n)$ is written $\O(\log n)$.
The ledger of \Cref{sec:ram27-explicit-ledger} tracks the hidden powers
explicitly; the dense work per attempt is $O(mP^{14}(\log\log n)^{19})$, which
is $O(m(\log\log n)^{40})$ since $P=O(\log\log n)$.
Factors of $\log\log n$ are not optimized or traded against each other.
Sparse work is below $n^2$ by a fixed power of $n$; its logarithmic factors
are not counted and are absorbed in the $o(1)$ of its exponent, as in
$n^{15/8+o(1)}$.

\subsection{Machine model and notation}
\label{sec:ram27-model}

The word RAM has indexed memory, comparisons, Boolean operations, shifts, and
integer addition, subtraction, multiplication, quotient, and remainder on
$O(\log n)$-bit words, together with independent uniform random words.
A fixed number of wider products may occupy a fixed number of words.
Population count is implemented with bit operations.
Square roots, logarithms, powers, and exponentials use certified
approximations.
Exact geometric sampling may use many words; its actual work is charged before
the attempt cap.
Polynomial address space may be reserved without clearing it; the
initializable-array construction specifies which cells are read.

The augmentation in \Cref{sec:ram27-endpoints} gives a connected working graph
with $m=\Theta(n^2)$ labels and rank $r=n-1=\Theta(n)$.
From that point, $n,m,r$ refer to the working graph.
Its dimensions differ from the input dimensions by constant factors.
As an undirected graph it has bounded parallel multiplicity, so the structural
routines use a fixed number of simple layers.

\Cref{sec:ram27-symbols} lists the symbols in order of first appearance.
The local-step analysis writes the incidence matrix as $\BB$ throughout.
Products, quotients, powers, and inequalities between edge vectors are
coordinatewise.
For positive $\ww$, $\norm{\xx}_{\ww}^2=\sum_e \ww_e\xx_e^2$; matrix inequalities use the
positive-semidefinite order.
Laplacian inverses act on the quotient by their componentwise constant
vectors.
The local update defines $\HH,\ttau,\vvp,\ddelta,\vvs$ precisely in
\Cref{sec:ram-local}.
Logarithmic scales are rounded by integer bit lengths; local integer or dyadic
conventions change only constants.



\subsection{The algorithm and the two maintenance tasks}

The iteration controls stationarity for a weighted logarithmic capacity barrier
and tracks regularized leverage scores with adaptive weights $\llambda$.
Separate potentials keep the two errors away from their boundaries.
A residual test controls approximate flow feasibility during the iteration;
one terminal correction restores exact feasibility.

A \emph{frame} is a sparse physical update with reference and metadata
service; an \emph{attempt} runs a prescribed number of frames from a
checkpoint.
An \emph{original label} identifies a working edge, not a sampled copy.
A graph \emph{owner} stores labels in cores with certified cut-conductance
bounds and in explicit lists.
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=3pt]
\item \emph{Initialize.}
Augmentation gives an exactly feasible interior midpoint.
Initialize tracking and centrality, then save the physical variables and
certified references at a checkpoint.
\item \emph{Run an attempt.}
Each frame fixes its directions from the old state and draws one independent
original-label mask.
Only selected flow and tracking weights change.
Local drift controls both potentials.
Operational failure restores the checkpoint; the next attempt uses fresh
tapes, with no local resampling of a failed frame.
\item \emph{Maintain graphs and observations within each frame.}
Three graphs, changing only on selected labels, support the ordinary inverse,
physical solve, and observer.
Cores answer scalar queries; packed computations evaluate fine scores and
change sources.
Observations change metadata only.
\item \emph{Finish.}
Success installs a checkpoint with a constant-factor smaller $\mu$.
After enough successes, exact feasibility correction and integral rounding
yield the original flow and dual, or the augmentation yields an infeasibility
cut.
\end{enumerate}

The owner finds labels with the prescribed sampling probabilities; the observer
discovers references needing fine reads, without scanning all edges each frame.
Graph updates retain prices frozen before the batch.
Autonomous reads spend accumulated score-variation credit; selected reads use
frozen list prices.
Representation changes preserve outstanding debts and due flags.

\subsection{Where the single logarithm comes from}

The proof keeps the following costs separate:
\begin{itemize}
\item dense expected work of one attempt, $\O(m)$;
\item additional sparse work of one attempt,
$n^{15/8+o(1)}+n^{1011/512+o(1)}$;
\item cold initialization, once, $\O(m\log n)$;
\item exact output, once, $O((m+n)\log n)$.
\end{itemize}
Each attempt is capped at a fixed multiple of its expected work.
The phase analysis gives a positive constant conditional success probability,
and $O(\log n)$ total attempts supply both progress and confidence.
Since $m=\Theta(n^2)$, the $O(\log n)$ attempts cost $\O(n^2\log n)$ in
total, and the sparse exponents' strict gaps below two absorb their
logarithmic factors.

Within a stopped attempt, the per-frame scales $B_*$ sum to $O(m)$
(\Cref{lem:ram-attempt-budgets}).
Two constructions convert this budget into the dense work bound.
The core owner bounds \emph{moments} of current update prices, including
low-capacity records; individual low-density cores may still be expensive.
Packed score reads and source assembly process the confidence lanes together,
avoiding an additional factor $\log n$ at every dense event.

For the complete argument, read the execution order (\Cref{sec:ram27-joint}),
phase analysis (\Cref{sec:ram-phases}), and contract closure
(\Cref{sec:ram27-contract-closure}), followed by the ledger and main proof
(\Cref{sec:ram27-explicit-ledger,sec:ram27-main-theorem}).

\section{One independent physical step}
\label{sec:ram-local}

An independent mask selects the labels whose flow and weight change.
Given certified directions and observations, we prove that the step reduces
$\mu$, controls feasibility, and contracts the central and leverage-tracking
potentials.
\Cref{sec:ram-finite-direction} implements this step with finite words; later
sections construct its inputs.

\subsection{State, directions, and one independent mask}

Let $\BB$ be an $m\times n$ incidence matrix of rank $r=n-1$, with
$m=\Theta(n^2)$, and put $s=r/m$.
The flow is strictly inside its positive capacities, $0<\ff_e<\vvu_e^{\max}$.
Write $\llambda$ for the edge weights and $\yy$ for the vertex potential.
Define coordinatewise
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \zz=(\ff^{-2}+(\vvu^{\max}-\ff)^{-2})^{-1/2},\quad
 \XXibar=\zz(\ff^{-1}-(\vvu^{\max}-\ff)^{-1}),\quad
 \DD=\zz^3(\ff^{-3}-(\vvu^{\max}-\ff)^{-3}),\\
 \ww=2s+\llambda,\qquad
 \XXi=\frac{\zz(\cc-\BB\yy)}{\mu \ww}=\XXibar+\varepsilon\vvs,\qquad
 \vvr=\zz/\ww,\qquad \HH=\BB^T\diag(\vvr^2)\BB.
\end{gathered}
\]
For the logarithmic box barrier, $\zz$ is the inverse square root of the second
derivative, $\XXibar$ is minus the first derivative times $\zz$, and $\DD=dz/df$.
Thus $e$ measures centrality in the local flow scale.
Here $\llambda\ge s$, $\mu>0$, and $|\vvs|<1$.
Work on the rank-$r$ quotient, so that $\HH^{-1/2}$ denotes its ordinary inverse
there.
Set
\[
 \UU=\diag(\vvr)\BB\HH^{-1/2},\quad
 \PPi=\UU\UU^T,\quad \ttau=\diag\PPi,
 \quad \widehat\ttau=s+\ttau,
 \quad t=\log(\llambda/\widehat\ttau),\quad
 \delta=1-|t|/\kappa.
\]
Assume $|\vvp|<\kappa$ for fixed $\varepsilon,\kappa\le10^{-2}$.
Then $\sum\llambda\le2e^\kappa r$ and $\sum \ww\le(2+2e^\kappa)r$.
The tracking error $\vvp$ and central error $e$ have potentials
\[
 \psi_\varkappa(x)=(1-x)^{-\varkappa}+(1+x)^{-\varkappa},\quad
 \Psi_R=\sum_e\psi_p(\vvp_e/\kappa),\quad
 \Psi_Z=\sum_e\psi_P(\vvs_e),\quad p=512,
\]
\[
\begin{gathered}
10^5n^{1/4}\le M\le2\cdot10^5n^{1/4},\\
\tfrac12 c_h n^{-3/p}P^{-16}\le h\le c_h n^{-3/p}P^{-16}.
\end{gathered}
\]
\[
\beta_f=h/M,\quad\beta_s=\gamma\beta_f,\quad
B_*=\beta_fm/\sqrt r,\qquad B_*\le\widehat B_*\le2B_*.
\]
Choose $M,h,\gamma,\widehat B_*$ dyadic; $B_*$ is analytical only.
The constants $c_h,\gamma$ are fixed below.
The direction scale is $\beta_f$, the source radius is $\beta_s$, and $B_*$
measures dense work per frame.

\paragraph{Tracking feedback.}
Let $\pi=\llambda(1+\varepsilon\vvs)$ and $\mathcal
N(v)=\norm{v}_\infty+M\norm{v}_\pi$.
This mixed norm controls both coordinate jumps and weighted movement.
The tracking feedback $\pphi$ obeys
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal N(\pphi)\le h,\qquad
 \GG^T\pphi\le-0.97h\norm{\GG}_{\mathcal N^*}+C hB_R,
 \qquad \GG=\nabla_t\Psi_R,
 \quad B_R=C_{p,\kappa}\norm{\mathbf1}_{\mathcal N^*}.
 \label{eq:ram-feedback-input}
\end{equation}
A lower-wall guard sets negative feedback to zero when $\llambda<(1+2a_0)s$,
where $a_0$ upper-bounds every realized coordinate jump.
Such guarding loses only $C hB_R$: a positive gradient removed by it can occur
only when $0<t<\log(1+2a_0)$, where the derivative is bounded by the fixed
constant included in $C_{p,\kappa}$.
\Cref{lem:ram-class-feedback} constructs this feedback from the maintained joint
classes.

\paragraph{Central source.}
The source points inward from a representative $\vvsbar_e$ of the central error.
Assume
\begin{equation}
 |\vvs_e-\vvsbar_e|\le\eta(1-|\vvsbar_e|)/P^4,
 \qquad d_{Z,e}=1-|\vvsbar_e|,
 \qquad G_{Z,e}=|\psi_P'(\vvsbar_e)|,
 \qquad J_e=\psi_P''(\vvsbar_e),
 \label{eq:ram-central-representatives}
\end{equation}
where $\eta$ is a sufficiently small fixed constant.
Let $\widetilde \ww_e/\ww_e\in[1-\eta,1+\eta]$ be constant within the classes used
for the source.
Use certified rational class values
\[
 G_{Z,e}\le \GG^c_{Z,e}\le(1+\eta)G_{Z,e},\qquad
 J_e\le J^c_e\le(1+\eta)J_e.
\]
The zero-gradient class uses $\GG^c_{Z,e}=0$ exactly.
Define the capped waterfill
\begin{equation}
 \FF_e=-\sgn(\vvsbar_e)
       \min\{c_Z d_{Z,e}/P^2,\ \gamma h,
                         \vartheta \GG^c_{Z,e}/\widetilde \ww_e\}.
 \label{eq:ram-central-source}
\end{equation}
Choose rational $\vartheta$ so that $(1-\eta)\beta_s\le\norm{\FF}_{\ww}\le\beta_s$
when possible.
If the fully capped source has norm at most $\beta_s$, it may instead be
returned with $\vartheta^{-1}=0$, whether or not its norm lies in the filled
range.
Use this fully capped branch when the filled range cannot be reached.

The nonzero source-cap set consists of coordinates with $\FF_e\ne0$ at which
either fixed cap $c_Zd_{Z,e}/P^2$ or $\gamma h$ attains the minimum, including
ties.
Let $\mathcal J$ be its complement.
A zero-gradient coordinate has $\FF_e=0$ and is never forced by the source; it
stays in $\mathcal J$, where its positive Hessian contributes to the curvature
normalizer.
Define
\[
 A_Z=\sum_eG_{Z,e}|\FF_e|,\qquad
 V=\widehat B_*+\beta_s^2/\vartheta.
\]
Freeze both inputs, $\pphi,\FF$, and the source-cap set before the mask.

\paragraph{Solving for the flow direction.}
Choose the actual next ratio $\theta=\mu'/\mu$ with $\beta_s/(2\sqrt
r)\le1-\theta\le\beta_s/\sqrt r$ and put
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \gg_0=(\theta-1)\XXi+\theta\varepsilon \FF,\quad
 \gg=\gg_0+\XXi\pphi/3,\quad \YY=3\llambda/\ww,\quad
 \kk=DC+\YY(1-DP_0),\\
 \LL=\BB^T\diag
       \left(\frac{3\llambda \zz^2}{\mu \ww^2 \kk}\right)\BB.
\end{gathered}
\]
The two metrics have different roles: the solve uses $\LL$ to control
feasibility, while the tracked leverage scores belong to $\HH$.
One has $1-\varepsilon\le \kk\le3+\varepsilon$.
Use a direction $\xxdir$ satisfying
\begin{equation}
 \LL \xxdir=\rho+\BB^T\diag(\YY\zz/\kk)\gg-r_{\rm sol},
 \quad \rho=\BB^T\ff-b_{\rm dem},\quad
 \norm{r_{\rm sol}}_{\LL^{-1}}
                        \le c_{\rm sol}\beta_s\sqrt\mu,
 \label{eq:ram-solve}
\end{equation}
and the old residual has norm at most $\beta_s\sqrt\mu$.
Define
\[
 \qq=\zz\BB \xxdir/(\mu \ww),\quad \UUps=(\gg-\qq)/\kk,\quad
 \dd=|\UUps|+|\pphi|,\quad
 \nnu=(\gg_0-\qq)/(\varepsilon\theta).
\]
Here $\dd$ is the movement scale before the mask is applied, and $\nnu$ is the
coefficient of the central noise.
The bounds below give $U_{\nnu}=O(\beta_f)$ with $\norm{\nnu}_{\ww}\le U_{\nnu}$.

\paragraph{The sampling rates.}
Let $a_{\rm jump}>0$ be a sufficiently small fixed scalar jump allowance,
distinct from the row-weight vector $\vvr=\zz/\ww$.
Let $\overline\ell_e$ be a supplied upper bound for the actual leverage of row
$e$ in $\LL$.
Let $\ddeltabar_e$ be the stored certified tracking-margin scale, with a fixed
known constant $C_\delta\ge1$ such that $C_\delta^{-1}\ddelta_e\le\ddeltabar_e\le
C_\delta\ddelta_e$.
Freeze all inputs, and write $\mathcal F$ for this history before the fresh mask
coins.
Form a fixed number of rational component probabilities, upward within fixed
factors of the following clipped rates:
\begin{equation}
 K\log n\llambda_e,\quad K\log n\overline\ell_e,\quad
 \frac{KLr \ww_ed_e^2}{\beta_s^2},\quad
 \frac{C_\delta \dd_e}{a_{\rm jump}\ddeltabar_e}.
 \label{eq:ram-core-floors}
\end{equation}
The implemented margin target uses only $\ddeltabar_e$ and dominates the
analytical floor $\dd_e/(a_{\rm jump}\ddelta_e)$.
Every local estimate below continues to use that analytical lower bound;
evaluating a proposal probability requires no fresh fine-score read.
On $\mathcal J$, also include
\begin{equation}
 \frac{k_P|\nnu_e|}{d_{Z,e}},\qquad
 \frac{k_P B_{\nnu}|\nnu_e|\sqrt{J^c_e}}{V},\qquad
 B_{\nnu}\ge U_{\nnu}\sqrt{\sum_{e\in\mathcal J}J^c_e/\ww_e},
 \qquad k_P=C P^{12}.
 \label{eq:ram-central-floors}
\end{equation}
The leverage and quadratic rates give the matrix and residual concentration
bounds below.
The tracking and central wall rates limit individual jumps; the remaining
central rate controls second-order potential cost.
\Cref{lem:ram-global-normalizer} chooses its $B_{\nnu}$ from common scalars without
a reciprocal scan.
Force the nonzero source-cap set.
These are all the physical-mask components; \eqref{eq:ram-theoretical-alpha}
supplies the gradient bound without another sampling rate.

\paragraph{The selected update and its commit.}
Draw the components independently between original labels.
Their union indicators $I_e$ are independent, with actual union probabilities
$p_e$.
Use $\TT_e=I_e/p_e$ with the zero-target no-op convention; the denominator is the
union probability, not the sum of component probabilities.
Set $v=Tu$, $\zeta=\TT\pphi$, and on selected labels define
\begin{equation}
 \llambda'=s+[\ww(1+\zeta/3)-3s](1+Dv)^2,
 \qquad \ww'=2s+\llambda'.
 \label{eq:ram-weight-update}
\end{equation}
Set $\yy'=\yy-\xxdir$ and $\mu'=\theta\mu$.
Choose the new flow by the following scalar root equation, which will make the
central noise exactly $(\TT-1)\nnu$ in \eqref{eq:ram-exact-central}.
The root near $\ff$ solves
\begin{equation}
 \frac{\zz(\ff')(\cc-\BB\yy')}{\theta\mu \ww'}-\XXibar(\ff')
 =\frac{C+\qq}{\theta}-\XXibar+
                          \frac{kv-C\zeta/3}{\theta}.
 \label{eq:ram-root-update}
\end{equation}
Unselected labels retain their stored $\ff,\llambda$ and structural coefficients.
Each frame has one candidate: a failed residual or source-normalizer check
aborts the whole clean attempt, with no local retry.
No moment bound conditions on these checks or eventual phase success.

Buffer candidate points and their residual/source-normalizer data.
Retain the old $\HH$ and ordinary owner through all conserved-source products and
old-observer batches; then commit and service queued reads at frozen old prices,
in Steps~\ref{item:ram30-old-work}--\ref{item:ram30-refresh}.

\subsection{Scalar identities and direction bounds}

\begin{lemma}
\label{lem:ram-scalar}
Put $\XX_e=T_ed_e$ and $\sigma_e=|\qq_e|+|1-\theta|$.
For $\XX_e\le10^{-4}$ and $\sigma_e\le10^{-4}$, the root in $|\ff'_e-\ff_e|\le \zz_e/40$
exists uniquely and stays in the box.
The guard gives $\llambda'_e\ge s$.
Before numerical rounding,
\begin{align}
 \vvs'&=\vvs+\FF+(\TT-1)\nnu,\label{eq:ram-exact-central}\\
 \rho'&=r_{\rm sol}+\BB^T\diag(\YY\zz)(\LLam-\UUps)
       +\BB^T\diag(\zz)r_f,\label{eq:ram-exact-residual}\\
 (\ff'-\ff)/\zz&=\YY\LLam+r_f,\qquad
       |r_f|\le C(X^2+\sigma X),\label{eq:ram-local-flow}\\
 (a'/a)-1&=Dv-\zeta/3+r_a,\qquad
       |r_a|\le C(X^2+\sigma X).\label{eq:ram-local-row}
\end{align}
All constants are uniform as $\ff$ approaches either capacity wall.
\end{lemma} \begin{proof} Suppress the edge index.
Write $\alpha=\zz/\ff$ and $\beta=\zz/(\vvu^{\max}-\ff)$, so $\alpha^2+\beta^2=1$,
$\XXibar=\alpha-\beta$, and $\DD=\alpha^3-\beta^3$.
Hence $\DD=(1+\alpha\beta)\XXibar$, $|\DD|,|\XXibar|\le1$, and $1\le1+\alpha\beta\le3/2$.
Direct differentiation gives $dz/df=\DD$ and $dP_0/df=(DP_0-1)/\zz$.

For normalized displacement $x=(\ff'-\ff)/\zz$, put $\RR(x)=\zz(\ff+zx)/\zz$ and
$\KK_0=(C+\qq)/(\theta(1+b_{\ww}))$, where $b_{\ww}=\ww'/\ww-1$.
Let $\Phi(\varkappa)=\RR(\varkappa)\KK_0-\XXibar(\ff+\zz\varkappa)$.
At the displaced point, with its own $D_x,\alpha_x,\beta_x$, differentiation
gives
\[
 \RR'=D_x,\qquad
 \Phi'=\frac{1+D_x\Phi}{\RR},\qquad
 D_x'=-\frac{3\alpha_x^2\beta_x^2(1+2\alpha_x\beta_x)}\RR,
 \qquad \Phi''=D_x'\Phi/\RR.
\]
For $|x|\le1/40$ the hypotheses imply $0.975\le \RR\le1.025$, $|\Phi|<0.062$,
$\Phi'>0.915$, and $|\Phi''|<0.1$.
These bounds follow directly from $|D_x|\le1$, $|D_x'|\le3/(2\RR)$,
$|\XXi-\XXibar|\le0.01$, and the stated jump bounds.

Let $l=(\llambda-s)/\ww$.
Expansion of \eqref{eq:ram-weight-update} gives
\[
 b_{\ww}=\zeta/3+2l\DD\LLam+r_{\ww},\qquad |r_{\ww}|\le2\XX^2,
 \qquad \YY=1+2l.
\]
If $T_0$ is the target in \eqref{eq:ram-root-update}, direct subtraction gives
\begin{align*}
 \theta\{T_0-\Phi(0)-\YY\LLam\Phi'(0)\}
 ={}&\qq\zeta/3+[-Dq+\YY(1-\theta)(1-DP_0)]v+(C+\qq)r_{\ww}\\
 &-\frac{(C+\qq)b_{\ww}(\zeta/3-Dv+r_{\ww})}{1+b_{\ww}}.
\end{align*}
Its magnitude is at most $5X^2+4\sigma X$.
Also $0.989<\Phi'(0)<1.011$ and $|T_0-\Phi(0)|<3.034X$.
Monotonicity and the endpoint displacements give the unique root, initially
$|x|<4X$, and Taylor's formula then gives $|x-\YY\LLam|\le6X^2+5\sigma X$.
Since $\RR(x)=1+Dx+O(x^2)$ and $\vvr'/\vvr=\RR(x)/(1+b_{\ww})$, the row estimate follows.
The entire window lies in the box because $\zz\le\min\{\ff,\vvu^{\max}-\ff\}$.

If the guard is active, $\zeta\ge0$.
Otherwise $\ww(1+\zeta/3)\ge s(3+2a_0)(1-a_0/3)\ge3s$, proving the lower weight
bound.
Substituting $ku=\gg-\qq$ and $\gg=\gg_0+\XXi\pphi/3$ proves
\eqref{eq:ram-exact-central}.
The solve equation gives $\BB^T\diag(\YY\zz)\UUps=-\rho+r_{\rm sol}$, which proves
\eqref{eq:ram-exact-residual}.
For $I_e=0$, the old coordinate is exactly the root.
\end{proof}

The next lemma analyzes the unsampled first-order step, obtained by setting
every $\TT_e=1$.
Under the independent mask its derivative becomes the mean linear increment,
before any restriction to a safety event.

\begin{lemma}
\label{lem:ram-contraction}
Let $\LL_0=\mu\LL$ and let $\ell_e$ be its row leverage.
Define
\[
 \QQ=\diag(\vvr)\BB\LL_0^{-1}
                \BB^T\diag(3\llambda\vvr/\kk),\quad
 \KK_0=\diag(\kk)^{-1}(\II-\QQ),
\]
\[
 A_{\ttau}=\diag(\widehat\ttau)^{-1}(\PPi\circ\PPi),
 \qquad \iiota=1-s/\llambda-\ttau/\widehat\ttau.
\]
Then
\[
 \ell_e\le\min\{1,4\llambda_e^2/s\},\quad
 \ell_e/\llambda_e\le2/\sqrt s,\quad
 \norm{\QQ\xx}_\infty\le3s^{-1/4}\norm{\xx}_{\llambda}.
\]
The feedback part of the first-order increment of $\vvp$ is $(\II-\mathcal
\RR)\pphi$, where
\[
 \mathcal R=\frac23(A_{\ttau}+\diag\iiota)
           [\II-\diag(\DD)\KK_0\diag(\XXi)],
 \qquad \norm{\mathcal R}_{\mathcal N\to\mathcal N}<0.86.
\]
The other first-order terms have $\mathcal N$ norm at most $C\gamma h$.
Moreover
\[
 \norm{\dd}_{\ww}+\norm{\nnu}_{\ww}\le C\beta_f,\qquad
 \norm{\dd}_\infty+\norm{\qq}_\infty+|1-\theta|\le Ch,
 \qquad \mathcal N(\dd)\le C_{\rm amp}h.
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Since $\LL_0\succeq3sH/(3+\varepsilon)$ and $\ttau_e\le e^\kappa\llambda_e$,
inverse comparison proves $\ell_e\le4\llambda_e^2/s$.
Every leverage is at most one; taking the smaller bound proves
$\ell_e/\llambda_e\le2/\sqrt s$.
The matrix $\QQ$ is an orthogonal projection in the $\llambda/\kk$ inner product.
Row Cauchy--Schwarz proves its displayed infinity bound.
In a simple augmented dense graph, $s^{-1/4}\le Cn^{1/4}$; the fixed
augmentation constant can be absorbed in $M$ if necessary.

The matrix $\KK_0$ is self-adjoint positive semidefinite in the $\llambda$ inner
product and $0\preceq \KK_0\preceq\diag(1/\kk)$ there.
With $\alpha=\zz/\ff$ and $\beta=\zz/(\vvu^{\max}-\ff)$ as in the scalar calculation, put
$\chi=1+\alpha\beta\in[1,3/2]$, so $\DD=\chi \XXibar$.
The matrix $\diag(\DD)\KK_0\diag(\XXibar)$ is positive semidefinite in the
$\llambda/\chi$ inner product and bounded by $(1-\varepsilon)^{-1}\II$.
Consequently
\[
 \norm{\II-\diag(\DD)\KK_0\diag(\XXi)
                }_{\llambda\to\llambda}
 \le b_0:=\sqrt{3/2}+\varepsilon/(1-\varepsilon).
\]
The perturbation from $\XXi-\XXibar=\varepsilon\vvs$ contributes the second term.
The projection identity $\PPi^2=\PPi$ and weighted Cauchy--Schwarz give
\[
 \norm{A_{\ttau}}_{\llambda\to\llambda}\le e^\kappa,
 \quad \norm{A_{\ttau}\xx}_\infty\le e^{\kappa/2}\norm{\xx}_{\llambda},
 \quad \norm{\iiota}_\infty\le J_0:=e^\kappa-1.
\]
Combining these weighted bounds in the mixed norm gives at most
\[
 \frac23 b_0\left[e^\kappa\sqrt{\frac{1+\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}}
       +\frac{e^{\kappa/2}}{10^5\sqrt{1-\varepsilon}}\right]
 +\frac23J_0\max\left\{
 \frac2{1-\varepsilon}+
 \frac{20(1+\varepsilon)}{10^5(1-\varepsilon)^{3/2}},\,
 b_0\sqrt{\frac{1+\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}<0.854.
\]
Differentiating the local weight and row formulas gives
\[
 \Delta\vvp^{(1)}=\pphi+
       2(A_{\ttau}+\diag\iiota)(Du-\pphi/3).
\]
The feedback contribution to $\UUps$ is $\KK_0\diag(\XXi)\pphi/3$, giving the asserted
response.
For the other terms, write $\qq=Qg+q_{\rm res}$, where $q_{\rm res}$ is the
contribution of $\rho-r_{\rm sol}$ in \eqref{eq:ram-solve}.
We have $\norm{\gg_0}_{\ww}\le C\beta_s$, the projection $\QQ$ is bounded by an absolute
constant in $\ww$, and
\[
 \norm{q_{\rm res}}_{\ww}
 \le\sqrt{1+\varepsilon}
    \norm{\rho-r_{\rm sol}}_{\LL^{-1}}/\sqrt\mu
 \le C\beta_s.
\]
Its infinity norm and that of $Qg_0$ are at most $Cn^{1/4}\beta_s$.
Inserting these inequalities in the displayed derivative and the bounds for
$A_{\ttau},\iiota$ gives $C\gamma h$.
Applying the same estimates to $\XXi\pphi/3$ gives all remaining direction
bounds.
Constants may depend on the fixed $\varepsilon$, because $\nnu$ divides by it.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Independent moments and high-probability safety}

The direction bounds and jump floor put every mask outcome in the scalar window
of \Cref{lem:ram-scalar}.
For independent mean-zero Hilbert vectors $Z_i$ with $\norm{Z_i}\le R_{\rm B}$ and
$\sum_i\Ex\norm{Z_i}^2\le\sigma^2$, use
\[
 \Pr\{\norm{\sum_i Z_i}\ge t\}
 \le2\exp\{-c_\star t^2/(\sigma^2+R_{\rm B}t)\}.
\]
This dimension-free bound applies to matrices with the Frobenius inner product.

This is the Hilbert-space case of Pinelis' martingale inequality
\cite[Theorem~3.4]{P94:journal}: its Bennett exponent implies the displayed
bound because $(1+x)\log(1+x)-x\ge x^2/(2(1+x))$ for $x\ge0$.
The same statement applies conditionally on a frozen pre-state; zero variance or
zero packet bound gives the deterministic zero sum.

In matrix-change and observer formulas, $\ssigma_e$ denotes a relative coefficient
change; centrality formulas use it for the stored representative.

\begin{lemma}
\label{lem:ram-matrix-moments}
Suppose an actual structural coefficient change is $\ssigma_e=I_e\ssigma_e^{\rm sel}$ with
$|\ssigma_e^{\rm sel}|\le C \dd_e/p_e$.
This includes the squared row change from \Cref{lem:ram-scalar}, and its
absolute value.
Let $\EE=\UU^T\diag(\ssigma)\UU$ and $\EE_{\rm abs}=\UU^T\diag(|\ssigma|)\UU$.
For a sufficiently large $K$, at a fixed valid pre-state:
\begin{align*}
 \norm{\Ex \EE}_{\rm op},\ \norm{\Ex \EE_{\rm abs}}_{\rm op}
       &\le Ch,\\
 \norm{\Ex \EE}_F,\ \norm{\Ex \EE_{\rm abs}}_F&\le C\beta_f,\\
 \Ex\norm{\EE-\Ex \EE}_F^2,
 \ \Ex\norm{\EE_{\rm abs}-\Ex \EE_{\rm abs}}_F^2
       &\le C\beta_s^2/(K\log n).
\end{align*}
With any prescribed polynomial confidence,
\[
 \norm{\EE-\Ex \EE}_F\le c_1\beta_s,\quad
 \norm{\EE}_{\rm op}\le Ch,\quad
 \EE_{\rm abs}\preceq ChI,\quad \norm{\EE}_F\le C\beta_f.
\]
Also, if $\Gamma\le C_\Gamma\sum_e\llambda_eI_ed_e/p_e$ up to a negligible
deterministic numerical reserve, then
\[
 \Ex\Gamma\le C\beta_f\sqrt r,
 \qquad \Gamma\le C_{\Gamma,\rm cap}\beta_f\sqrt r
\]
with any prescribed polynomial confidence after increasing fixed constants and
handling the finitely many bounded inputs separately.
\end{lemma} \begin{proof} The mean row coefficient has magnitude at most $Cd_e$.
Since $\UU^T\UU=\II$, its operator norm is at most $C\norm{\dd}_\infty$.
Put $S=\PPi\circ\PPi$.
The elementary Schur-product identity $\diag(\ttau)-S=\PPi\circ(\II-\PPi)\succeq0$
gives $\norm{\Ex \EE}_F^2\le C\sum_e\ttau_ed_e^2 \le C\norm{\dd}_{\ww}^2$; the same holds
for absolute coefficients.

Saturated coordinates are deterministic and omitted from the variance
calculation.
For every nonsaturated coordinate,
\[
 \sum_e\Var(\ssigma_e)\ttau_e^2
 \le C\sum_{e:p_e<1}\frac{\dd_e^2\ttau_e^2}{p_e}
 \le\frac{C\beta_s^2}{KLr}\sum_e\frac{\ttau_e^2}{\ww_e}
 \le\frac{C\beta_s^2}{K\log n}.
\]
The geometric mean of the ordinary and quadratic floors gives
\[
 |\ssigma_e^{\rm sel}|\ttau_e
 \le\frac{C\beta_s}{K\log n\sqrt r}
           \sqrt{\ttau_e/\ww_e}
 \le\frac{C\beta_s}{K\log n\sqrt r}.
\]
Independence makes cross terms in the Frobenius second moment zero.
Hilbert Bernstein at $c_1\beta_s$ now has exponent $\Omega(K\log n)$, after $K$ is
chosen for the desired confidence.
Apply it to both signed and absolute coefficients.
The absolute matrix is positive semidefinite on every outcome, so its operator
bound is the asserted order bound.
A fixed number of such fields on the same ordinary graph is handled by a fixed
union bound.
A different graph requires its own leverage-domination premise before reusing
this estimate.

For $\Gamma$, its expectation is bounded by $C\sum\llambda_ed_e\le C\beta_f\sqrt
r$.
Each nonsaturated summand has size at most $Ch/(K\log n)$, by the ordinary floor.
Its variance sum is at most $(Ch/(K\log n))C\beta_f\sqrt r$.
Scalar Bernstein at a sufficiently large fixed multiple of $\beta_f\sqrt r$ has
exponent
\[
 \Omega\left(K\log n\frac{\beta_f\sqrt r}{h}\right)
 =\Omega(K\log n\sqrt r/M)=\Omega(K\log n n^{1/4}/10^5).
\]
Saturated contributions are deterministic and already included in the mean
bound.
This proves the source-normalizer statement for the original unconditioned mask.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}
\label{lem:ram-residual}
Choose $a_{\rm jump},c_{\rm sol}$ small compared with the fixed $\gamma$, then
$K$ large.
With any prescribed polynomial confidence the new residual is at most a fixed
small fraction of $\beta_s\sqrt\mu$ in the old metric.
Uniform comparison of the new and old physical metrics then gives
$\norm{\rho'}_{(\LL')^{-1}}\le\beta_s\sqrt{\mu'}$.
A finite norm test with a constant gap may abort the attempt on failure.
Its failure probability is inverse polynomial; it need not be used for local
rejection sampling.
\end{lemma} \begin{proof} The selected normalized displacement is a fixed
old-state value $\xxi_e^{\rm sel}$ with $|\xxi_e^{\rm sel}|\le Cd_e/p_e$.
Its nonlinear mean differs from $Y_eu_e$ by at most $C(a_{\rm jump}+h)\dd_e$,
since $\Ex \XX_e=\dd_e$ and $\Ex \XX_e^2=\dd_e^2/p_e\le a_{\rm jump}\delta_ed_e$.
The physical incidence contraction is
\[
 \norm{\BB^T\diag(\zz)x}_{\LL^{-1}}
                         \le C\sqrt\mu\,\norm{\xx}_{\ww}.
\]
Hence the mean residual has norm at most $c_{\rm sol}\beta_s\sqrt\mu+ C(a_{\rm
jump}+h)\beta_f\sqrt\mu$.

The centered \emph{actual displacement} is an independent vector sum.
Its coordinate packet norm is at most $C\sqrt{\mu \ww_e\ell_e}\,\dd_e/p_e$.
The quadratic floor gives total second moment $C\mu\beta_s^2/(K\log n)$, using
$\sum\ell_e=r$.
The geometric mean of the physical-leverage and quadratic floors gives maximum
packet $C\beta_s\sqrt\mu/(K\log n\sqrt r)$.
Hilbert Bernstein therefore bounds this full nonlinear noise by any fixed small
multiple of $\beta_s\sqrt\mu$ with exponent $\Omega(K\log n)$.

The scalar window gives constant comparisons of $\zz',\ww',\llambda'$ with their old
values.
Both $\kk,\kk'$ lie in $[1-\varepsilon,3+\varepsilon]$ and $\theta$ is close to one,
so the new physical metric is uniformly comparable to the old.
Choose the old-metric threshold with the corresponding fixed reserve.
\Cref{lem:ram27-candidate-tests} implements the test between separated
thresholds using certified inverse estimates, and includes its cost in sparse
preparation.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Expected nonlinear tracking drift}

\begin{theorem}
\label{thm:ram-tracking}
At a valid pre-state with $\Psi_R\le C_{R,\rm out}m$, use the law above.
On the simultaneous safety event of \Cref{lem:ram-matrix-moments}, the tracking
coordinate stays inside its tube.
On a primitive safety or arithmetic first failure, assign the analytical
candidate potential zero before evaluating a barrier outside its domain.
The resulting nonnegative potential satisfies
\[
 \Ex[\Psi_R'\mid\mathcal F]
 \le(1-a_R)\Psi_R+C a_Rm+\epsilon_{\rm num},
 \qquad a_R=c_\star\beta_f/\sqrt r.
\]
The horizon sum of $\epsilon_{\rm num}$ can be made arbitrarily small.
This is an unconditional pre-mask expectation, not an expectation conditional on
passing a later phase test.
\end{theorem} \begin{proof} Separate each edge's own update from the
inverse-Laplacian change.
Write $\mathcal G$ for the safety event used in the Taylor calculation; whenever
a nonlinear tracking bound uses $\mathcal G$, its expectation includes that
indicator.
Its nonnegative majorant is averaged under the original mask law.
The signed linear term remains defined on all masks and is treated in the final
paragraph.

\paragraph{Direction moments and the inverse remainder.}
All gradients below are with respect to the raw coordinate $\vvp$.
Set $H_e^R=|\GG_e|+C_{p,\kappa}$ and $\GG_* =\norm{\GG}_{\mathcal N^*}$.
Coordinatewise sign invariance of the mixed norm and \Cref{lem:ram-contraction}
give
\begin{equation}
 \alpha_*:=\sum_eH_e^Rd_e
       \le C_{\rm amp}h(\GG_*+B_R).
 \label{eq:ram-theoretical-alpha}
\end{equation}
Let $\ssigma_e=(\vvr_e'/\vvr_e)^2-1$ and $\EE=\UU^T\diag(\ssigma)\UU$.
\Cref{lem:ram-scalar} gives
\[
 |\ssigma_e|\le CX_e,\qquad
 \Ex \ssigma_e=2D_eu_e-2\pphi_e/3+r_{b,e},\qquad
 |r_{b,e}|\le C(a_{\rm jump}+h)\dd_e.
\]
For a symmetric $\TT$, define $\mathscr D(\TT)_e=\UU_eT\UU_e^T/\widehat\ttau_e$.
Weighted Cauchy--Schwarz proves
\[
 \norm{\mathscr D(\TT)}_\infty\le\norm{\TT}_{\rm op},\qquad
 \norm{\mathscr D(\TT)}_{\llambda}\le e^{\kappa/2}\norm{\TT}_F.
\]
Indeed $(\UU_eT\UU_e^T)^2 \le\ttau_e\UU_eT^2\UU_e^T$, and
summing uses $\llambda_e/\widehat\ttau_e\le e^\kappa$.
The moment lemma implies
\begin{equation}
 \Ex\norm{\mathscr D(\EE)}_{\mathcal N}\le Ch.
 \label{eq:ram-diagonal-mean}
\end{equation}
On its good event, $\norm{\EE}_{\rm op}\le Ch$ and $\norm{\EE^2(\II+\EE)^{-1}}_F\le
Ch\norm{\EE}_F$.
Thus the expected mixed norm of the resolvent remainder is $O(h^2)$, using
$M\beta_f=h$.

\paragraph{Separating the two changes.}
To separate the coordinate update from the inverse change, put
\[
 V_e=\mathscr D(\EE)_e,\quad
 \mathrm{Rem}_e=\mathscr D(\EE^2(\II+\EE)^{-1})_e,\quad
 \cc_e=\frac{1+\ssigma_e}{1+(\ttau_e/\widehat\ttau_e)\ssigma_e}.
\]
The inverse identity gives
\[
 \frac{s+\ttau_e'}{s+\ttau_e}
 =\left(1+\frac{\ttau_e}{\widehat\ttau_e}\ssigma_e\right)
                  [1-\cc_e(V_e-\mathrm{Rem}_e)].
\]
Consequently $\Delta\vvp=\log n+S$, where $\log n,S$ are edge vectors used only in this
proof.
The local term is
\[
 L_e=\log(\llambda_e'/\llambda_e)
      -\log(1+(\ttau_e/\widehat\ttau_e)\ssigma_e),
\]
and the inverse term is $S_e=-\log[1-\cc_e(V_e-\mathrm{Rem}_e)]$.
Expansion of the local scalar formulas gives
\[
 L_e=\TT_e[(1-2\iiota_e/3)\pphi_e+2\iiota_eD_eu_e]
                      +O(\XX_e^2+hX_e).
\]
Also $|\cc_e-1|\le Ca_{\rm jump}$, $\norm{S}_\infty\le Ch$ on the good event, and,
by \eqref{eq:ram-diagonal-mean},
\[
 \Ex\norm{S-\mathscr D(\EE)}_{\mathcal N}
                                      \le C(a_{\rm jump}+h)h.
\]
The mean linear increment is therefore precisely the derivative in
\Cref{lem:ram-contraction}, up to a mixed error $C(a_{\rm jump}+h)h$.
The local linear noise and the matrix linear noise have zero expectation,
because $\Ex \TT_e=1$.

\paragraph{Curvature of the two terms.}
For fixed $p,\kappa$, direct differentiation of $\psi_p$ gives
\[
 \partial_{\vvp_e}^2\Psi_R
            \le C_{p,\kappa}H_e^R/\ddelta_e.
\]
The same bound, within a fixed factor, holds on any segment whose relative
coordinate displacement is a sufficiently small fixed fraction of $\ddelta_e$.
The local jump floor gives $\XX_e\le a_{\rm jump}\ddelta_e$ on nonsaturated
coordinates.
On saturated coordinates, $\XX_e=\dd_e\le Ch\le a_{\rm jump}\ddelta_e$ after the
step-size choice.
Therefore
\[
 \Ex\sum_eH_e^R \XX_e^2/\ddelta_e
       \le a_{\rm jump}\sum_eH_e^R\Ex \XX_e=a_{\rm jump}\alpha_*.
\]
The same calculation bounds the local scalar remainder by $C(a_{\rm
jump}+h)\alpha_*$.

For the inverse term $S$, duality and the good-event infinity bound give
\[
 \Ex\sum_e\frac{H_e^R S_e^2}{\ddelta_e}
 \le\frac{Ch}{\delta_{\min}}(\GG_*+B_R)
                        \Ex\norm{S}_{\mathcal N}
 \le\frac{Ch^2}{\delta_{\min}}(\GG_*+B_R).
\]
The outer cap implies $\delta_{\min}\ge(C_{R,\rm out}m)^{-1/p}$.
Thus $h/\delta_{\min}\le Cc_hn^{-1/p}P^{-16}$, which is uniformly small after
choosing $c_h$.
\paragraph{Descent and affine contraction.} Taylor's formula now gives
\[
 \Ex\Delta\Psi_R
 \le-(0.97-0.86-C\gamma)hG_*+ChB_R
      +C(a_{\rm jump}+h+h/\delta_{\min})h(\GG_*+B_R)
      +\epsilon_{\rm num}.
\]
Choose $\gamma$ small, then $a_{\rm jump}$ small, then $K$ large for safety, and
finally $c_h$ small.
The result is $-c_\star hG_*+C hB_R+\epsilon_{\rm num}$.

Write $W_\pi=\sum_e\pi_e$.
Since $\mathcal N(\xx)\le(1+M\sqrt{W_\pi})\norm{\xx}_\infty$, the mixed unit ball
contains the infinity ball of radius $(1+M\sqrt{W_\pi})^{-1}$.
Hence
\[
 \GG_*\ge\frac{\norm{\GG}_1}{1+M\sqrt{W_\pi}}
       \ge c_\star(\Psi_R-2m)/(M\sqrt r).
\]
Here $W_\pi\le Cr$, and $|\psi_p'(x)|\ge p(\psi_p(x)-2)$ follows directly from
the two powers; the raw-$\vvp$ derivative also contains the fixed factor
$1/\kappa$.
Separately, $\mathcal N(\xx)\ge M\norm{\xx}_\pi$ and weighted Cauchy--Schwarz give
\[
 \norm{\mathbf1}_{\mathcal N^*}
 \le\frac1M\left(\sum_e\pi_e^{-1}\right)^{1/2}
 \le\frac{m}{M\sqrt{(1-\varepsilon)r}},
\]
because $\pi_e\ge(1-\varepsilon)s$ and $s=r/m$.
Thus $B_R\le Cm/(M\sqrt r)$, proving the affine drift.

\paragraph{The same-frame exceptional event.}
To account for $\mathcal G^c$, specify the signed linear term on every mask
outcome:
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \ssigma_e^{(1)}=2D_eT_eu_e-2\TT_e\pphi_e/3,\qquad
 \EE_1=\UU^T\diag(\ssigma^{(1)})\UU,\\
 L_{1,e}=\TT_e[(1-2\iiota_e/3)\pphi_e+2\iiota_eD_eu_e],
 \qquad Z=\GG^T[L_1+\mathscr D(\EE_1)].
\end{gathered}
\]
Its all-mask expectation is exactly the coupled first-order derivative.
The nonlinear row remainder is not centered: instead use the nonnegative
majorant $|\GG|^TA_{\ttau}|\ssigma-\ssigma^{(1)}|$ for its signed contribution on $\mathcal G$.
Since $\Ex|\ssigma-\ssigma^{(1)}|\le C(a_{\rm jump}+h)\dd$ and $A_{\ttau}$ is bounded in the
mixed norm, its full expectation is at most $C(a_{\rm jump}+h)hG_*$.
This is the previously reserved remainder.
The old gradient is bounded by the outer cap and $\XX_e\le a_{\rm jump}\ddelta_e$,
so $\sup|Z|\le Ca_{\rm jump}\norm{\GG}_1\le C_{p,\kappa} (C_{R,\rm out}m)^{1+1/p}$
on every mask outcome.
Explicitly use
\[
 \Ex[Z\mathbf1_{\mathcal G}]
 =\Ex Z-\Ex[Z\mathbf1_{\mathcal G^c}],
 \qquad
 |\Ex[Z\mathbf1_{\mathcal G^c}]|
 \le\sup|Z|\Pr(\mathcal G^c).
\]
Thus zero mean is used before good-event restriction, never after it.
On the bad event the analytical new potential is zero; omitting a nonnegative
remainder only helps.
Choose the polynomial confidence exponent after the all-outcome polynomial bound
so the displayed correction is included in $\epsilon_{\rm num}$.
No barrier is evaluated outside its domain and no future-success conditioning is
used.
Any further operational or numerical kill only lowers this nonnegative
matrix-killed candidate; no new signed cancellation is required.
For the finite implementation of \Cref{sec:ram-finite-direction}, use $\UUps_c,\dd_c$
in the all-mask expressions above.
The deterministic direction bias $\UUps_c-\UUps$ and the final endpoint-storage errors
are charged separately to $\epsilon_{\rm num}$ by that section; neither is
included in a purported centered random remainder.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Expected central drift and its dependence on \texorpdfstring{$P$}{P}}

\begin{theorem}
\label{thm:ram-central}
For the same single independent mask,
\[
 \Ex[\Psi_Z'\mid\mathcal F]
       \le\Psi_Z-cA_Z+C_\gamma P^2B_*+\epsilon_{\rm num},
\]
and
\[
 A_Z\ge c_\star\frac{P\beta_s}{\sqrt r}(\Psi_Z-Cm).
\]
Consequently the affine contraction rate is $a_Z=cP\beta_s/\sqrt r$, with
equilibrium $O_\gamma(Pm)$.
Every coordinate remains inside its central domain before numerical rounding, on
every mask outcome.
No high-probability central potential inequality is required.
\end{theorem} \begin{proof} Use the scalar identity
\eqref{eq:ram-exact-central}.

\paragraph{Curvature and waterfill bounds.}
The elementary explicit derivative bounds, valid for $P\ge2$, are
\[
 J_e\le CP G_{Z,e}/d_{Z,e}+CP^2,
 \qquad G_{Z,e}d_{Z,e}\ge P(\psi_P(\vvsbar_e)-2).
\]
For the first, split $|\vvsbar|\le1/P$, where both denominators give $J=\O(1)$, from
$|\vvsbar|>1/P$, where the larger derivative term dominates the smaller by a fixed
factor.
The second follows by subtracting the two explicit powers.
Moreover $|(\log\psi_P'')'(x)|\le(P+2)/(1-|x|)$.
The representative error and the coordinate displacement bounds therefore keep
true curvatures within a fixed factor of $J_e$.

For finite $\vartheta$, the literal rational waterfill and the derivative and
weight comparisons give $G_{Z,e}|\FF_e|\ge c_\star \ww_eF_e^2/\vartheta$ on every label.
The uncapped formula and actual filled radius then imply
\[
 \sum_{e\in\mathcal J}G_{Z,e}^2/\ww_e
                         \le C\beta_s^2/\vartheta^2,
 \qquad A_Z\ge c_\star\beta_s^2/\vartheta,
 \qquad V\le C(A_Z+B_*).
\]
For the fully capped branch, all uncapped $G_{Z,e}$ are zero and the assertions
use $\vartheta^{-1}=0$.

\paragraph{Noise and one-step drift.}
Put $\xxi=(\TT-1)\nnu$.
Forced and saturated coordinates have $\xxi_e=0$.
On all other coordinates the wall floor gives $|\xxi_e|\le d_{Z,e}/k_P$.
Thus $|\FF_e+\xxi_e|\le C d_{Z,e}/P^2$, which stays inside the true domain by
\eqref{eq:ram-central-representatives}.
For each coordinate, $\Ex\xxi_e=0$ and $\Ex\xxi_e^2=(1-p_e)\nnu_e^2/p_e$.
The curvature floor yields
\[
 \Ex\sum_{e\in\mathcal J}J_e\xxi_e^2
 \le\frac{V}{k_PB_{\nnu}}
                \sum_{e\in\mathcal J}|\nnu_e|\sqrt{J_e}
 \le V/k_P.
\]
The linear-noise expectation is exactly zero: $\Ex\sum\psi_P'(\vvs_e)\xxi_e=0$.

The representative reserve and the derivative bound give
\[
 \sum_e\psi_P'(\vvs_e)\FF_e
                    \le-(1-o(1))A_Z+CP^2B_*.
\]
For example the error per coordinate is at most $C\eta(G_{Z,e}/P^3+1/P^2)|\FF_e|$;
sum it using $\sum|\FF_e|\le\beta_s\sqrt{m/(3s)}=O_\gamma(B_*)$.
The deterministic source curvature is at most
\[
 C\sum J_eF_e^2
 \le CP\max_e(|\FF_e|/d_{Z,e})A_Z+CP^2\beta_s^2/(3s)
 \le(Cc_Z/P)A_Z+CP^2\beta_s^2/(3s).
\]
Since $(\beta_s^2/s)/B_*=\gamma^2\beta_f/\sqrt r$, the last term is at most
$CB_*$ for the chosen step size.
Taylor's theorem, $(\FF+\xxi)^2\le2\FF^2+2\xxi^2$, and $V\le C(A_Z+B_*)$ prove the
drift when $c_Z$ is small and $k_P$ is large.
The stated $P^2B_*$ allowance is conservative.

\paragraph{Progress of the inward source.}
For the lower bound, put $W=\sum \ww$ and $b_0=\beta_s/\sqrt W$.
The step size ensures $b_0\le\gamma h$ and $b_0\le c_Z/P^2$.
The feasible comparison source has inward magnitude
$\min\{c_Zd_{Z,e}/P^2,b_0\}\ge b_0d_{Z,e}$.
The waterfill maximizes the $G_Z^c$ linear objective for the $\widetilde \ww$
ellipsoid at its attained radius.
Its objective is within a factor $1+\eta$ of the true objective.
Scaling any $\ww$-feasible comparison by $(1-\eta)\sqrt{(1-\eta)/(1+\eta)}$ makes
it feasible for that ellipsoid and its possibly reduced filled radius.
Thus its objective is at least a fixed factor of the comparison objective; the
fully capped branch is immediate.
Consequently
\[
 A_Z\ge c_\star b_0\sum G_{Z,e}d_{Z,e}
     \ge cP\beta_s(\Psi_Z-Cm)/\sqrt r.
\]
The last comparison uses the multiplicative bound between $\psi_P(\vvs_e)$ and
$\psi_P(\vvsbar_e)$ implied by the representative reserve.
This proves the claimed affine rate and equilibrium.

\paragraph{Stopping on failures.}
The wall/source caps make the all-mask candidate's potential defined and
nonnegative.
Killing it at any additional matrix, field, operational, or arithmetic failure
only decreases it, so the same inequality holds for the killed candidate without
conditioning the noise on a good event.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}
\label{lem:ram-global-normalizer}
Assume the outer central cap $\Psi_Z\le C_{Z,\rm out}P^2m$.
For a sufficiently large constant depending only on that outer allowance and
fixed parameters,
\[
 B_{\nnu}=C_*P\widehat B_*\sqrt{1+\beta_f^2/(\vartheta\widehat B_*)}
\]
upper-bounds the normalizer required in \eqref{eq:ram-central-floors}, uniformly
over $P\ge512$.
It may be enclosed upward by a fixed factor.
\end{lemma} \begin{proof} The central cap and power-mean inequality imply $\sum
d_Z^{-2}\le C m(C_{Z,\rm out}P^2)^{2/P}=O(m)$, uniformly in $P$.
With $\omega=3s$,
\begin{align*}
\sum_{\mathcal J}J^c_e/\ww_e
 &\le CP\left(\sum_{\mathcal J}G_{Z,e}^2/\ww_e\right)^{1/2}
      \left(\sum_{\mathcal J}1/(w_ed_{Z,e}^2)\right)^{1/2}
      +CP^2\sum_{\mathcal J}1/\ww_e\\
 &\le CP\frac{\beta_s}{\vartheta}\sqrt{m/\omega}+CP^2m/\omega.
\end{align*}
Multiply by $U_{\nnu}^2=O(\beta_f^2)$, use $\sqrt{m/\omega}=\Theta(m/\sqrt r)$ and
$B_*=\beta_fm/\sqrt r$, and take a square root.
Increasing $C_*$ only increases probabilities, improving the moment bounds
without changing the preceding drift constants.
Thus outer allowances may be chosen after those constants.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Finite arithmetic and stopping rules}

Fix certified deterministic rounding before the mask.
A selected candidate uses only frozen data and its actual union probability;
evaluation may wait until selection.
Its displacement and exact rational coefficient change are two-point functions
of its own $I_e$, with a literal no-op at $I_e=0$, so change packets remain
independent.

\paragraph{Solving the scalar update.}
On a declared polynomial state range, the derivative bounds of
\Cref{lem:ram-scalar} permit certified Newton iteration.
Let $x_*$ be its root in the normalized coordinate $x$, and start at the old
point $x_0=0$.
The initial error is at most $4X\le4\cdot10^{-4}$.
Evaluate the residual and its derivative with outward absolute error at most
$\epsilon_N$, divide by a derivative enclosure bounded below by $0.9$, and round
the iterate to the same fixed grid.
Throughout the stated window the mean-value identity gives
\[
 |x_{j+1}-x_*|\le C|x_j-x_*|^2+C\epsilon_N
\]
with an absolute $C$; the sharper derivative and second-derivative bounds above
make the initial error lie strictly in its quadratic convergence region.
Choose $\epsilon_N$ a sufficiently small fixed fraction of the requested
normalized accuracy, and perform a predetermined $O(\log\log n)$ number of
stages.
The final outward residual enclosure, divided by $0.9$, certifies the error.
Window and positive-denominator guards precede every evaluation; they do not
fire under the stated scalar-window and range hypotheses.
All stages use one fixed precision, so rational denominators do not accumulate.
For square roots use the integer-Newton enclosure in the proof of
\Cref{lem:ram-algebraic-rate-enclosure}; scalar powers use the bounded-power
arithmetic of \Cref{sec:ram27-parameter-hierarchy}.
The scalar work is $\O(1)$ per selected label.

\paragraph{Precision and actual coefficients.}
\Cref{sec:ram30-polynomial-ranges} and \ref{sec:ram27-parameter-hierarchy} bound
physical quantities, inverse margins, and every fixed-order derivative by fixed
powers of $n$, uniformly for $P=O(\log\log n)$.
Choose precision after those bounds and the horizon so the summed residual,
normalizer, and drift defects are at most $n^{-c}$.
Central identities then have this additive storage defect.

The stored metric uses exact rational $\vvr_e^2=\zz_e^2/\ww_e^2$.
Polynomial sensitivity and a relative coefficient error $\epsilon$ give Loewner
error $\epsilon$ and regularized-log-score error $O(\epsilon)$.
\Cref{sec:ram-finite-direction} charges these errors and proves two-point
moments for the actual stored-state changes, including arbitrarily small ideal
directions.

\paragraph{Operational checks and proof-only stopping.}
Check the residual, $\Gamma$, ledger, and work operationally; kill the reference
at the first hidden matrix-norm or observer-projection failure, under the
\hyperref[par:ram30-finite-arithmetic]{finite arithmetic convention}.
The deterministic row cap gives $\norm{\EE}_{\rm op}=O(a_{\rm jump})$ and
$\II+\EE\succ0$; \Cref{lem:ram-matrix-moments} supplies the stronger $O(h)$ bound
required by tiny tracking margins.

\section{A finite implementation of the physical step}
\label{sec:ram-finite-direction}

Fix the direction mesh $\Delta_D$ after the polynomial physical ranges and
horizon, before the finer state and source/filter grids.
Every nonzero computational direction size $d_{c,e}=|\UUps_{c,e}|+|\pphi_e|$ is at
least $\Delta_D$; when it is zero, the label's stored $(\ff_e,\llambda_e)$ is
unchanged.
Thus point-storage error can be made smaller than every nonzero $d_{c,e}$, even
near cancellation, without refining $\Delta_D$.

\subsection{Finite global scalars}

Use the integer $P$ and dyadic $M,h,\gamma,\widehat B_*$ of
\Cref{sec:ram-local}.
The scale $B_*$ remains analytical: use $\widehat B_*\in[B_*,2B_*]$ in $V$ and
the curvature normalizer, with an upward enclosure of the latter's square root.

Store $\mu$ on one fixed mesh.
Let $\mu_{\rm low}>0$ be its supplied lower bound throughout the expanded
attempt range, and choose $\Delta_\mu\le c_\star\mu_{\rm low}\beta_s/\sqrt r$.
At each frame choose a grid value $\mu'$ such that its actual rational ratio
$\theta=\mu'/\mu$ satisfies
\[
 1-\beta_s/\sqrt r\le\theta
           \le1-\beta_s/(2\sqrt r).
\]
The interval length is at least $\mu_{\rm low}\beta_s/(2\sqrt r)$, so a rounded
certified midpoint has reserve.
Use this $\mu'$ and its actual ratio $\theta$ throughout; both have $O(\log
n)$-bit numerators and denominators.

\subsection{The computational direction and its exact identities}

First store the class feedback $\pphi$ on the grid $\Delta_D\mathbb Z$,
rounding toward zero and reserving its uniform objective error in the feedback
computation.
This cannot increase its mixed norm or violate a lower-wall guard.
Use this actual stored $\pphi$ in $\gg$ and in the physical solve.
The source $\FF$, scalars $\mu,\theta$, and returned vertex direction are also
their actual represented values; the solve's defect is measured against those
values.

For each original label, replace the ideal direction $\UUps=(\gg-\qq)/\kk$ by a
deterministic certified scalar evaluation and grid rounding:
\[
 \UUps_{c,e}\in\Delta_D\mathbb Z,\qquad
 |\UUps_{c,e}-\vvu_e|\le2\Delta_D.
\]
Enclose $\vvu_e$ to error $\Delta_D/100$ and round the deterministic midpoint.
This uses only local flow/weight data and the frozen vertex direction's
endpoints, so it defines a fixed old-state function even when evaluated lazily,
without a leverage-score read.

Set
\[
 \dd_c=|\UUps_c|+|\pphi|,\qquad
 \nnu_c=\frac{\kk \UUps_c-\XXi\pphi/3}{\varepsilon\theta},\qquad
 \delta \UUps=\UUps_c-\UUps,\qquad
 \delta\nnu=\frac{\kk\delta \UUps}{\varepsilon\theta},\qquad
 \FF_c=\FF+\delta\nnu.
\]
The direction rounding introduces deterministic biases $\delta \UUps,\delta\nnu$.
Use $\dd_c$ for physical jumps, quadratic rates, owner bounds, and observer
bounds; use $\nnu_c$ for central wall rates, curvature rates, and scheduler
bounds.
Source waterfill headers and forced caps continue to use the class source $\FF$;
$\FF_c$ is only its analytical, uniformly small correction.

Draw the original-label independent mask with these actual computational
targets.
The selected point map uses $v=\TT \UUps_c$ and $\zeta=\TT\pphi$.
If $d_{c,e}=0$, return the old stored $\ff_e,\llambda_e$ and their exact rational
coefficients literally, regardless of any unrelated floor selecting it.

\begin{lemma}
\label{lem:ram-computational-identities}
Before point storage rounding, the computational map satisfies
\begin{align*}
 \vvs'&=\vvs+\FF_c+(\TT-1)\nnu_c,\\
 \rho'&=r_{\rm sol}
       +\BB^T\diag(\YY\zz)(\TT-1)\UUps_c
       +\BB^T\diag(\YY\zz)\delta \UUps
       +\BB^T\diag(\zz)r_f,
\end{align*}
where $|r_f|\le C(X_c^2+hX_c)$ and $X_c=\TT \dd_c$.
\end{lemma} \begin{proof} Subtraction in the root equation gives
\[
 \vvs'-\vvs=\FF-\nnu+\TT\frac{\kk \UUps_c-\XXi\pphi/3}{\varepsilon\theta}
       =\FF+\delta\nnu+(\TT-1)\nnu_c.
\]
The original solve still gives $\BB^T\diag(\YY\zz)\UUps=-\rho+r_{\rm sol}$.
Insert $\TT \UUps_c-\UUps=(\TT-1)\UUps_c+\delta \UUps$ to get the residual identity.
The scalar root proof uses the actual point arguments $\UUps_c,\pphi$ and hence
gives the same remainder in terms of $X_c$.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{A local lower bound for the central radii.}
Fix a dyadic range parameter $d_{Z,\min}>0$ that lower-bounds $1-|\vvsbar_e|$
throughout the declared one-step expanded range of occupied central classes.
Fix the positive rational constant $c_{\rm cen}$ used by the central scheduler,
and choose a dyadic $\rho_{\min}$ with
\[
 \frac{c_{\rm cen}}4P^{-8}\min\{d_{Z,\min},P^{-1}\}
 \le\rho_{\min}\le
 \frac{c_{\rm cen}}2P^{-8}\min\{d_{Z,\min},P^{-1}\}.
\]
Every scheduler radius obtained by downward dyadic rounding within a factor two of
\[
c_{\rm cen}P^{-8}\min\{1-|\vvsbar_e|,P^{-1}\}
\]
is then at least $\rho_{\min}$ in
the direction-mesh and point-interval accuracy conditions.

\subsection{The deterministic perturbation budget}

Since $\sum w\le Cr$ and $1-\varepsilon\le \kk\le3+\varepsilon$,
\[
 \norm{\delta \UUps}_{\ww}\le C\Delta_D\sqrt r,\qquad
 \norm{\delta\nnu}_{\ww}\le C_\varepsilon\Delta_D\sqrt r,
 \qquad \norm{\delta\nnu}_\infty\le C_\varepsilon\Delta_D.
\]
Choose $\Delta_D\sqrt r\ll\beta_s$ and $\Delta_D\ll\rho_{\min}$ on the fixed
expanded range.
Let $C_{\nnu}$ be a fixed constant for the ideal bound $\norm{\nnu}_{\ww}\le
C_{\nnu}\beta_f$ in \Cref{lem:ram-contraction}.
After a fixed increase of constants, $\dd_c,\nnu_c$ retain the ideal norm and
infinity bounds.
Let $B_{\nnu,c}$ be the normalizer of \Cref{lem:ram-global-normalizer}, with
$C_*$ enlarged to dominate $2C_{\nnu}\beta_f\sqrt{\sum_{\mathcal J}J^c_e/\ww_e}$.
Its proof uses the original class waterfill and only the noise norm, so no new
dense aggregate is needed.

The mean residual defect from rounding has norm at most $C\Delta_D\sqrt{r\mu}$.
The mean tracking derivative changes by $2(A_{\ttau}+\diag\iiota)\DD\delta \UUps$, whose
mixed norm is at most $C\Delta_D(1+M\sqrt r)$.
The extra central deterministic linear term is bounded by
\[
 \left|\sum_e\psi_P'(\vvs_e)\delta\nnu_e\right|
 \le C_\varepsilon\Delta_D
             P d_{Z,\min}^{-1}\Psi_Z.
\]
All associated quadratic terms obey the same polynomial range bounds.
If the declared number of frames is $n^{A_T}$ and these old-state derivative and
norm bounds are at most $n^{A_E}$, choose $\Delta_D\le n^{-A_T-A_E-c_\star-10}$.
Their total perturbation is then at most $n^{-c}$.
These bounds are fixed before sampling, independently of whether the attempt
later succeeds.

For the owner energy bound, add to the direct class occurrence the envelope
$C_\varepsilon\Delta_D\sqrt{\ww_e}$.
Its weighted squared envelope mass is at most $Cr\Delta_D^2$, so the
physical-envelope energy remains $O(\beta_f^2)$.
This is the only change needed in that owner's point envelope for $\dd_c$ and
$\nnu_c$.

\subsection{Stored points and exact coefficient changes}

After fixing $\Delta_D$, choose the state mesh fine enough that every selected
point/root storage error, and the consequent change in its metric coefficient,
contributes at most $c_\star\Delta_D$ in the normalized scalar inequalities.
Polynomial bounds on $\ff,\zz,\ww,\mu$ and their required derivatives permit this
using a fixed number of words.
The ordinary coefficient is always the exact rational function of the stored
state,
\[
 \vvr_e^2=\frac{\zz_e^2}{\ww_e^2}
 =\frac{\ff_e^2(\vvu_e^{\max}-\ff_e)^2}
 {\ww_e^2\bigl(\ff_e^2+(\vvu_e^{\max}-\ff_e)^2\bigr)}.
\]
Thus $\HH=\BB^T\diag(\vvr^2)\BB$ retains its original meaning without materializing a
dense rational sum.
Every source and observer uses the exact rational change $\ssigma_e=\vvr_e'^2/\vvr_e^2-1$
between stored states.

\paragraph{Preserving the weight floor.}
Write $\Delta_{\rm state}=2^{-B_{\rm state}}$ and store the weight on the
shifted grid $\llambda_e=s+\qq_e^{\rm grid}\Delta_{\rm state}$ with integer
$\qq_e^{\rm grid}\ge0$.
Round the ideal nonnegative offset upward, or round and clamp that integer
offset at zero.
The error is at most one mesh unit, the wall $\llambda_e\ge s$ holds exactly, and
the common denominator is $m2^{B_{\rm state}}$.
No-ops retain their stored offset.
Since $\Delta_D\le d_{c,e}\le d_{c,e}/p_e$ whenever $d_{c,e}>0$, the actual
coefficient change obeys the two-point bound
\[
 \ssigma_e=I_e \ssigma_e^{\rm sel},\qquad
 |\ssigma_e^{\rm sel}|\le C d_{c,e}/p_e,
 \qquad \Ex|\ssigma_e|\le C d_{c,e}.
\]
These are the observer's two-point bounds.
The central and residual point-rounding defects enter the numerical reserve
fixed before the mask.

\paragraph{Summing the source normalizer.}\label{par:ram30-source-normalizer}
To compute $\Gamma$ in \eqref{eq:ram27-gamma}, we sum enclosures of the changes
while retaining the exact coefficients.
Stored point operands have $W=O(\log n)$ bits.
Each coefficient, old--new difference, and $\llambda_e|\ssigma_e|$ is a fixed-depth
rational expression, with numerator and denominator lengths at most $C_{\rm
rat}W$.
Thus every nonzero difference or summand is at least $2^{-C_\Gamma W}$ for a
fixed enlarged constant.
After this bound is fixed, sum upward enclosures on a common grid of width at
most $\eta_\Gamma2^{-C_\Gamma W}$.
Exact rational comparison recognizes zero; upward division gives fixed relative
error using a fixed number of words per summand.
Retain the exact coefficients and identity $\vvr_e^2|\ssigma_e|=|\vvr_e'^2-\vvr_e^2|$.
The exact coefficients need not lie on the enclosure grid.
Sum only the enclosures of $\llambda_e|\ssigma_e|$, avoiding a dense exact rational
sum.

\subsection{Rational sampling rates}

Sampling uses a rational enclosure of $|\nnu_c|$, whereas the point map keeps its
exact noise coefficient $\nnu_c$.
The next lemma supplies relative accuracy and an exact zero test without
changing either physical grid.

\begin{lemma}
\label{lem:ram-algebraic-rate-enclosure}
Fix the direction and state grids first, and let $W=O(\log(2+n))$ bound the
logical numerator and denominator lengths of their stored operands, the
represented vertex direction, and the rational design constants.
In particular take $\varepsilon>0$ rational.
For any fixed rational $0<\eta_{\rm rate}<1$, there is a deterministic point
routine returning a rational $\nnu_e^+$ such that
\[
 |\nu_{c,e}|\le\nnu_e^+\le
       (1+\eta_{\rm rate})|\nu_{c,e}|,
 \qquad \nnu_e^+=0\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\nu_{c,e}=0.
\]
It also determines the exact sign of $\nu_{c,e}$, uses $O(\log W)=\O(1)$ word
operations on a fixed number of words, and has no numerical background rate.
Its precision is chosen after $W$ and does not refine either physical storage
grid.
\end{lemma} \begin{proof} Suppress the label and set
\[
 \ZZ=\zz^2,\qquad \RR=(\cc-\BB\yy)/(\mu\ww),\qquad
 \SS=\ff^{-3}-(\vvu^{\max}-\ff)^{-3},\qquad
 \RR_0=\ff^{-1}-(\vvu^{\max}-\ff)^{-1}.
\]
All four quantities are rational fixed-depth expressions in the stored operands,
and $Z>0$.
The shared positive square root $\zz=\sqrt\ZZ$ gives
\[
 \XXi=\RR\sqrt\ZZ,\quad \XXibar=\RR_0\sqrt\ZZ,\quad \DD=\ZZ\SS\sqrt\ZZ,
 \quad \kk=\ZZ^2\SS\RR+\YY(1-\ZZ^2\SS\RR_0).
\]
Thus $\kk$ is evaluated by its exact rational expression, and
\[
 \nnu_c=\alpha_{\nnu}+\beta_{\nnu}\sqrt\ZZ,\qquad
 \alpha_{\nnu}=\frac{ku_c}{\varepsilon\theta},\qquad
 \beta_{\nnu}=-\frac{\RR\pphi}{3\varepsilon\theta}.
\]
Choose $H_{\rm bit}=C_{\rm bits}W\ge1$ so that the numerators and positive denominators of
$\alpha_{\nnu},\beta_{\nnu},Z$ have absolute value at most $2^H$.
The constant $C_{\rm bits}$ is fixed: these expressions have fixed depth, and
the reduced cost uses only the two endpoint coordinates of $\yy$.

\paragraph{Exact zero and sign.}
If $\alpha_{\nnu}=\beta_{\nnu}=0$, return zero.
If exactly one vanishes, use the sign of the other, since $Z>0$.
If both have the same nonzero sign, that is the sign of their sum.
Otherwise compare the rational numbers $\alpha_{\nnu}^2$ and $\beta_{\nnu}^2\ZZ$ by exact cross
multiplication.
Equality means $\nnu_c=0$; otherwise its sign is that of $\alpha_{\nnu}$ when $\alpha_{\nnu}^2>\beta_{\nnu}^2\ZZ$, and
that of $\beta_{\nnu}$ when $\alpha_{\nnu}^2<\beta_{\nnu}^2\ZZ$.
The sign test is necessary: equality of the squares alone does not imply
cancellation when the signs agree.

\paragraph{Separation from zero.}
There is a uniform nonzero separation bound
\[
 \nnu_c\ne0\quad\Longrightarrow\quad |\nnu_c|\ge2^{-8H_{\rm bit}}.
\]
For terms of one sign this follows from their rational lower bounds and $\sqrt
Z\ge2^{-H_{\rm bit}/2}$.
In the opposite-sign nonzero case, $\alpha_{\nnu}^2-\beta_{\nnu}^2\ZZ$ is a nonzero rational with
denominator at most $2^{5H_{\rm bit}}$, and
\[
 |\nnu_c|
 =\frac{|\alpha_{\nnu}^2-\beta_{\nnu}^2\ZZ|}{|\alpha_{\nnu}-\beta_{\nnu}\sqrt\ZZ|}
 \ge\frac{2^{-5H_{\rm bit}}}{2^H+2^{3H_{\rm bit}/2}}
 \ge2^{-8H_{\rm bit}}.
\]

\paragraph{A certified square-root enclosure.}
Write $Z=N_1/N_2$ with positive integers $N_1,N_2$, and take
$R_*=9H_{\rm bit}+ \log_2(16/\eta_{\rm rate}) $.
Form
\[
 \QQ=\left\lfloor\frac{2^{2R_*}N_1}{N_2}\right\rfloor,
 \qquad j=\lfloor\sqrt \QQ\rfloor.
\]
Then $[j2^{-R_*},(j+1)2^{-R_*}]$ encloses $\sqrt\ZZ$.
All these integers have $O(H_{\rm bit})$ bits.
Binary search on bit positions finds an initial power-of-two upper bound for
$\sqrt \QQ$ in $O(\log H_{\rm bit})$ word operations.
Integer Newton iteration $x\leftarrow\lfloor(x+\lfloor \QQ/x\rfloor)/2\rfloor$,
started at that upper bound and stopped when the next value does not decrease,
returns $j$ in $O(\log H_{\rm bit})$ iterations.
The usual Newton error-squaring bound, followed by the final integer check,
proves this count.
It does not use a unit-cost square-root or most-significant-bit instruction.

Multiply the enclosure by the signed rational $\beta_{\nnu}$ and add $\alpha_{\nnu}$ exactly.
Its width is at most $2^H2^{-R_*}\le\eta_{\rm rate}2^{-8H_{\rm bit}}/16$.
For a nonzero value, take the upper endpoint of its absolute value using the
already determined exact sign.
It is positive, rational, and at most $(1+\eta_{\rm rate}/16)|\nnu_c|$.
If a common dyadic output grid is desired, round upward on a grid of width at
most $\eta_{\rm rate}2^{-8H_{\rm bit}}/16$; the displayed lemma still holds.
For the exact-zero case return zero before the square-root calculation.
All rational expressions have $O(H_{\rm bit})$-bit numerators and denominators, so a fixed
enlargement of the eventual arithmetic word suffices.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Using the enclosure.}
Use $\nnu_e^+$ only in sampling rates, with constant-factor rational upper
enclosures of $B_{\nnu,c}$ and $\sqrt{J_e^c}$.
The resulting rational probabilities bound the clipped wall and curvature
targets upward within fixed factors.
The union of these fixed many components has exact rational probability $p_e$,
determined by frozen data even when evaluated lazily after a proposal.

The point map still uses $\TT=\II/p$, $v=Tu_c$, and $\zeta=\TT\pphi$; its noise
coefficient remains $\nnu_c$ in \Cref{lem:ram-computational-identities}.
Increasing the floors preserves safety and variance bounds, and the fixed-factor
increase preserves weighted-rate work bounds.

Choose the rate grid after the logical state and direction bit bounds, before
the exact-stream input exponent and final word width.
It refines only rational probabilities; the state grid and $\UUps_c$ stay fixed.

The direction also has the form $(\alpha_{\nnu}\sqrt\ZZ+\beta_{\nnu})/\kk$ with new rational scalar
coefficients $\alpha_{\nnu},\beta_{\nnu}$.
Its nonzero separation depends on the stored-flow mesh and may be a higher power
of that mesh.
Fixing $\Delta_D$ first avoids circular refinement; its perturbation was charged
above.

\section{Original-edge cores and adaptive pruning}

To avoid full original-edge scans at every recursive level, a degree guard
supplies known references and a game on a fresh sparse sample finds a cut or a
near-expander.
Trimming and degree-bias repair produce an original induced core; a final pass
assigns original labels.
The repair uses weighted pruning charged by stored sample atoms.
Installed cores use unweighted pruning charged per genuine deletion at a
density-dependent price.
Both pruners keep their birth references.

Original graphs are simple and undirected.
An original label is an edge, stored with two direct incidence handles.
A constant number of simple families may be treated separately, for example the
two orientations of a simple directed graph.
A weighted sparse atom is stored once regardless of its weight.
Loops count once in degree and zero in cuts.
References are positive integers fixed at core birth.
A \emph{ghost arc} is a deleted edge retained only in the pruner's private flow
state.
For a reference $r$, write $r(X)=\sum_{u\in X}r_u$, $D_r=\diag(r)$,
$\norm{\xx}_r^2=x^TD_rx$, and $\norm{\xx}_{r,1}=\sum_u r_u|x_u|$.
For a row embedding $X$ the squared norm sums over its columns.
Operator Hilbert--Schmidt norm is
$\norm{\TT}_{\HS,r}=\norm{D_r^{1/2}TD_r^{-1/2}}_F$.

An induced $h$-core has $\partial_{G[A]}(U)\ge h\min\{r(U),r(A\setminus U)\}$.
A near-expander in a larger graph $\GG$ has instead $\partial_G(U)\ge h r(U)$ for
$U\subseteq A$ with $r(U)\le r(A)/2$.
The game returns near-expanders; the subsequent conversion must certify
expansion inside the retained set.
Original-label access counts row scans, probes, and all selected sample
positions, including rejected ones; arithmetic, sparse processing, and indexing
are charged separately.
All words have $O(\log n)$ bits, which includes a fixed polynomial call
horizon; the desired overall failure probability is
$\delta=n^{-O(1)}$.
Constants in $n^{o(1)}$ are absolute and can be large.

The only graph-algorithm inputs are the sparse approximate Cholesky construction
of Kyng--Sachdeva, including its low-degree elimination variant
\cite{KS16:conf}; standard dynamic-tree link, cut, path-minimum, path-add, and
edge-value operations \cite{ST83:journal}; and deterministic exact flow for
integral data in $m^{1+o(1)}$ time with logarithmic capacity and cost factors
\cite[Theorem~1.1]{BCPKLPSS23:conf}.
Flow is used only on explicit sparse networks.
We also use elementary independent scalar and matrix concentration.
\Cref{struct27:sec:finite} supplies finite arithmetic, the sparse flow
reduction, and work caps.

For $\qq>0$, define the density deficit $D(\qq)=1+\max\{0, \log_2(n^2/\qq) \}$.
Each residual phase processes the preceding phase's uncovered edges, starting
with the full input.
Write $h_j$ for the reference-conductance guarantee of its phase-$j$ cores.

\begin{theorem}\label{struct27:thm:main}
Let $\GG$ be a frozen simple graph on at most $n$ vertices with $\qq$ labels.
There is a construction with polynomial confidence that partitions its labels
into cores and an explicit residual of size at most $\ttau= n^{9/8} $.
Put $D_j=D(q_j)$ for the size $q_j>\ttau$ of each residual phase.
For a fixed absolute $A$, put $S_j=AD_j^2$.
Every phase-$j$ core has a known reference $r$, retained from its local
construction, such that
\[
 h_j\ge cD_j^{-5},\qquad
 \min r_u\ge q_j/(4nS_j),\qquad
 \deg_{\rm core}(\UUps)\le r_u.
\]
The construction uses
\[
 O(\qq+n\log(2+\qq/\ttau))\quad\text{original-label work},\qquad
 n^{1+o(1)}\quad\text{additional work}.
\]
Total core vertex population is $n^{1+o(1)}$.
On installing a core, use its actual original degrees as immutable birth
reference and round $\phi$ down within a factor two of $h_j$.
There is a deterministic structure for any adaptive sequence of genuine
deletions, exporting $O(U/\phi)$ live labels after $U$ such deletions, with
total work
\[
 O(q_{\rm birth}+v_{\rm birth}+H_{\rm dep}+UD_j/\phi^2),
 \qquad H_{\rm dep}=O(D_j/\phi).
\]
Every retained live cut has conductance at least $\phi/4$ in that birth
reference.
Exports are not fed back as genuine deletions.
\end{theorem}

Here $q_{\rm birth},v_{\rm birth}$ are birth sizes and $H_{\rm dep}$ is pruning height.
Cores are induced in their creating phase's residual.
For $\qq=0$ return nothing.
Construction is Monte Carlo with a deterministic work cap; pruning is
deterministic conditional on a valid birth certificate.
\Cref{sec:ram27-core-proof} proves these two parts in order.

\section{Maintaining original edges within an attempt}
\label{sec:ram27-owner}

An original label's update cost depends on its current core.
We bound current price moments, price fresh proposals, and charge historical
copying under an admission cap.
This includes small records with expensive deletions.
All prices are frozen before the mask.

Put $m=\Theta(n^2)$, $r=\Theta(n)$, $s=r/m$.
All implicit constants are independent of $n$.
Bounded parallel-label multiplicity permits a fixed number of simple layers
before coefficient bucketing.
In each of the fixed number of metrics, every label has one live analytical
owner: its current core or explicit route.

\paragraph{Core guarantees used below.}
Use the static cover and fixed-birth pruner of \Cref{struct27:thm:main}, with
residual cutoff $\tau_0\asymp n^{9/8}$ and its stated birth-work and population
bounds.
The cover-phase sizes $q_j$ decrease by at least a factor two
(\Cref{struct27:lem:covercharge}).
Write $D_j=D(q_j)$; then $\phi_j^{-1}\le CD_j^5$.
Per genuine deletion, charge $CD_j\phi_j^{-2}$ for pruning and $C\phi_j^{-1}$
exported live labels, through retirement.
Use the theorem's immutable actual-birth-degree references and current cut
certificates.

Store coefficients in dyadic amplitude buckets.
Choose the fixed-reference weighted median $g_C$ from the stored field
coordinates, breaking ties by vertex ID, and share it at both endpoints.
This needs no gauge rounding.
Apply any common scalar normalization to the median too; constant fields then
have exactly zero centered differences.
Let $F_{eo}$ be the coefficient-scaled centered field value at endpoint
occurrence $o$ of label $e$; an explicit route instead uses the scaled edge
difference.
Apply Cheeger's inequality to the core completed to its birth degrees by loops,
centered at the birth-reference median, to get
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ram27-owner-gate}
 \sum_{e,o}\phi_{eo}^2 F_{eo}^2\le C\norm{v}_K^2
\end{equation}
for the appropriate coefficient graph $K$ and vertex field $v$.
If the core containing $e$ was created in cover phase $j$, write $D_e=D_j$.
An explicit route uses its actual edge difference, with $D_e=1$ and $\phi=1$ in
all price and moment bounds.
Header subdivisions do not increase the fixed number of analytical occurrences
per label.

\subsection{Current prices in the mixed-radix counter}

Use powers of two $\tau_0\asymp n^{9/8}$, $R\asymp n^{1/16}$ and $T_0\asymp
m/\log^{1024} n$.
The cutoff limits the population of expensive small records; the lower radix
limits the number of carries paid by each token.
The finitely many $n$ for which $T_0<\tau_0$ are handled by the bounded-input
branch.
Historical admission tokens remain as tombstones after deletion.
A genuine admission or pruner export enters the pending list of its current
amplitude bucket and receives one token; carries only regroup existing tokens.
In each bucket, full $\tau_0$-token blocks enter a counter.
Below $T_0$ its radix is $R$, except for one final power-of-two radix in
$[2,R]$; above $T_0$ it is binary.
A carry rebuilds on the children's current live labels and discards their
private flow state.
A record's final explicit residual stays in that record until a real carry.

\paragraph{Bulk initialization.}
In a bucket with $q_b$ labels, write $\lfloor q_b/\tau_0\rfloor$ in the mixed
radices.
Partition the labels into records of the indicated capacities and a pending list
of fewer than $\tau_0$ labels.
Build each record's cover once, with birth clocks and handles from the current
coefficients.
Since all labels are live, this directly constructs the final
sequential-admission state.

The initial record count is $O(R+\log n)$ per amplitude bucket.
Original label scans sum to $O(m)$, and sparse cover work is
$n^{17/16+o(1)}$; the explicit routes and proxy/header preparation have
the larger $n^{19/16+o(1)}$ allowance.
Grouping labels by bucket and installing scalar/deadline metadata adds
$\O(m)$.
A clean checkpoint refreshes references before zeroing and saving debts.
A retry restores them literally, without random fine reads, and may rebuild the
owner while preserving the saved inner certificates.
Within an attempt, counters persist; the initialization and historical-token
charges are separate.
Let $N$ count all admission tokens so far, including initial tokens and
tombstones.

\begin{lemma}
\label{lem:ram27-current-moments}
Under any predeclared polynomial admission stop $N\le n^A$, for every fixed
$0\le j\le512$,
\[
 \sum_{e\ \mathrm{current}}D_e^j\le C_{A,j}m(\log\log n)^j.
\]
Give a deletion from an explicit route the price one, and replace every core
deletion price by its maximum with one.
If $W_e$ sums these old deletion prices over all affected metric owners, then
\[
 \sum W_e^{12}=\O(m),\qquad
 \sum_{e,o}\phi_{eo}^{-12}=\O(m),\qquad
 \max W_e+\max\phi_{eo}^{-1}=n^{o(1)}.
\]
These are pathwise statements about the frozen state before a mask.
They do not require an event count or a bound on historical copying.
\end{lemma} \begin{proof} For consecutive lower capacities $c_j$ and radices
$r_j$,
\[
 \sum_{c_j<T_0}(r_j-1)c_j=T_0-\tau_0.
\]
Adding the unfinished pending block shows that all lower records have total
capacity less than $T_0$ per amplitude bucket.
There are a constant number of lower levels: the ratio from $\tau_0$ to $m$ has
exponent $7/8$, whereas one full radix has exponent $1/16$.
Thus their record count is $O(R)$ per bucket.
There are $O(\log n)$ amplitude buckets and $O_A(\log n)$ upper records per bucket under
the polynomial stop.

Every live label in a cover phase with $q_j\ge T_0$ has $D_j=\O(1)$.
After an upper record's first cover phase below $T_0$, the remaining input sizes
sum to less than $2T_0$.
The lower-capacity population and these upper-record tails therefore contain at
most $C_A \log^2 nT_0$ labels.
Their deficits are at most $C\log n$.
It follows that
\[
 \sum D_e^j\le C_jm(\log\log n)^j+C_{A,j}\log^2 nT_0\log^j n
 \le C_jm(\log\log n)^j+C_{A,j}m\log^{j+2-1024} n.
\]
Each deletion price is at most $CD^{11}$; constant-layer sums and the fixed
occurrence count give the remaining assertions without independence between
metrics.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Pricing proposals, updates, and reads}

Price each raw proposal for its own work, structural callbacks, and an
old-margin fine read.
The valid-state bounds of \Cref{sec:ram-local} give
\[
 \ww_e\ge c_\star s,\quad
 \sum\ddeltabar_e^{-p}\le Cm,\quad
 \sum d_{Z,e}^{-P}\le CP^2m,\quad p=512,\quad P\ge512,
\]
and $\norm{\dd}_{\ww}+\norm{\nnu}_w\le C\beta_f$.
Use the stored certified tracking scale with
$C_\delta^{-1}\delta\le\ddeltabar\le C_\delta\delta$ for the fixed known
comparison constant $C_\delta$ introduced there.
For every fixed order $j\le36$, the central premise implies $\sum d_Z^{-j}\le
m(CP^2)^{j/P}=O_j(m)$.
Retain the source multiplier $\vartheta$, central derivative magnitude
$G_{Z,e}$, certified curvature upper bound $J_e^c$, and uncapped set $\mathcal
J$ from the physical step.
For a filled source the finite waterfill gives
\[
 \norm{G_{Z,\mathcal J}/\sqrt w}_2\le C\beta_s/\vartheta,
 \qquad J_e^c\le CP G_{Z,e}/d_{Z,e}+CP^2.
\]
In the fully capped branch set $1/\vartheta=0$; $G_{Z,e}=0$ on $\mathcal J$,
even when the source norm lies in the filled range.
Put
\[
 B_*:=\beta_f\sqrt{m/s}=\beta_fm/\sqrt r,\quad
 \zeta_*:=\beta_f^2/(\vartheta B_*),\quad
 V:=B_*+\beta_s^2/\vartheta,
 \qquad \beta_s=\gamma\beta_f.
\]
The implemented upward normalizer can be chosen within a fixed factor of $P
B_*\sqrt{1+\zeta_*}$, so that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ram27-curvature-normalizer}
 B_{\nnu}\ge C\beta_f\sqrt{\sum_{\mathcal J}J_e^c/\ww_e},
 \qquad B_{\nnu}/V\le C_\gamma P(1+\zeta_*)^{-1/2}.
\end{equation}
This is \Cref{lem:ram-global-normalizer} and its finite-direction extension in
\Cref{sec:ram-finite-direction}: use $\widehat B_*\asymp B_*$ and
$V=B_*(1+\gamma^2\zeta_*)$.

The physical base owner has coefficient $\zz^2/\ww$.
Its two endpoint occurrences, together with one direct source-class occurrence,
give nonnegative $F_{eo}$ with
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ram27-physical-envelope}
 \sqrt{\ww_e}\max\{d_e,|\nnu_e|\}\le C\sum_oF_{eo},
 \qquad \sum_{e,o}\phi_{eo}^2F_{eo}^2\le C\beta_f^2.
\end{equation}
For the endpoint terms take $\zz_e|v_y(u)-g_C|/(\mu\sqrt{\ww_e})$.
For the direct term take a fixed multiple of
$\sqrt{\ww_e}(|1-\theta|+|F_e|+|\pphi_e|)$.
The direction estimates and \eqref{eq:ram27-owner-gate} prove both claims, with
fixed envelope factors for coefficient buckets.

For the implemented direction, replace $d,\nnu$ by $d_c,\nnu_c$ and add
