$C_\varepsilon\Delta_D\sqrt{\ww_e}$ to this direct occurrence.
\Cref{sec:ram-finite-direction} gives $|u_c-u|\le2\Delta_D$ and $|\nnu_c-\nnu|\le
C_\varepsilon\Delta_D$.
This adds squared energy $O(\Delta_D^2\sum w)=O(r\Delta_D^2)$; the direction
mesh makes its stopped weighted charge negligible.
It adds no graph occurrence or score read.
Below use these actual directions and the enlarged direct envelope.

The combined point price $R_e$ below is distinct from the counter radix $R$.

\begin{lemma}
\label{lem:ram27-weighted-rates}
Freeze all old core identities, margins, classes and prices, and put
\[
 R_e=1+W_e+\ddeltabar_e^{-3}.
\]
The three dense component families
\[
 \min\{1,C_\delta d_e/(a_{\rm jump}\ddeltabar_e)\},\quad
 \min\{1,k_P|\nnu_e|/d_{Z,e}\},\quad
\min\{1,k_P B_{\nnu}|\nnu_e|\sqrt{J_e^c}/V\},
 \qquad k_P=CP^{12},
\]
where the last two families are restricted to the uncapped source set $\mathcal
J$, have total conditional $R$-priced raw proposal expectation at most
$\O(B_*)$.
The same bound pays the forced source-wall cap list.
The constants may depend on the fixed $a_{\rm jump},\gamma,C_\delta$.
Every actual selected label's $W$-priced structural callbacks and its old-margin
fine read are paid by these prices or by the sparse component rows below.
\end{lemma} \begin{proof} The current moment lemma and the tracking cap give
$\sum R^{12}=\O(m)$.
H\"older, with exponents $3,3,3$, yields
\[
 \sum R^4\phi^{-4}d_Z^{-2}=\O(m),
 \qquad \sum R^2\phi^{-2}=\O(m).
\]
All sums include the fixed number of direct/endpoint occurrences.
Using the derivative bound and then the uncapped source estimate,
\begin{align*}
 S_R&:=\sum_{e\in\mathcal J}\sum_o R_e^2\phi_{eo}^{-2}J_e^c/\ww_e\\
 &\le CP\norm{G_{Z,\mathcal J}/\sqrt w}_2
       \left(\sum R^4\phi^{-4}/(w d_Z^2)\right)^{1/2}
       +CP^2\sum R^2\phi^{-2}/w\\
 &\le C_\gamma P^2(\log\log n)^{32}(m/s)(1+\zeta_*).
\end{align*}
Equations \eqref{eq:ram27-physical-envelope} and
\eqref{eq:ram27-curvature-normalizer} therefore bound the raw curvature envelope
by
\[
 C k_P(B_{\nnu}/V)\beta_f\sqrt{S_R}
           \le C_\gamma k_PP^2(\log\log n)^{16}B_*.
\]
The margin families instead use $\sum R^2\phi^{-2}\ddeltabar^{-2}$ and $\sum
R^2\phi^{-2}d_Z^{-2}$.
Their respective sixth moments and H\"older bound both sums by $\O(m)$;
Cauchy--Schwarz in the owner energy bound completes their price bounds.

If a nonzero source is capped at $c_Zd_Z/P^2$, its actual rounded magnitude is
within a fixed factor of that cap.
Consequently $1\le CP^2|F_e|/d_Z$.
Weighted Cauchy gives the forced price
\[
 CP^2\sum R_e|F_e|/d_{Z,e}
 \le CP^2\beta_s s^{-1/2}
          (\sum R^4)^{1/4}(\sum d_Z^{-4})^{1/4}
 \le CP^2(\log\log n)^{11}B_*.
\]
The flat $\gamma h$ cap is charged in the sparse rows.

Use homogeneous incidence lists and exact original-label thinning.
The untruncated envelopes pay the frozen $R_e$ price of every raw success,
including rejected and duplicate proposals.
Each label has only a fixed number of list occurrences.
Coalescing the component masks by original label can only reduce cost.
Each retained label gets one final packed read at cost
$\O(\bar\delta_{e,\mathrm{old}}^{-3})$, by the supplied one-frame margin
comparison.
Freeze the whole batch's old owners before its coins and detach all genuine
changes before pruning or carrying, so that later bits never acquire a newly
chosen price.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{The sparse component rows.}
The ordinary floor $K\log n\llambda$ has raw mass $O(r\log n)$ using the actual-$\llambda$
bins.
The quadratic floor $KLrwd^2/\beta_s^2$ has raw mass $rn^{o(1)}$ by the
squared version of \eqref{eq:ram27-physical-envelope} and
$\max\phi^{-2}=n^{o(1)}$.
\Cref{lem:ram27-upper-scores} supplies a physical-leverage envelope of mass
$n^{19/16+o(1)}$, including cores and explicit labels, without a dense
leverage table.
The number of nonzero flat source caps is at most
$C\beta_s^2/(s\gamma^2h^2)=O(n^{1/2})$.

All these rows may pay the worst point price $R_{\max}\le n^{6/p+o(1)}$.
For at most $T=n^{3/4+3/p+o(1)}$ frames their largest exponent is
$3/4+3/p+19/16+6/p=1001/512<2$.
List starts, medians, class-header scans and global alias construction cost
$[n(R+\log n)+\tau_0(R+\log n)]n^{o(1)}$ per field, not per output.
Additional inverse, source, and observer fields use their preparation bounds
below.
Numerical background rates have summable total weighted mass at most one.

\subsection{Admissions and historical copying}

\begin{lemma}
\label{prop:ram27-owner-attempt}
Suppose a clean attempt has at most the preceding $T$ frames and its predictable
physical budget is $\sum B_*\le Cm$.
Then its structural, selected-list and metadata work has expected cost
$\O(m)$, with an additional constant attempt-abort probability as small
as desired.
The statement uses a deterministic sharp token cap and actual expected scalar
costs.
\end{lemma} \begin{proof} \emph{Expected genuine-change cost.} First impose the
coarse stop $N\le n^A$, with $A$ fixed large.
This is enough for the predictable current moments and hence the weighted
raw-rate theorem.
Tower expectation at the predictable event that a frame begins gives
\[
 \Ex Z\le \O(m),
 \qquad Z=\sum_{\rm genuine}W_e.
\]
The sparse rows are smaller.
Normalize core deletion prices to dominate $\max\{1,D_C\phi_C^{-2}\}$ and give
explicit routes price one.
There are $N_{\rm init}=O(m)$ initial tokens.
A core receiving $U$ genuine deletions exports at most $16U/\phi_C$ labels in
pruning and $64U/\phi_C$ at retirement (\Cref{struct27:thm:unweighted}).
Carries mint no tokens, and genuine reinsertions number at most the genuine
metric deletions.
Summing gives the cumulative certificate
\[
             N\le N_{\rm init}+81Z.                 \tag{OA.1}\label{eq:ram27-admission-certificate}
\]
A retirement may spend deletion credit accumulated in earlier batches.

\paragraph{A deterministic admission cap.}
Choose a fixed polynomial $B(P,\log\log n)$ and a constant $C_0$ so that $N_{\rm
sharp}=C_0mB(P,\log\log n)$ exceeds an arbitrarily large constant multiple of the
displayed expectation.
Before structural commit, use the batch's prices frozen before its mask to
compute $Z_{\rm pros}=Z+\sum_{e\in\mathrm{batch}}W_e$.
Abort if $N_{\rm init}+81Z_{\rm pros}>N_{\rm sharp}$.
Otherwise \eqref{eq:ram27-admission-certificate} bounds every commit prefix by
$N_{\rm sharp}$.
Compute the test from the enumerated genuine batch in constant work per affected
metric occurrence.
Also guard every insertion and resulting carry by the literal token count, on
every outcome.

Markov's inequality on the coarse-stopped reference, including the proposed
crossing batch, bounds this guard's failure probability by the desired small
constant without conditioning masks on survival.
For large $n$, the sharp cap is below the coarse cap.

\paragraph{Copying and sparse births.}
Each historical token participates in constantly many lower carries and at most
$1+\log_2(1+N_{\rm sharp}/T_0)=\O(1)$ upper carries.
Thus literal live-label copies and original cover passes are pathwise at most
$CN\log\log n$.
The number of births is at most $2N/\tau_0$.
Their sparse construction work is bounded using the sharp cap by
\[
 (N_{\rm sharp}/\tau_0)n^{1+o(1)}
            =n^{15/8+o(1)}=o(m).
\]
Retiring arrays and private state is charged to their birth sizes.
Central, source and tracking class moves use direct handles, paid by
displacement or an already-paid read, and never enter a pruner.
Owner migrations preserve signed clock-minus-baseline debts and due flags.

\paragraph{Scalar costs and the final work cap.}
List and signed-deadline updates cost $\O(1)$ per touched label; header
scans are paid at field preparation.
Exact stream enumeration and rational thinning cost conditional expected $\O(1)$
per start and raw proposal.
Combine these with $\Ex Z$, then impose the attempt work cap.
Markov gives a small constant exhaustion probability; exhaustion aborts the
entire attempt.
\end{proof}

\Cref{sec:ram-scalar-metadata,sec:ram-finite-direction} implement the scalar
operations and encoded update.
This section pays selected reads and admissions; the observer pays autonomous
reads.
\Cref{prop:ram27-contract-closure} combines their accounts.

\section{Finite sparse inverses and physical solves}
\label{sec:ram27-linear}

Fixed sparse inverses support physical directions and later source fields.
We implement low-degree approximate Cholesky \cite[Theorem B.1]{KS16:conf},
sample the physical right-hand side, and test candidates through an exact
residual cache.
Numerical ranges use the ambient $n$, including for small child graphs.

\subsection{Constructing and applying a fixed sparse inverse}

\begin{lemma}
\label{lem:ram27-sparse-inverse}
Let $P$ be a connected graph Laplacian with $q$ stored edge copies, positive
common-dyadic-grid weights in $[n^{-A},n^A]$, and at most $n$ vertices.
Assume also $q\le n^A$ and that its common input mesh is $2^{-b_{\rm in}}$ with
$0\le b_{\rm in}\le A\log_2n$; enlarging the fixed $A$ covers all three bounds.
For fixed $A,c_\star>0$, one can construct, except with probability $n^{-c}$, a fixed
symmetric operator $T_P$ satisfying
\[
 (1-\epsilon)P^\dagger\preceq T_P\preceq P^\dagger
 \quad\hbox{on }\mathbf1^\perp.
\]
For constant $\epsilon$, construction and application use $qn^{o(1)}$ word
work; for $\epsilon=n^{-O(1)}$ they still use this bound with another fixed
logarithmic exponent.
A finite application can have any prescribed inverse-polynomial absolute energy
error on a supplied polynomial-range common-grid vector using $O(\log n)$-bit
words.
The analytical $T_P$ is linear and symmetric; its rounded evaluation need not be
linear.
\end{lemma} \begin{proof} \emph{Elimination and exact sampling.} Write $v$ for
the number of vertices of $P$.
The one-vertex case returns the zero map in constant work; the stated
sparse-work bounds concern the nontrivial connected case $q\ge1$.
Split each input edge into $\rho=\Theta_c(\log^2 n)$ equal copies, choosing
$\rho$ to be a power of two.
A split copy has leverage at most $1/\rho$ relative to $P$.
At each elimination choose uniformly among live vertices of multiedge degree at
most twice the average.
Rejection from uniform live-vertex draws implements this rule with success
probability at least $1/2$.

Use the published clique sampler with its incident list and weighted
distribution frozen for the whole elimination bag.
If it emits a harmonic weight $h=w_1w_2/(w_1+w_2)$, replace it by $\delta\lfloor
h/\delta\rfloor$, discarding zero copies.
The fine dyadic mesh $\delta$ divides the input mesh after splitting.
All weighted choices use the actual current integer grid weights; integer
aliases and uniform-integer rejection give exact choices.

\paragraph{Concentration with downward rounding.}
In original vertex coordinates, let $Z_j$ be the sum of the emitted rank-one
factor terms and the current rounded Schur graph.
The clique expectation identity on that current graph gives
\[
 Z_j=P+\mathcal M_j-\mathcal D_j,\qquad
 \mathcal D_j\succeq0,
\]
where $\mathcal M_j$ sums the centered unrounded clique samples and $\mathcal
D_j$ sums the downward edge errors in original coordinates.
Later triangular factors do not conjugate these errors.
If the two incident copies have leverage at most $1/\rho$, resistance triangle
inequality gives
\[
 h R_P(u,v)\le
 \frac{w_1w_2}{w_1+w_2}
 \left(\frac1{\rho w_1}+\frac1{\rho w_2}\right)=1/\rho.
\]
Rounding preserves this leverage invariant.
The variance calculation below follows the two random choices in an elimination:
first the pivot, then the independent samples in its clique.
At an earlier prefix with $\mathcal M_i\preceq\epsilon_0P$, the negative drift
gives $Z_i\preceq(1+\epsilon_0)P$, as required by the upper-stopped argument.
Set $\epsilon_0=1/4$ and $\overline X=P^{\dagger/2}XP^{\dagger/2}$.
Let $A_i$ indicate that $\mathcal M_j\preceq\epsilon_0P$ for every $j<i$.
In bag $i$, condition first on the past and then on the chosen pivot $u$.
Write $S$ for the current rounded Schur graph, $S_u$ for its incident-star
Laplacian at $u$, and $C_u(S)$ for the clique Laplacian obtained by eliminating
$u$ from that star.
For a centered unrounded roll $X=\YY-\Ex \YY$, $\norm{\overline X}\le1/\rho$ and
\[
 B_i:=A_i\sum_{\rm rolls}\Ex\overline X^2
       \preceq \frac{A_i}{\rho}\overline{C_u(S)},
 \qquad \norm{B_i}\le\frac5{4\rho}.
\]
Here $S\preceq Z_{i-1}$ and $C_u(S)\preceq S$.
With $a$ live vertices the eligible set has at least $a/2$ members, and $\sum_u
C_u(S)\preceq\sum_u S_u=2S$.
Thus, averaging only over the uniform eligible pivot, still conditional on the
past,
\[
 \Ex_u B_i\preceq\frac5{\rho a}\II.
\]
The bags-of-dice bound \cite[Theorems~4.3 and~4.5]{KS16:conf} permits
$\sigma_1=1/\rho$, $\sigma_2^2=5/(4\rho)$, and deterministic
$\Omega_i=5\II/(\rho(v-i+1))$, whose sum has norm at most $5\log v/\rho$.
The predictable indicator $A_i$ is fixed throughout the bag, preserving
conditional independence.
Upper stopping with $\epsilon_0=1/4$ and a sufficiently large constant in $\rho$
gives
\[
 -P/4\preceq\mathcal M_{v-1}\preceq P/4
\]
with any fixed polynomial confidence.
The concentration applies to the centered unrounded samples.

\paragraph{Rounding drift and zero-pivot failures.}
There are at most $\rho q$ live copies.
Low-degree elimination therefore emits at most
\[
 J\le4\rho q\sum_{a=2}^n a^{-1}
       =O(q\log^3n)
\]
samples.
If $w_{\min}$ is the minimum input weight, a path in $P$ gives $R_P(u,v)\le
n/w_{\min}$.
Hence
\[
 \norm{P^{\dagger/2}\mathcal D_{v-1}P^{\dagger/2}}
                       \le J\delta n/w_{\min}.
\]
Choose $\delta\le w_{\min}/(16nJ)$ using the deterministic upper bound for $J$,
with $\delta$ a dyadic divisor of the split input mesh.
The bounds on $q$ and the input mesh make $\delta$ inverse polynomial.
The emitted factor $Z$ then satisfies $11P/16\preceq Z\preceq5P/4$ on the
concentration event.
A zero pivot while at least two vertices remain is a reported failure.
For its analytical continuation, freeze the previous factor sum $F$, the current
graph $S$, and both $\mathcal M,\mathcal D$ at that instant, and append zero
bags for the remaining steps.
The displayed original-coordinate identity is then unchanged, and future
conditional variances are zero.
If $\kk$ vertices have already been eliminated and $a=v-\kk\ge2$ remain, the
isolated live vertex implies $\rank S\le a-2$, while $\rank F\le \kk$.
Thus $Z=F+S$ has rank at most $v-2$ and an extra kernel direction, contradicting
the lower spectral event.
The frozen zero-bag continuation therefore accounts for early reporting within
the same failure bound.

\paragraph{Stable finite factor applications.}
Ground the last eliminated vertex.
The unit triangular factor is $\mathcal \log n=\II-T$, with $T\ge0$ strictly lower
triangular and column sums at most one.
An entry of $\mathcal \log^{-1} n=\sum_{j=0}^{v-2}T^j$ is the visit probability of a
vertex in an acyclic substochastic chain, and is at most one.
Thus both triangular solves have only polynomial error amplification.
Positive pivots are at least $\delta$; pivots and coefficients are polynomially
bounded.
Store each coefficient as a word-sized rational.
After each rational product or division round back to a fixed common vector
grid.
Denominators do not multiply along a solve.
With $s_Z$ factor entries, a sufficiently fine fixed grid makes the total
absolute error as small as prescribed in $O(s_Z)$ operations.
Subtracting the output mean supplies the ungrounded action for zero-sum inputs.

\paragraph{Refining the inverse.}
For $t$ stages define the exact fixed map
\[
 T_P=\sum_{j=0}^{t-1}(\II-\tfrac12Z^\dagger P)^j
                                      \tfrac12Z^\dagger.
\]
Conjugation by $P^{1/2}$ makes it $\II-(\II-\tfrac12P^{1/2}Z^\dagger P^{1/2})^t$.
The parenthesized matrix has spectrum in a fixed subinterval of $[0,1)$, proving
symmetry and the claimed inverse comparison with $t=O(1+\log(1/\epsilon))$.
Sparse products and rounded factor applications implement these stages to the
prescribed accuracy.
On the spectral event, contraction and polynomial conditioning bound all
intermediates.
The \hyperref[par:ram30-finite-arithmetic]{pre-operation guards} reject operands
outside these fixed ranges on every tape; the chosen reserves ensure they pass
on the spectral event.

\paragraph{A cap for random work.}
Each exact uniform-integer rejection trial succeeds with probability at least
$1/2$; combining the range and eligible-vertex tests gives success probability
at least $1/4$ for a pivot trial.
There are at most $K=O(J+v)$ logical random choices, each using a constant
number of such rejection calls.
For their conditional trial counts $G_j$ and $\llambda=\log(8/7)$,
$\Ex[e^{\llambda G_j}\mid \text{previous calls}]\le2$.
Padding to a deterministic upper bound $\KK_0=O(J+v)$ and iterating this
inequality gives
\[
 \Pr\!\left[\sum_jG_j>T\right]\le 2^{K_0}e^{-\llambda T}.
\]
The predetermined cap $T=C_c(J+v+\log n)$ therefore fails with probability at
most $n^{-c}$ after adjusting its constant.
No independence between different rejection counts is assumed.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Grounded blocks.}
Adjoin one ground vertex to handle a grounded Laplacian block.
For a right-hand side $b$, lift it to $(b,-\mathbf1^Tb)$.
Applying the exact Laplacian pseudoinverse and subtracting the ground coordinate
gives the exact inverse of the grounded block.
The same lifting and subtraction transfer the preceding inverse approximations
and finite evaluation guarantees.
Independent connected components are processed separately.

\subsection{Sparse proxies from current cores}

Current core certificates supply the leverage bounds for proxies and the
physical right-hand-side sampler, without a prior inverse.

\begin{lemma}
\label{lem:ram27-upper-scores}
Suppose a maintained owner partitions its original labels into explicit labels
and cores of current fixed-reference conductance at least $\phi_C/4$, with
current degrees at most the birth reference $r_C$.
Suppose all target coefficients in one bucket are within fixed factors of its
representative.
There are dyadic upper leverage scores
\[
 U_e=\min\{1,C\phi_C^{-2}(r_C(u)^{-1}+r_C(v)^{-1})\}
\]
on a core label $e=uv$, with each summand rounded upward within a factor two;
use $U_e=1$ on explicit labels.
They dominate the leverage in the full target graph and satisfy
\[
 \sum_eU_e\le
 [n(R+\log n)+\tau_0(R+\log n)]n^{o(1)}
              =n^{19/16+o(1)}.
\]
They can be sampled through the owner's incidence lists with this same bound on
total raw envelope mass.
No dense leverage table or previous inverse is required.
\end{lemma} \begin{proof} Fixed-reference Cheeger gives, on a core of bucket
coefficient $\gg$,
\[
 K\succeq c_\star \gg L_C\succeq c'\gg\phi_C^2
       (D_{r_C}-r_Cr_C^T/r_C(C)).
\]
The inverse quadratic form of the last matrix at $e_v-e_u$ is
$r_C(u)^{-1}+r_C(v)^{-1}$.
Energy monotonicity for this zero-sum load gives the claimed full-graph
resistance upper bound.
Multiplication by the actual target edge coefficient changes only fixed
constants.
Summing endpoint terms uses $\deg_C^{\rm live}(u)/r_C(u)\le1$.
The owner's vertex and explicit populations give the stated mass.
Endpoint list length times its constant endpoint rate gives the same raw, rather
than only retained, bound.
All rounded scores have one common dyadic grid.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Finite categorical proxy law.}\label{par:ram30-categorical}
For target graph $K=\BB^T\diag(c_\star)\BB$, set $\rho_e=C\epsilon^{-2}\log nU_e$ and fix a
public polynomial integer $B_{\rm pkt}$ larger than every permitted total
proposal mass.
At each of its independent positions retain $e$ with probability $\rho_e/B_{\rm
pkt}$, or return zero.
A retained packet is $c_eB_e^TB_e/\rho_e$.
Its expected sum is exactly $K$, and its $K$-normalized norm is at most
$\epsilon^2/(C\log n)$.
Matrix Chernoff gives a $(1\pm\epsilon/2)$ proxy with the desired conditional
polynomial confidence.
Its expected copy count is $O(\epsilon^{-2}\log n\sum U_e)$ and its corresponding
count tail permits the public sparse-work cap.

An actual private envelope of total $V$ implements this law by activating a
position with probability $V/B_{\rm pkt}$, drawing from its alias, and thinning
to the target rate.
The resulting edge/null probability is independent of the valid envelope.
\Cref{thm:ram-exact-streams} enumerates the active positions by exact skipping.
The trial count never changes with private $V$.
Round each retained positive weight to a common grid with relative error at most
$\epsilon/10$; summing positive semidefinite errors reserves the remaining
spectral tolerance.
Every target coefficient may be a fixed-depth word-sized rational before that
packet rounding.

\subsection{Sampling and solving the physical right-hand side}

The forcing vector may have a dense edge representation.
We sample it before applying the proxy inverse and bound the error in the true
physical metric.

\begin{lemma}
\label{lem:ram27-physical-solve}
Use the state, classes and physical metric $\LL$ of the local step.
Assume the old residual obeys $\norm{\rho}_{\LL^\dagger}\le\beta_s\sqrt\mu$.
Fix any positive constant $c_{\rm sol}\le10^{-2}$ before the construction; its
work constants may depend on $c_{\rm sol}$.
Using the physical-base owner of coefficient $\zz^2/w$, one can construct a
common-grid direction $\xxdir$ with
\[
 \norm{\rho+\BB^T\diag(\YY\zz/\kk)\gg-\LL \xxdir
                       }_{\LL^\dagger}
                  \le c_{\rm sol}\beta_s\sqrt\mu
\]
with any fixed polynomial confidence, in $n^{19/16+o(1)}$ sparse
preparation work.
There is no dense residual multiplication in the algorithm.
\end{lemma} \begin{proof} \emph{The forcing vector and its metric.} Write
$b=\rho+\BB^T\diag(\YY\zz/\kk)\gg$.
The class-constant envelope
\[
 H_j=1.01(|\theta-1|+|\pphi_j|/3)+\varepsilon|F_j|
\]
dominates $|\gg_e|$ for $e\in j$.
Writing $H_e=H_j$ on each class, the direction/source radii give
$\norm{\HH}_{\ww}\le4\beta_f$.
Incidence contraction gives
\[
 \norm{\BB^T\diag(\YY\zz/\kk)\gg}_{\LL^\dagger}
 \le\sqrt\mu\,\norm{\sqrt{\ww\YY/\kk}\,\gg}_2
 \le C\beta_f\sqrt\mu.
\]
The actual metric coefficient is $(\zz^2/w)\YY/(\mu \kk)$, with $\YY/\kk$ bounded above
and below by fixed constants.
The physical owner therefore supplies the preceding upper scores for this
metric, even though $\kk$ and $\mu$ are not structural bucket keys.
Construct a $(1\pm c_\star\gamma)$ proxy $P$ for $\LL$ by the public categorical proxy
law, including its positive weight rounding reserve.

\paragraph{Sampling the right-hand side.}
Put $\vvr_e^{\rm rhs}=w_eH_j^2/(16\beta_f^2)$, so that $\sum \vvr_e^{\rm rhs}\le1$,
and use the rates $\rho_e^{\rm rhs}=Q(U_e+\vvr_e^{\rm rhs})$ with
$Q=C\gamma^{-2}\log n$.
Use these rational rates, or their upward constant-factor encodings, in both the
sampling probabilities and denominators.
At each of a public polynomial number of positions return label $e$ with
probability $\rho_e^{\rm rhs}/B_{\rm rhs}$ and vector packet
\[
      (Y_ez_e/\kk_e)\gg_e B_e^T/\rho_e^{\rm rhs}.
\]
Add these packets to $\rho$.
The analytical sum has expectation $b$.
If $\ell_e\le U_e$ is the actual physical leverage, the undivided packet norm is
\[
 |(Y_ez_e/\kk_e)\gg_e|\norm{B_e^T}_{\LL^\dagger}
       =\sqrt{\mu \ww_e(\YY_e/\kk_e)\ell_e}\,|\gg_e|.
\]
The inequality $\sqrt{aU}/(a+U)\le1/2$ bounds a divided packet by
$C\beta_f\sqrt\mu/Q$.
Its total variance trace is at most $C\mu\beta_f^2/Q$.
Centering the independent positions only decreases covariance and changes the
maximum packet bound by at most two.
The Hilbert-space Bernstein estimate therefore gives error at most $(c_{\rm
sol}/16)\beta_s\sqrt\mu$ after increasing the fixed constant in $Q$ as a
function of $c_{\rm sol}$.

\paragraph{Conserved packet rounding.}
Enclose the scalar packet, round to a common grid, and add opposite endpoint
integers.
The stored sum is zero-sum and its deterministic error is at most
\[
 B_{\rm rhs}\epsilon_{\rm pkt}
                  \max_e\norm{B_e^T}_{\LL^\dagger}.
\]
Polynomial ranges and the public count make this less than $(c_{\rm
sol}/16)\beta_s\sqrt\mu$ with fixed precision on every outcome.
Write $\widehat b$ for the stored vector.

\paragraph{Solving and grounding the returned direction.}
Refine the fixed inverse of $P$ to relative error $\eta=c_\star\gamma$.
If $\delta$ is the proxy error, then on the quotient
\[
 \norm{\II-\LL^{1/2}T_P\LL^{1/2}}
                              \le\eta+2\delta.
\]
Choose their constants so that this norm times $\norm{\widehat
b}_{\LL^\dagger}\le C_b\beta_f\sqrt\mu$ is at most $(c_{\rm
sol}/4)\beta_s\sqrt\mu$.
Evaluate and round $T_P\widehat b$ with energy error at most $(c_{\rm
sol}/16)\beta_s\sqrt\mu$.
Triangle inequality proves the claimed true-metric residual bound.
Round onto the fixed vertex-potential grid within the reserve, then subtract the
ground coordinate.
This exact $O(n)$ operation preserves edge differences and makes $\yy'=\yy-\xxdir$
exact on the same grounded grid.

\paragraph{Implementing the class stream.}
Refine classes into narrow $w$ bins and use the
\hyperref[par:ram30-class-sampling]{class sampler}, with an upward common-dyadic
enclosure $E_j\asymp H_j^2w_j^+$ that preserves zero, and member thinning
probability $H_j^2\ww_e/E_j$.
Header preparation is sparse work; swap deletion and cache updates cost
$\O(1)$ per touched label.
Core endpoint lists supply the upper-score stream.
Total rate mass is $n^{19/16+o(1)}$ and all inverses are sparse.
Fix the direction before the physical mask, charging preparation failure
separately.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Checking the candidate and preparing later inverses}

\begin{lemma}
\label{lem:ram27-candidate-tests}
The residual and source-normalizer tests in the local step can be implemented in
the same sparse preparation row, without a dense candidate scan.
Their passing thresholds certify the required returned-state residual bound.
On a valid old state their abort probabilities are inverse polynomial, after the
local constants are chosen with the test reserves.
\end{lemma} \begin{proof} \emph{Exact candidate residual.} Maintain the vertex
vector $\rho=\BB^T\ff-b_{\rm dem}$ exactly on the common primal grid.
Initially it is zero.
Each selected stored change $\ff'_e-\ff_e$ updates only its two endpoints in a
candidate copy of this vector.
The copy has $n$ entries, and its incidence updates take constant work per
selected label.
Install the copy only on commit.
Thus $\rho'$ is the stored candidate's exact residual, including root rounding.

\paragraph{A certified residual test.}
Retain the old physical proxy and its fixed inverse from the direction
computation.
Let its analytical map $T_{\rm phys}$ satisfy $\frac12\LL^\dagger\preceq T_{\rm
phys} \preceq2\LL^\dagger$.
The scalar-window comparisons give a fixed $C_{\rm cmp}\ge1$ such that
$(\LL')^\dagger\preceq C_{\rm cmp}\LL^\dagger$ on every candidate for which the
scalar premises hold.
Fix $\ttau^2=\beta_s^2\mu/(16C_{\rm cmp})$.
Compute $q=\rho'^TT_{\rm phys}\rho'$ to absolute error at most $\ttau^2/100$, and
accept this test only when the computed value is at most $\ttau^2/4$.
Passing implies $\norm{\rho'}_{\LL^\dagger}^2<\ttau^2$ and hence
$\norm{\rho'}_{(\LL')^\dagger}<\beta_s\sqrt{\mu'}$, since $\mu'\ge\mu/2$.
Conversely an old-metric residual at most $\ttau/4$ passes.
\Cref{lem:ram-residual} supplies this stronger bound at inverse-polynomial
failure probability by choosing $a_{\rm jump},c_{\rm sol}$ smaller and $K$
larger.
No mask is conditioned on this test passing; a failure aborts the attempt.

One fixed-inverse application and dot product have the stated sparse cost.
Polynomial ranges, including the lower bound on $\mu$, permit the error reserve
with fixed word width; the threshold $\ttau^2$ is rational.

\paragraph{The source-normalizer test.}
On the selected list, compute the
\hyperref[par:ram30-source-normalizer]{common-grid upward sum} of actual
$\llambda_e|\ssigma_e|$.
Its zero branch is exact.
A test of $\Gamma^2\le C_{\Gamma,\rm cap}^2\beta_f^2r$, with a fixed
sufficiently large threshold constant, is exact rational word arithmetic and
certifies $\Gamma\le C_{\Gamma,\rm cap}\beta_f\sqrt r$.
The independent normalizer-safety bound includes the upward enclosure factor and
makes its abort probability inverse polynomial.
Both tests finish while the old owners and inverse are still installed.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Source inverse on the old graph.}
Let $\HH$ be the old pre-mask ordinary graph.
Prepare or retain an ordinary proxy $P$ with $3\HH/4\preceq P\preceq5\HH/4$ and
$3P^\dagger/4\preceq T_P\preceq P^\dagger$, then use $T_H=T_P/2$.
This scaling gives $0.3\HH^\dagger\preceq T_H\preceq(2/3)\HH^\dagger$, including the
upper bound required by the raw source moment proof.
Fix this inverse before the physical mask and retain it until all change-source
replicas and old-view clock increments finish.
The candidate coefficients define $b$ while these operations still use the old
$\HH$.

\paragraph{Fine fields after the commit.}
Only after that old-graph work is complete, install the candidate metric and
prepare its fresh fine fields.
Update actual-$\llambda$ dyadic bins on the already enumerated physical commit,
before any new fine read.
Their total upper mass is $O(r)$.
Sampling at rates $C\log n\epsilon_f^{-2}\llambda_e$ gives a fine ordinary proxy,
since $\ttau_e\le C\llambda_e$.
With $\epsilon_f$ a fixed fraction of $\delta_{\min}$ and $\delta_{\min}\ge c_\star
n^{-2/p}$, it has $n^{1+4/p+o(1)}$ copies.
All $\Theta(\log n\delta_{\min}^{-2})$ fine lanes and inverse actions cost
$n^{1+8/p+o(1)}$ per frame.
These bins do not depend on joint classes that are awaiting fine observations.

\section{Exact independent Bernoulli streams on a word RAM}
\label{sec:ram-exact-streams}

Geometric gaps enumerate successes without testing every list position.
We use a published exact sampler inside attempts.
The one-time endpoint routines use a finite implementation coupled to the same
law, with a separately budgeted failure probability.

Write $N=2+n$.
Fix a constant $A_{\rm st}$ before the word length.
A call supplies integers $0\le N_1\le N_2\le N^{A_{\rm st}}$, $N_2\ge1$, the rational $p=N_1/N_2$,
and a remaining length $0\le d_{\rm st}\le N^{A_{\rm st}}$.
The declared reference horizon has at most $N^{A_{\rm st}}$ calls.
For $\bar p=1-p$, the truncated failure gap has law
\[
 \Pr(G=j)=p \bar p^j\quad(0\le j<d_{\rm st}),\qquad
 \Pr(G=d_{\rm st})=\bar p^d.
\]
Handle $d_{\rm st}=0$ and $p=0,1$ directly.
All other calls use fresh tapes.

\subsection{Exact gaps and adaptive streams}

Bringmann--Friedrich~\cite[Theorem~5]{BF13:conf} sample this truncated law
exactly in expected time
\[
 O(1+\log(\min\{1/p,d_{\rm st}\})/w)
\]
on a word RAM with $w=\Omega(\log\log(1/p))$.
For a rational $p$ whose numerator and denominator occupy a constant number of
words, the rational implementation has polynomial worst-case space in $d_{\rm st}$ and
$1/p$.

Its input convention also supplies magnitude exponents for $p,d_{\rm st}$ and
approximations to the normalized mantissa of $p$.
Binary search among bit positions finds both exponents in $\O(1)$ work; rational
division supplies every requested precision, with multiword operations charged
by their actual cost.
Thus a sufficiently large fixed $w=\Theta(\log n)$ gives polynomial address space and
conditional expected gap cost $\O(1)$, including setup.
Finite expected cost also gives almost sure termination.

\begin{theorem}
\label{thm:ram-exact-streams}
Under these input bounds, enumerating a length-$d_{\rm st}$ independent Bernoulli($p$)
stream costs $\O((\Ex Z+1))$ in expectation, where $Z$ is its number of
successes.
More generally, an adaptive computation making $C_{\rm gap}$ calls costs $\O(\Ex
C_{\rm gap})$ in expectation.
Each call has the stated conditional kernel given the history fixing its inputs.
For fixed list contents and rates, the returned indicators are independent
Bernoulli variables; disjoint list tapes give independent vectors.
Rates chosen from earlier outputs need not give unconditionally independent
outputs.
\end{theorem} \begin{proof} A stream needs at most $Z+1$ gap calls, including
its exhausted suffix.
The tower rule with predictable call-start indicators sums the uniform
conditional cost bound.
We retain the looser $\O(1)$ price used by the scalar ledger.

For a fixed indicator vector with $z$ successes, let $g_1,\ldots,g_z$ be the
failure gaps preceding them and $g_{z+1}$ the final failure suffix.
Since $\sum_i g_i=d_{\rm st}-z$, fresh gap kernels give probability
\[
 \left(\prod_{i=1}^{z}\bar p^{g_i}p\right)\bar p^{g_{z+1}}
       =p^zq^{d-z}.
\]
This proves the product law, including $p=0,1$ and empty suffixes.
For adaptive lists, condition at each start before its fresh tape.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Finite endpoint gaps.}\label{par:ram-stream-endpoint}
The cold and terminal routines need an all-tape work cap, without repeating
their dense scans.
Use the following separate implementation there.
Read $b_0=B \log n$ bits specifying a uniform dyadic cell of width $2^{-b_0}$, and
couple it to a continuous uniform $U$ in that cell.
The ideal gap is determined by the ordered thresholds $\bar p^j$, $1\le j\le d_{\rm st}$.
Binary-search these thresholds using outward-rounded repeated squaring on a
dyadic mesh $\zeta$.
Enclose $\bar p$ on this mesh first, and clamp all power intervals to $[0,1]$.
Each enclosure has width $O(d_{\rm st}\zeta)$: multiplication on $[0,1]$ adds operand
errors, and the repeated-squaring expression for $\bar p^j$ has at most $O(j)$ leaf
and multiplication errors when unrolled.
The actual evaluation uses only $O(\log n)$ multiplications.

If the uniform cell overlaps a queried power interval, report failure; otherwise
the comparison is exact.
Every completed call returns the ideal gap on this coupling, and its work is
deterministically $O(\log^2 n)$.
All possible ambiguous cells lie within $2^{-b_0}+O(d_{\rm st}\zeta)$ of the at most $d_{\rm st}$
thresholds.
Thus
\[
 \Pr(\text{failure}\mid\text{call inputs})
       \le C\bigl(d_{\rm st}2^{-b_0}+d_{\rm st}^2\zeta\bigr).
\]
Choose $B$ and $\zeta=2^{-D \log n}$ after $A_{\rm st}$ and the desired confidence.
A union bound over the declared horizon makes the total coupling failure
arbitrarily small inverse polynomial.
No failed gap is redrawn.
These $O(\log^2 n)$ costs occur only in sparse endpoint preparation; restartable
dense attempts use the exact sampler above.

\subsection{Thinning incidence proposals to original labels}

Thin each original label once, at its first incidence proposal.

\paragraph{Envelope domination and the first proposal.}
Suppose one target component has a fixed number $k$ of rational rate
contributions $r_{ej}\ge0$ and desired probability $p_e=\min\{1,\sum_{j=1}^k
r_{ej}\}$.
Suppose each rate has at most $N_2$ nonnegative incidence envelopes, where $N_2$ is
fixed, and $r_{ej}\le\sum_{x=1}^b a_{ej,x}$.
Zero envelopes may pad a shorter list.
The value $N_2=3$ permits the two endpoint occurrences and a direct class
occurrence without identifying them.
An incidence occurrence is put in a homogeneous random-access list with a
constant dyadic proposal probability in
\[
 [\min\{1,bk a_{ej,x}\},\ \min\{1,2bk a_{ej,x}\}].
\]
Freeze the lists, incidence probabilities, and targets before their independent
proposal tapes.
At label $e$'s first proposal, compute its incidence-union probability $q_e$,
use an independent exact $p_e/q_e$ coin, and stamp it processed even on
rejection.
At least one envelope is at least $(\sum_jr_{ej})/(bk)$, so $q_e\ge p_e$,
including the saturated case.
Thus the retained label has probability exactly $p_e$.
The product-vector identity makes incidence indicators independent.
Different labels use disjoint groups of them and independent retention tapes, so
their retained indicators are independent.
With all component inputs frozen, apply this construction separately to each
prescribed component, with fresh stamps and tapes.
Their union is still independent between labels and has probability
$1-\prod_j(1-p_{e,j})$.

\paragraph{Exact coins and raw work.}
For probability $N_1/N_2$, draw a uniform integer in
$\{0,\ldots,2^{\lceil\log_2N_2\rceil}-1\}$, reject draws at least $N_2$, and accept
the coin if the retained draw is less than $N_1$.
Its conditional expected trial count is less than two.
All denominators here contain a fixed number of word-sized factors.

Charge every list start and raw proposal, including rejections, at its scalar
and label-evaluation prices.
List maintenance and weighted envelope bounds are supplied separately.
Indexed stamps cost $O(1)$ and reset only at a paid rebuild or the polynomial
horizon.

\paragraph{Use with an attempt work cap.}
Couple an attempt to the uncapped same-tape computation until its work cap.
The exact sampler has polynomial memory; only the capped implementation stores
an actual-work counter.
Apply Markov's inequality to the unconditional stopped expected cost.
A cap restarts the whole attempt from its checkpoint, never an individual slow
gap.
The law is not conditioned on eventual attempt success.

\section{Scalar classes and central inspections}
\label{sec:ram-scalar-metadata}

Cheap central inspections maintain representatives without fine leverage reads.
Signed deadlines locate inspections; a two-window scheduler charges them to
motion, paid visits, or handle moves.
Joint classes supply feedback and potential certificates.
Clocks and deadlines use a fixed common integer grid with guarded polynomial
ranges.
Fix their key universe before the arithmetic word, whose enlargement must leave
the keys unchanged.

\subsection{Signed deadlines and lazy initialization}

\begin{lemma}
\label{lem:ram-deadline-index}
For keys in $\{0,\ldots,2^b-1\}$ with $b=O(\log n)$, a word RAM with polynomial
address space supports insertion, deletion, minimum, and maximum in $O(\log b)$
worst-case time per changed key, except that minimum and maximum are constant
time.
A new empty instance costs $O(1)$ after one shared $O(\log n)$ size-table
preparation.
Its actual touched memory is charged to its actual operations; no universe-sized
initialization is performed.
\end{lemma} \begin{proof} For $b\le1$, use a constant-size record.
Otherwise use a van Emde Boas recursion, separating a key into a high $ b/2 $-bit cluster number and a low $\lfloor b/2\rfloor$-bit key.
A node stores its minimum, its cached maximum, a summary of nonempty clusters,
and recursively represented children.
Only the minimum is excluded from the child clusters; the maximum remains there
unless the set is a singleton.
Insert into an empty child in constant time and recurse only on the summary;
otherwise recurse only on that child.
The analogous deletion rule makes its sole nonconstant recursive call on the
child if it stays nonempty, and on the summary if that child becomes empty.
Deleting the minimum obtains the next minimum from the summary minimum and that
child's minimum, then follows this same rule.
Thus each operation satisfies $T(b)\le T( b/2 )+O(1)$, including
cases with an empty or singleton set.
Store duplicate-key labels in a doubly linked bucket; only the first insertion
or last deletion changes the integer key set.

For explicit addressing, precompute the reserved recursive block sizes $S(b)$ by
\[
 S(b)=O(1)+S( b/2 )
                    +2^{ b/2 }S(\lfloor b/2\rfloor).
\]
The child address is its parent's base plus the summary block and its high-key
index times the fixed child-block size.
The recurrence gives polynomial address space $S(b)=2^{O(b)}$; one shared table
for $b=0,\ldots,b_{\max}$ costs $O(\log n)$.

To avoid clearing reserved blocks, use an initializable-array dictionary for
node cells.
An array $\mathrm{pos}[i]$ may initially contain arbitrary machine words.
A stack $\mathrm{key}[0],\ldots,\mathrm{key}[k-1]$ records initialized cells.
Cell $i$ is initialized exactly when $0\le\mathrm{pos}[i]<k$ and
$\mathrm{key}[\mathrm{pos}[i]]=i$; only after the range test is that stack entry
read.
Otherwise its value is the empty default.
Initializing a cell appends $i$ and fills its constant-size record.
Each tree gets a fresh disjoint reserved block, abandoned on discard; old
descendants are never reused by resetting only the root.
The polynomial instance count bounds all addresses.
Fix that count and the key width before choosing the arithmetic word.
\end{proof}

A signed clock $C$ accumulates field increments.
For each attached label, $B_e^{\rm clk}$ is its baseline at the last inspection
and $R_e$ its allowed radius; the signed debt is $C-B_e^{\rm clk}$.
Keep two deadline indices, storing upper deadlines $B_e^{\rm clk}+R_e$ and lower
deadlines $B_e^{\rm clk}-R_e$.
When the clock is changed, repeatedly report upper deadlines at most $C$ and
lower deadlines at least $C$.
The first report queues the original label and removes all its fixed-number
deadline handles, suppressing duplicate service in that frame.
There is $\O(1)$ work per insertion, deletion, or report and $O(1)$ work for a
clock change that reports nothing.
Shift signed integers by a fixed offset into the key universe.

When a handle moves to a new clock, preserve its signed debt by setting
$B_e^{\rm clk,new}=C^{\rm new}-(C^{\rm old}-B_e^{\rm clk,old})$.
Preserve an already due flag.
Its radius stays fixed until an actual inspection.
If a change of occurrence representation does not preserve individual debts (for
example two core endpoint occurrences becoming one explicit occurrence), queue a
cheap exact central inspection at that paid handle move.
That inspection has the same scalar price as the move and uses no fine-score
read.

\subsection{The two-window central scheduler}

For a central representative $b\in(-1,1)$ choose a downward dyadic radius within
a factor two of
\[
 \rho(\vvsbar)=c_{\rm cen}P^{-8}\min\{1-|\vvsbar|,P^{-1}\}.
\]
Maintain the outer invariant $|e-b|\le2\rho(\vvsbar)$ between inspections.
At an inspection, enclose the current $e$ in a certified interval of radius at
most $\rho_{\min}/1000$.
Retain $b$ only if this interval lies in $[b-\rho(\vvsbar),b+\rho(\vvsbar)]$; otherwise
choose a new dyadic center $b'$ for which it lies in
$[b'-\rho(b')/4,b'+\rho(b')/4]$.
Passing inspections reset clock baselines but retain the center and
tracking-score epoch.

Such centers can be located with exponent comparisons and quotient.
For $d_Z=1-|\vvsbar|\le P^{-1}$ use overlapping dyadic margin shells and spacing a fixed
small fraction of $c_{\rm cen}P^{-8}2^{-j}$ in the shell $d\asymp2^{-j}$.
For $d>P^{-1}$ use spacing a small fraction of $c_{\rm cen}P^{-9}$.
There are $\O(\log n)$ centers on a polynomial margin range.
The overlaps and spacing smaller than $\rho/100$ ensure the inner-quarter
placement, including a shell-boundary point.

\begin{lemma}
\label{lem:ram-central-scheduler}
Suppose selected labels receive a final central inspection.
Suppose the physical owner supplies a fixed-number occurrence decomposition for
the clock field satisfying
\[
 \sum_{e,o}\phi_{eo}^2F_{eo}^2\le C\beta_f^2,
 \qquad
 F_{e,v}=\zz_e|h_v-g_C|/\sqrt{\ww_e},
 \qquad h=v_y/(\varepsilon\mu\theta),
\]
using one common core gauge at both endpoints.
An explicit route uses its actual edge difference and $\phi=1$; its per-field
header evaluation is paid in the sparse row.
Assume $\sum\phi^{-4}=\O(m)$ and $\Psi_Z\le CP^2m$.
Then the scheduler preserves its outer invariant, and through a clean attempt
its autonomous central inspection work is
\[
 O\left(\O(1)
                    [m+\sum_t B_{*,t}+N_{\rm move}]
       +\O(1)N_{\rm paid}\right).
\]
Here $N_{\rm move}$ counts paid occurrence-representation moves that require an
inspection, and $N_{\rm paid}$ counts selected or already paid fine visits.
Under the owner and observer attempt budgets this is $\O(m)$ plus the
supplied sparse work.
Changing the central class as part of such an inspection does not itself create
a new occurrence-representation move: the same core-vertex clocks and gauge are
retained, and only list membership changes.
Thus $N_{\rm move}$ does not recursively count the inspections whose number this
bound is proving.
\end{lemma} \begin{proof} \emph{Clocks and finite representation.} An unselected
label has fixed $\vvr_e=\zz_e/\ww_e$ and its exact central increment is
\[
 \Delta\vvs_e=\vvr_e B_eh+
              \frac{(1-\theta)C_e}{\varepsilon\theta}.
\]
Maintain signed clocks for its endpoint occurrences and a global positive clock
with increment $(1+\varepsilon)(1-\theta)/(\varepsilon\theta)$.
At an inspection save their baselines.
Use endpoint radii within fixed factors of $\rho(\vvsbar)/\vvr_e^+$ and a common radius
within fixed factors of $\rho(\vvsbar)$, where $\vvr_e\le \vvr_e^+\le2\vvr_e$.
Choose the fixed factors so that the sum of all pre-deadline possible movements
is at most $\rho(\vvsbar)/4$; for two endpoints, radii in
$[\rho/(24\vvr^+),\rho/(12\vvr^+)]$ and common radius in $[\rho/24,\rho/12]$ suffice.
Their positive lower bounds permit the work charge; owner moves translate signed
debts exactly.

Round each frame's $h_v=v_{y,v}/(\varepsilon\mu')$ onto the fixed dyadic clock
mesh $\Delta_C$.
Use one common-grid gauge at both endpoints of a core occurrence; explicit
routes use the rounded endpoint difference.
Clock increments, baselines and radii are integers in these units.
For public horizon $T_{\max}$ and $\vvr_e\le a_{\max}$, the unselected-coordinate
discrepancy over an inspection interval is at most
\[
 2T_{\max}a_{\max}\Delta_C.
\]
Choose the mesh as in \Cref{sec:ram27-parameter-hierarchy} so this is below
$\rho_{\min}/1000$ and total normalized rounding variation over all labels and
frames is at most one.
Include the perturbation of the energy bound and upward rounding of the common
positive increment in that reserve.
Baselines and due flags then move by exact integer arithmetic.

\paragraph{Validity through the end of a frame.}
In the following overshoot estimates, $h$ is the scalar step size of
\Cref{sec:ram-local}; the vertex clock field above enters through its
accumulated increments.
At an inspection the true point is within $\rho(\vvsbar)$ of the retained or new
center.
Before the next deadline, the clock triangle inequality gives further motion at
most $\rho/4$.
At the first crossing, buffer the label until the completed frame.
An unselected label has $p_e<1$, so the wall floor gives $|\nnu_e|\le
d_{Z,e}/k_P$.
Its last unselected increment is $\FF_e-\nnu_e$, hence has magnitude at most
$\gamma h+d_{Z,e}/k_P$, plus the numerical reserve.
The ratio
\[
 \frac{d_Z}{P^{12}\rho(\vvsbar)}
 \le C\max\{P^{-4},P^{-3}\}
\]
is uniformly small after the multiplier in $k_P$ is chosen.
Also $d_Z\ge c_\star n^{-2/p}$ from the central cap and $P\ge p$; therefore $h/\rho\le
Cc_h n^{-1/p}P^{-7}$.
Choose $c_h$ small and the numerical grid fine so the total last-frame overshoot
is less than $3\rho/100$.
The old error, unreported movement, and last-frame overshoot sum to less than
$2\rho$.
Selected labels are forced into the final queue, and no next physical query uses
their old representative before the queue is drained.

For the finite step use $\nnu_c$ and $\FF_c=F+O(\Delta_D)$;
$\Delta_D\ll\rho_{\min}$ preserves the bound without dividing by a sampling
probability.

\paragraph{Charging inspections to motion.}
Use proof-only credit equal to absolute variation divided by each account's
fixed radius, truncated at one.
A deadline report consumes a fixed positive amount; a passing inspection creates
none.
Transport preserves credit and debt, while selected/fine visits and paid
representation moves pay their own resets.
Thus autonomous reports are bounded pathwise by normalized variation,
initialization, and paid resets, including credit accumulated in earlier frames.

For endpoint clocks, Cauchy--Schwarz in the owner energy bound gives
\[
 \sum_{e,o}\frac{\vvr_e^+|\Delta C_{eo}|}{\rho(\vvsbar_e)}
 \le C\beta_f\left(\sum_{e,o}
       \frac{\phi_{eo}^{-2}}{\ww_e\rho(\vvsbar_e)^2}\right)^{1/2}
 \le \O(B_*).
\]
Indeed $\rho^{-2}\le CP^{18}d_Z^{-2}$ and $\sum\phi^{-2}d_Z^{-2}\le
(\sum\phi^{-4})^{1/2}(\sum d_Z^{-4})^{1/2}$, while $w\ge3s$ and $\sum
d_Z^{-4}=O(m)$ uniformly in $P$.
For the common clock,
\[
 \sum_e\frac{(1-\theta)}{\rho(\vvsbar_e)}
 \le CP^9(\beta_s/\sqrt r)\sum_ed_{Z,e}^{-1}
 \le CP^9 B_*.
\]
Multiplying the lifetime reports by the $\O(1)$ deadline and
certified-point price proves the result.
Central evaluation uses only stored $\ff,\llambda,c_\star,\mu$ and two vertex potentials.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Transaction order.}
Use Steps~\ref{item:ram30-old-work}--\ref{item:ram30-refresh}: finish old-view
increments before the physical/owner commit, preserve queued flags through
moves, and inspect the final queue before installing final classes.
Clock moves preserve their debts or request the paid cheap inspection above.
No new physical query uses central or tracking metadata whose final service is
pending.

\subsection{Joint class implementation and certificates}
\label{sec:ram30-joint-classes}

\paragraph{Membership and class totals.}
The joint metadata key is the tuple of a certified tracking cell, central
center, narrow actual-$\llambda$ bin, and guard flag.
Keep the tracking cell until an already paid fine inspection supplies its
replacement.
A central-only visit changes only the central key.
Actual-$\llambda$ bins and guard flags change only on selected physical labels.
Recomputing waterfill branches changes headers, not member lists.

The tracking centers can be chosen in overlapping dyadic margin shells with
spacing $c_\star\delta/p$, giving $O(p\log n)$ centers.
A supplied fine observation must guarantee
$|\vvp_e/\kappa-x_{j(e)}|\le\ddelta_e/(256p)$ until its next inspection.
Narrow multiplicative bins for $\llambda$ have $O(\log n)$ boundaries and can be
located by binary search in $\O(1)$ comparisons, or by certified scalar
logarithms.
The central construction supplies $\O(\log n)$ centers.
Thus the total number of possible joint headers is $n^{o(1)}$, independent of
$m$.

Store each class and homogeneous incidence list in a compact array with
original-label back-pointers.
Append insertion and swap deletion cost $O(1)$ for each of a label's fixed
number of roles.
Header scans, aliases, and powers belong to preparation.
Test the exact count $N_j=|j|$ first: empty headers contribute zero and evaluate
no barrier, gradient, curvature, breakpoint, or reciprocal.
The potential bound controls only occupied headers.

\paragraph{Sampling from a class.}\label{par:ram30-class-sampling}
For supplied rational member weights within a fixed factor, propose a uniform
class position and retain it with its exact weight divided by the class upper
bound.
The conditional expected trial count is constant.
For weights $H_j/\ww_e$, the class header factor is constant and the narrow
$\llambda$ bin makes $\ww_e$ narrow; use $H_j/w_{j,\min}$ as the envelope.
Round the class upper envelopes upward onto one dyadic common grid within a
fixed factor (zero stays zero).
Their count-weighted masses are then integer multiples of one mesh and can be
summed exactly in a fixed number of words; no product of all class denominators
is formed.
Choose classes from those masses using a header alias table and exact rational
coins.
Thinning gives the exact requested categorical law, including a null outcome
when the prescribed packet law requires one.
Use the envelope normalizer directly; no dense exact $\sum1/\ww_e$ is needed.

\paragraph{Computing the tracking feedback.}
Use $\pi=\llambda(1+\varepsilon\vvs)$, $\GG=\nabla_{\vvp}\Psi_R$, and the mixed norm
$\mathcal N$ of \Cref{sec:ram-local}.
The next lemma constructs the required feedback before the physical mask.
Its optimization coefficients $b_j$ are local to the proof; they are unrelated
to the central representatives denoted by $b_j$ below.

\begin{lemma}
\label{lem:ram-class-feedback}
Suppose there are $J=n^{o(1)}$ joint classes.
Within a class the weights $\pi_e$ differ by a factor at most $1+\eta_c$, and a
class-constant signed observation obeys $|\widehat
G_e-G_e|\le\eta_c(|G_e|+C_{p,\kappa})$.
Suppose the class counts and positive masses $W_j=\sum_{e\in j}\pi_e$ are
available with arbitrarily small fixed relative error.
For sufficiently small fixed $\eta_c$, one can construct
\eqref{eq:ram-feedback-input}, including its guard, in $\O(nJ)$ frame work.
This does not require a computed gradient-normalized sampling rate.
\end{lemma} \begin{proof} \emph{Reduction to class magnitudes.} For the observed
objective, replacing a feasible vector by its unweighted class mean preserves
the objective and cannot increase its infinity norm.
Jensen's inequality and the within-class weight ratio increase its weighted norm
by at most $\sqrt{1+\eta_c}$.
A small rescaling thus makes class-constant optimization arbitrarily close to
optimization over the full mixed ball.
Replacing $G$ by $\widehat G$ changes any mixed-ball objective by at most
$\eta_c h(\norm{G}_{\mathcal N^*}+B_R)$, by coordinatewise sign invariance of
the mixed norm and duality.

\paragraph{A one-parameter optimization.}
Let $N_j=|j|$ be the number of coordinates in class $j$.
For a trial maximum coordinate $v\in[0,h]$, write $b_j=N_j|\widehat G_j|$ and
solve
\[
 \max\sum_jb_jx_j:\quad 0\le x_j\le v,\quad
 \sum_jW_jx_j^2\le((h-v)/M)^2.
\]
The solution is $x_j=\min\{v,\alpha b_j/W_j\}$.
Sort the $J$ breakpoints in $\alpha$ and solve the resulting quadratic norm
constraint with certified arithmetic.
Let $v_*$ be the infinity norm of a nonzero optimum on the mixed-ball boundary.
It is at least $v_{\min}=h/(1+M\sqrt{\sum_jW_j})$.
Test a geometric grid of $v$ between $v_{\min}$ and $h$ with fixed ratio
$1+\eta_c$.
If $v\le v_*<(1+\eta_c)v$, scaling the optimum by $v/v_*$ keeps it feasible for
this trial, because $(v/v_*)(h-v_*)\le h-v$.
It retains at least a $1/(1+\eta_c)$ objective fraction.
There are $O(\log n)$ trials.
Choose the best, assign the opposite observed-gradient sign, and reduce the
radius slightly to cover the mass and scalar enclosures.
These fixed small losses can leave direct descent greater than
$0.98h\norm{G}_{\mathcal N^*}$.

Use hysteresis guard thresholds $(1+\kappa/10)s$ and $(1+\kappa/5)s$.
For $a_0\le\kappa/20$ these give the required lower-wall buffer.
Guarding only deletes downward coordinates and cannot increase the norm.
A deleted beneficial downward coordinate has positive true gradient only for
$0<t\le\log(1+\kappa/5)$, where it is bounded by the fixed baseline.
Its total lost objective is at most $ChB_R$.
Update flags only on selected labels, since the other $\llambda_e$ are literal
no-ops.

\paragraph{Maintaining the class masses.}
The positive class mass has the identity
\[
 W_j=\sum_{e\in j}\llambda_e(1-\XXibar_e)+
 \mu^{-1}\left[\sum_{e\in j}\llambda_ez_ec_e/\ww_e
 -\yy^TB^T(\mathbf1_j\llambda \zz/\ww)\right].
\]
Cache the scalar and incidence-vector coefficients by rounding each square-root
expression to a common integer grid.
A selected change or enumerated migration updates only its own scalar entries
and two incidence endpoints; the $J$ vertex dot products cost $O(nJ)$.
The true mass is at least $(1-\varepsilon)sN_j$.
Choose each term's absolute precision after polynomially bounding the number of
terms and the current $\mu^{-1},\yy$, so the entire header error is at most
$\eta_c(1-\varepsilon)s/100$.
This gives the required relative enclosure for every nonempty class, even when
large terms cancel.
Remove exactly the rounded contribution previously inserted; the cache then sums
only current terms and accumulates no historical rounding error.
Certified classes and enumerated migrations have their separately charged
maintenance costs.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Computing the central source.}
Here $G_j=|\psi_P'(b_j)|$, $J_j=\psi_P''(b_j)$, and $G_j^c,J_j^c$ are certified
upper enclosures.
For this waterfill $W_j=\sum_{e\in j}\ww_e$ and $\widetilde w_j=W_j/N_j$; selected
scalar updates and enumerated migrations maintain them.
Sort the nonzero-$G_j^c$ header breakpoints:
\[
 t_j=\frac{\widetilde w_j}{G^c_j}
            \min\{c_Zd_{Z,j}/P^2,\gamma h\}
\]
Between breakpoints the squared source norm is a fixed capped sum plus
$\vartheta^2$ times a fixed positive uncapped sum.
Choose the rational parameter by the rule below and store the literal minimum
\[
 F_j=-\sgn(b_j)
 \min\{c_Zd_{Z,j}/P^2,\gamma h,
                         \vartheta G^c_j/\widetilde w_j\};
\]
headers with $G_j^c=0$ return $F_j=0$.
Each coordinate is a fixed-depth rational with a fixed word bound; no further
coordinatewise rounding is used.

First test the fully capped branch of \eqref{eq:ram-central-source}.
For fixed $0<\eta<1/16$, enclose its squared norm with absolute error at most
$\eta\beta_s^2/100$.
If its upper endpoint is at most $\beta_s^2$, return the fully capped source
with $\vartheta^{-1}=0$, also when it lies in the filled range.
If this test fails, the fully capped source has true norm greater than
$(1-\eta/4)\beta_s$.
Monotonicity permits a rational $\vartheta$ with certified norm in
$[(1-\eta)\beta_s,(1-\eta/2)\beta_s]$.
Use sorted breakpoints and interval bisection, with the interval-sum mesh
smaller than a fixed fraction of this gap.
All nonzero breakpoints, coefficients and the search interval have polynomial
upper and lower bounds, so the header work is $n^{o(1)}$.
Fixed-grid interval sums avoid products of unrelated denominators.
They choose only $\vartheta$; the literal minimum above defines every
coordinate.

\paragraph{Derivative enclosures and guards.}
For each occupied header, the \hyperref[par:ram30-central-powers]{central-power
construction} constructs fixed-grid rational enclosures $G_j\le
G^c_j\le(1+\eta)G_j$ and $J_j\le J^c_j\le(1+\eta)J_j$ in $n^{o(1)}$ work; at
$b_j=0$, set $G^c_j=0$ exactly.
Skip empty headers first.
The cited construction uses rounded powers and pre-operation guards on every
tape, aborting before overflow; valid inputs pass.
Source caps and uncapped membership are exact rational comparisons.
The literal rational source therefore meets the hypotheses of
\Cref{thm:ram-central} and \Cref{lem:ram-global-normalizer}.
Their waterfill and comparison-source bounds apply to this construction.

\paragraph{Potential certificates.}
Compute the potential certificates by summing the positive header counts times
their center potentials, skipping zero counts before evaluating those
potentials.
Tracking relative margin error gives a summand ratio less than $e^{1/128}$;
central outer error $2\rho$ gives ratio $\exp(O(P^{-7}))$.
Outward fixed-grid power evaluation therefore gives computable $U_R,U_Z$ with
\[
 \Psi_R\le U_R\le2\Psi_R,\qquad
 \Psi_Z\le U_Z\le2\Psi_Z.
\]
Repeated squaring uses $\O(1)$ scalar operations for a central power, with all
errors enclosed on one fixed grid.
These computations belong to the header preparation row.
Their validity still depends on the supplied tracking observation invariant; a
central-only inspection never silently renews that separate certificate.

\section{Observing leverage changes within a clean attempt}
\label{sec:ram27-observer}

The observer discovers stale leverage references without scanning the dense
graph.
Random source fields capture actual coefficient changes; a filter frozen before
the mask separates vertex-clock work from a small residual handled by random
marks.
A lifetime credit identity pays delayed reads, and packed arithmetic removes the
confidence-lane factor from dense work.
\Cref{thm:ram27-clean-observer} gives the attempt bound;
\Cref{prop:ram27-contract-closure} verifies its inputs.

Put $m=\Theta(n^2)$,
$r=\Theta(n)$, $s=r/m$, and $p=512$.
All graph matrices below act on the quotient by component constants; where a
full-space norm is needed, it is stated explicitly.
Parallel original-label multiplicity is bounded by a fixed constant.
The ordinary graph $\HH$ uses the actual coefficients $\vvr_e^2$: fixed-depth,
word-sized rationals, equal to $\zz_e^2/\ww_e^2$ in the flow application.
Their denominators may differ.

\paragraph{State, direction, and coefficient-change inputs.}
At each valid frozen pre-mask state, write
\[
 \HH=\BB^T\diag(\vvr^2)\BB,\qquad
 \UU=\diag(\vvr)\BB\HH^{\dagger/2},\qquad
 \PPi=\UU\UU^T,\qquad \ttau_e=\PPi_{ee},\qquad S=\PPi\circ\PPi.
\]
Assume the frozen state and direction satisfy
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ram27-state}
\begin{gathered}
 \llambda_e\asymp s+\ttau_e,\quad \llambda_e\ge s,\quad
 \ww_e\asymp\llambda_e,\quad \sum_e\llambda_e=O(r),\\
 \sum_e\ddelta_e^{-p}=O(m),\quad
 0<\ddeltabar_e\le1,\quad \ddeltabar_e\asymp\ddelta_e,\quad
 \vvpbar_e\asymp\llambda_e\asymp s+\ttau_e,\quad
 \norm{d}_{\ww}\le C\beta,\quad d_e\ge0 .
\end{gathered}
\end{equation}
Here $\beta=\beta_f$ is the physical-step scale.
The positive reference $\vvpbar_e$ estimates $s+\ttau_e$; it is distinct from the
logarithmic tracking error $\vvp_e$ of \Cref{sec:ram-local}.
In particular, every fixed inverse-margin moment through order $p$ is $O(m)$,
and $\ddelta_e\ge c_\star n^{-2/p}$.
The tracking tube supplies both comparisons throughout each reference epoch;
both are used to convert additive score error to logarithmic error.
The nonnegative vector $d$ controls the mean absolute coefficient changes in
\eqref{eq:ram27-actual-coefficient-change} below.
Assume every final stored candidate is frozen in a pending transaction before
source construction, with actual coefficient changes satisfying
\[
 \ssigma_e=\frac{\vvr_e'^2}{\vvr_e^2}-1,\qquad
 \ssigma_e=0\ \hbox{on every unselected label},\qquad
 \Ex[|\ssigma_e|\mid\mathcal F]\le C d_e.                 \tag{O1}\label{eq:ram27-actual-coefficient-change}
\]
\paragraph{Frozen state and commit order.}
Here $\mathcal F$ includes the filter fixed before the mask.
Keep the old $\HH$, original-product owner, and inverse until all source and
observer batches finish.
Then commit, update owners and $\llambda$ bins, and prepare final-graph fine
fields, without an old-owner copy.
Use the unconditioned physical law: the RAM algorithm has independent label
coins and aborts the attempt on a failed residual or normalizer test, without
resampling.
The observer expectation itself does not require independence between physical
labels.

The normalizer is an actual upward common-grid enclosure satisfying
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ram27-gamma}
 C_\Gamma\sum_e\llambda_e|\ssigma_e|
 \le \Gamma\le 2C_\Gamma\sum_e\llambda_e|\ssigma_e|,
 \qquad \Gamma=0\ \Longleftrightarrow\ b=0 .
\end{equation}
Valid frames also satisfy the separately tested bound $\Gamma\le C_{\rm
cap}\beta\sqrt r$.
Correctness of the score certificate also assumes a first-failure event for the
physical update, outside which
\[
 \HH^{\dagger/2}\BB^T\diag(\vvr^2|\ssigma|)\BB\HH^{\dagger/2}
       \preceq q_{\rm op}\II,\quad q_{\rm op}<1/2,
\]
and each old certified margin remains comparable to the final margin through the
frame.
These correctness inputs do not condition
\eqref{eq:ram27-actual-coefficient-change} on future success.

\paragraph{The ordinary graph, base graph, and observer metric.}
The ordinary graph supplies source products, a maintained base graph supplies
proxy and residual proposals, and the observer metric prices fine reads.
Their coefficients are defined separately below.
The maintained base has coefficients $\mu_e\asymp \vvr_e^2/\llambda_e$.
Scale it once so that the actual metadata coefficient
\[
 \omega_e\asymp\frac{\vvr_e^2}{\vvpbar_e\ddeltabar_e^4},\qquad
 \MM=\BB^T\diag(\omega)\BB
\]
satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ram27-base}
 \mu_e\le\omega_e\le R_0\mu_e,\qquad
 R_0=\O(n^{8/p}),\qquad
 M\preceq C(R_0/s)\HH .
\end{equation}
The ordinary and base owners supply compact incidence arrays and, for every
frozen field $\zz$, nonnegative endpoint envelopes with
\[
 \begin{array}{c|c}
 \text{original-label target}&\text{total envelope mass}\\ \hline
 \vvr_e^2(A_ez)^2&A_L \zz^THz\\
 \mu_e(A_ez)^2&A_L \zz^TG_0\zz
 \end{array}
 \qquad
 G_0=\BB^T\diag(\mu)\BB,\quad A_L=n^{o(1)}.
                                                               \tag{O2}\label{eq:ram27-owner-envelopes}
\]
For the owner of \Cref{sec:ram27-owner}, \eqref{eq:ram27-owner-envelopes}
follows from \eqref{eq:ram27-owner-gate} and the worst-case bound on $\phi^{-1}$
in \Cref{lem:ram27-current-moments}.
Envelope construction is included in the sparse-preparation work $S_{\rm
sparse}$ below; evaluating one returned label costs $\O(1)$.
The maintained actual-$\llambda$ bins supply a nonnegative envelope of total mass
$O(r)$ for the target $\llambda_e$.
Keep the ordinary owner for source products: using the base metric alone could
lose a factor $1/s$.
Use the fixed symmetric maps and finite energy-error guarantees of
\Cref{sec:ram27-linear}.
Retain the source inverse's old-graph proxy $3\HH/4\preceq P_H\preceq5\HH/4$, on the
same spectral event, for the executable energy guard.

\paragraph{Numerical conventions.}
Apply the \hyperref[par:ram30-finite-arithmetic]{finite arithmetic convention}
and the declared inverse-polynomial nonzero lower bounds.
Fix state grids and coefficient ranges before source/packet grids, then source
output before the fixed-depth filter ladder.
On every reference outcome, local errors have total normalized energy and debt
charge at most one over the declared horizon.
Accumulated vectors, $\Gamma,\omega,\mu$ and proxy packets use common grids;
ordinary coefficients and change ratios need not.
Let $\delta_{\min},\lambda_{\min}>0$ be the deterministic lower bounds on
permitted margins and $\llambda_e$, fixed by the parameter hierarchy and
$\llambda_e\ge s$, before score reads.

\begin{theorem}
\label{thm:ram27-clean-observer}
Under the state, update, owner, inverse, and numerical hypotheses above, start a
mathematical attempt at a paid clean checkpoint and stop it before beginning any
frame with an invalid premise.
Let $J_t$ indicate that frame $t$ is begun.
Suppose its predictable budgets and selected-read charges obey
\[
 \sum_tJ_t B_t\le \O(m),
 \qquad B_t=\beta_t m/\sqrt r,\qquad
 \Ex\sum_tJ_t\sum_{e\ {\rm selected}}\bar\delta_{e,t}^{-3}
       \le \O(m).
                                                               \tag{O3}\label{eq:ram27-observer-input-budgets}
\]
Assume at most $T=n^{3/4+3/p+o(1)}$ frames and aggregate sparse-preparation
work $S_{\rm sparse}=o(m)\O(1)$; a fixed polynomial gap below $n^2$ with
arbitrary fixed powers of $\log n$ is sufficient.

There is a finite word-RAM source and observer implementation with expected work
\[
\begin{aligned}
 &O\!\Bigl(
     (\log\log n)^2\Ex\sum_tJ_t\sum_{e\ {\rm selected}}
                                  \bar\delta_{e,t}^{-3}
     +\O(1)\Ex\sum_tJ_t B_t\\
 &\qquad{}+\log\log n\,\Ex M_{\rm pkt}+nT\log n+S_{\rm sparse}
     +\O(m)\Bigr)\\
 &\qquad= O\!\left(\O(m)\right).
\end{aligned}                                                  \tag{O4}\label{eq:ram27-observer-attempt-work}
\]
Here $M_{\rm pkt}$ counts original selected endpoint packets, without a factor
for confidence replicas; it is at most twice the selected-label count.
The last dense term pays the final clean refresh, including when the endpoint is
subsequently rejected.
The source/capture and numerical implementation failure probability can be made
$n^{-c}$ for any fixed $c_\star$, in addition to the explicitly supplied primitive
failure probabilities.

Positive-account read payments use a lifetime ledger.
No bound on a frame's payments in terms of that frame's new credit is asserted.
The theorem supplies expected work inside one stopped attempt; phase success,
optimization progress, the selected-rate bound
\eqref{eq:ram27-observer-input-budgets}, and the sparse owner/inverse work
remain separate obligations.
\end{theorem}

\subsection{From selected changes to source fields}

Construct a stored field $Y$ with two guarantees.
Its raw second moment \eqref{eq:ram27-raw} will bound work after averaging over
the physical mask.
Its error relative to the exact field with the same source,
\eqref{eq:ram27-same-source}, will transfer the source's signal to $Y$ and prove
the per-label capture bound \eqref{eq:ram27-capture}.

\paragraph{Conserved source coefficients.}
Set
\[
 \Delta=\BB^T\diag(\vvr^2|\ssigma|)\BB,\qquad \zeta=(n\log n)^{-1}.
\]
For label $e$, the positive certificate $(S|\ssigma|)_e=\vvr_e^2A_eH^\dagger\Delta
\HH^\dagger \BB_e^T$ controls the inverse-induced score change by the inverse-order
argument below.
We estimate it without scanning all edges.
If $b=0$, return zero fields and no clock increments.
Otherwise traverse the selected list and obtain $\Gamma$ from the
\hyperref[par:ram30-source-normalizer]{common-grid normalizer construction},
using the exact old and final rational coefficients.
Zero terms stay zero.
For a fixed sufficiently large constant $A_{\rm src}$ and $b\ne0$, prepare short
dyadics
\[
 (1-u)\vvr_e\sqrt{|\ssigma_e|/\Gamma}\le\widehat \cc_e
          \le \vvr_e\sqrt{|\ssigma_e|/\Gamma},\qquad u=\log^{-A_{\rm src}} n\le1/4,
 \qquad \ff=\BB^T\diag(\widehat c_\star)\xi .
\]
These dyadics define the mathematical source; the covariance sandwich, not an
additive $\zeta$-reserve, controls their error.
Compare squares with $\vvr_e^2|\ssigma_e|/\Gamma$, without storing an exact $\vvr_e$.
Binary search over $O(\log n)$ exponents and an $\O(1)$-bit mantissa costs $\O(1)$
per selected label.
Give each nonzero selected coefficient an independent uniform sign $\xi_e$ and
use opposite incidence signs at its endpoints.
Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ram27-source-cov}
 (1-u)^2\Delta/\Gamma\preceq\Ex ff^T\preceq\Delta/\Gamma,
 \qquad \Ex\norm{\ff}_{H^\dagger}^2
       \le \frac{\sum_e\ttau_e|\ssigma_e|}{\Gamma}\le1,
\end{equation}
after fixing $C_\Gamma$.

\paragraph{Sampling an original-graph product.}
The source estimates hold for any valid fixed symmetric map
\[
 \tfrac3{10}\HH^\dagger\preceq T_H\preceq\tfrac23\HH^\dagger.
\]
It may depend on the actual $b$, but is fixed before source signs and product
tapes; all estimates are uniform in this choice.
The joint implementation uses the retained old inverse, already fixed before the
mask.
For an actual stored iterate $x$, encode
\[
 \vvr_e^2(A_ex)^2/\zeta+C \log n\llambda_e
 \le \rho_e\le
 2\bigl(\vvr_e^2(A_ex)^2/\zeta+C \log n\llambda_e\bigr).
\]
Choose a fixed public count $B_{\rm prod}$ dominating all permitted target and
envelope masses, and use that many independent categorical/null positions.
A position returns $e$ with probability $\rho_e/B_{\rm prod}$, with packet
\[
        \frac{\vvr_e^2(A_ex)}{\rho_e}\BB_e^T .
\]
Use the envelope thinning and exact skipping of the
\hyperref[par:ram30-categorical]{public-count kernel}.

\paragraph{Product guards and the analytical continuation.}
Before reading the product tape, test $x^TP_{\HH}\xx\le C_{\rm en}\log n$, coordinate
ranges, and prepared envelope mass at most $C_{\rm env}n\log^2 nA_L$, including
actual-$\llambda$ bins.
Use an outward common-grid quadratic-form enclosure of absolute error at most
one with a fixed threshold reserve; header masses give the other test without an
original-edge scan.
A failed guard aborts execution.
Its analytical continuation uses the exact product $\HH\xx$, then rounds
$\xx_i+T_H(\ff-\HH\xx_i)$ on the same iterate grid with the same componentwise zero-sum
closure; put this defect in $\nu_i$.
Write $\widehat{\HH\xx}$ for the unrounded packet sum at the stored input when
guards pass, and for $\HH\xx$ otherwise.
For every valid private representation,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ram27-product}
 \Ex[\widehat{\HH\xx}\mid x]=\HH\xx,\qquad
 \Cov(\widehat{\HH\xx}\mid x)\preceq\zeta \HH .
\end{equation}
Indeed $\sum_e \vvr_e^4(A_ex)^2\BB_e^TA_e/\rho_e\preceq\zeta \HH$, and subtracting
position means decreases covariance.
Occupancy counts need not be independent.
Store rounded packets; their possibly biased deviation from this ideal sum
belongs to $\nu_i$ in \eqref{eq:ram27-source-error-recurrence}.

\paragraph{The stored recurrence and its two source estimates.}
Start $\xx_0=0$ and use $j_R=C_R\log n$ iterations
\[
 x_{i+1}=\xx_i+T_H(\ff-\widehat{\HH\xx_i}),
\]
including the prescribed finite evaluation and common-grid errors.
Put $C_H=\HH^{1/2}T_H\HH^{1/2}$, $R=\II-C_H$, and $e_i=\HH^{1/2}(\xx_i-\HH^\dagger \ff)$.
With $\eta_i=\HH^{\dagger/2}(\widehat{\HH\xx_i}-\HH\xx_i)$, the normalized error has the
exact form
\[
 e_{i+1}=Re_i-C_H\eta_i+\nu_i,\qquad
 \Ex[\eta_i\mid\mathcal F_i,\ff]=0,\qquad
 \Ex[\eta_i\eta_i^T\mid\mathcal F_i,\ff]\preceq\zeta \II.
                                                               \tag{O5}\label{eq:ram27-source-error-recurrence}
\]
Evaluate kernels at the stored iterate.
Round each packet once to a common grid and add opposite endpoint integers; the
public position count bounds total error on every outcome.
Choose $\max_i\norm{\nu_i}$ so its geometric convolution has squared norm
$o(\zeta)$.
All iterations share one absolute iterate grid, closing the zero sum in each
component after coordinatewise rounding.

The martingale cross terms in \eqref{eq:ram27-source-error-recurrence} vanish
conditionally on $\ff$.
Since $R$ and $C_H$ commute,
\[
 \sum_{j\ge0}R^jC_H^2R^j
       =C_H(2\II-C_H)^{-1}\preceq \II .
\]
The normalized source covariance has trace at most one by
\eqref{eq:ram27-source-cov}; no commutation with $R,C_H$ is needed.
Choose $C_R$ so the initial-bias second moment is at most $\zeta \II$.
The final stored field $Y=x_{j_R}$ then satisfies, uniformly in $b,T_H$,
\begin{align}
 \Ex[\Gamma YY^T\mid\ssigma,\mathcal F]
   &\preceq C \HH^\dagger\Delta \HH^\dagger
              +C\Gamma\zeta \HH^\dagger ,
       \label{eq:ram27-raw}\\
 \Ex[(Y-\HH^\dagger \ff)(Y-\HH^\dagger \ff)^T
                                      \mid\ssigma,\mathcal F]
   &\preceq C\zeta \HH^\dagger . \label{eq:ram27-same-source}
\end{align}
The numerical errors may be biased and correlated with $\ff$; their deterministic
norm reserve and $(x+\yy)(x+\yy)^T\preceq2xx^T+2yy^T$ suffice.

\paragraph{Capturing each label's change.}
For $Z=a_eA_eH^\dagger \ff$, the Rademacher fourth moment is at most $3(\Ex
Z^2)^2$, and $\Ex Z^2\asymp (S|\ssigma|)_e/\Gamma$.
Paley--Zygmund gives a fixed lower probability for $Z^2\ge\Ex Z^2/2$.
Equation \eqref{eq:ram27-same-source} and Markov's inequality preserve a fixed
fraction of that probability whenever the signal is larger than a sufficiently
large multiple of $\zeta\llambda_e$.
Below that scale, the background term in \eqref{eq:ram27-capture} already bounds
$(S|\ssigma|)_e$.
For $J=C_J\log n$ fresh replicas, Chernoff's bound for the capture indicators and an
all-label union bound therefore give
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ram27-capture}
 (S|\ssigma|)_e\le C\Gamma\left[
       \frac{\vvr_e^2}{J}\sum_{j\le J}(\BB_eY_j)^2+
                                      \zeta\llambda_e\right]
 \quad\hbox{simultaneously for every original label}.
\end{equation}
Replicas are independent conditional on the frozen source prestate, including
$b,\Gamma$ and the shared $T_H$.
Each uses fresh source signs and product tapes.

\paragraph{Why the executable guards pass.}
Write $\HH^{\dagger/2}\ff=A_f\xxrand$, so $\tr(A_fA_f^T)\le1$.
For a proof-only standard Gaussian $g$, its exponential integral and the
Rademacher exponential-moment inequality give
\[
 \Ex_{\xxrand} e^{\norm{A_f\xxrand}^2/4}
 =\Ex_g\Ex_{\xxrand} e^{g^TA_f\xxrand/\sqrt2}
 \le\Ex_g e^{\norm{A_f^Tg}^2/4}\le e^{1/2}.
\]
Thus the source energy is $O(\log n)$ at polynomial confidence.
For one product packet, putting $\zz=\vvr_e|A_ex|$, its normalized norm is
$\zz\sqrt{\ttau_e}/\rho_e\le C\sqrt{\zeta/\log n}$: use the two terms in $\rho_e$,
AM--GM, and $\ttau_e\le C\llambda_e$.
In each vector of a proof-only orthonormal basis, the complete centered product
error has variance at most $\zeta$, while each centered position is bounded by
$C\sqrt{\zeta/\log n}$.
Scalar Bernstein at threshold
\[
 C_{\rm conf}\bigl(\sqrt{\zeta \log n}
             +\log n\sqrt{\zeta/\log n}\bigr)
                    =C'_{\rm conf}/\sqrt n
\]
has any prescribed inverse-polynomial failure probability after fixing the
constant.
A union over at most $n$ coordinates then bounds the complete error in
$\HH^\dagger$-norm by $O(1)$.
The basis is analytical and is never computed.
The contraction in \eqref{eq:ram27-source-error-recurrence} therefore gives
every iterate $\HH$-energy $O(\log n)$.
Its target mass is
\[
 \sum_e\rho_e\le C\bigl(\norm{x}_H^2/\zeta+\log n\sum_e\llambda_e\bigr)
                         =O(n\log^2 n).
\]
The owner and actual-$\llambda$ bins add $n^{o(1)}$.
On the proxy's spectral event this also bounds $x^TP_{\HH}\xx=O(\log n)$ and the prepared
envelope, so fixed reserves make the guards pass.
Scalar count bounds and the declared replica/stage counts pay the
sparse-preparation work.
Every reference outcome has polynomial coordinates: passing products have at
most $B_{\rm prod}$ polynomial-size packets; failing products use deterministic
contraction.
Choose grids from this bound.

\paragraph{Expected product work.}
The preceding $O(\log n)$ energy bound controls guard failures.
The mean energy on the full reference law gives a sharper work estimate.
At every reference stage,
\[
 \HH^{1/2}\xx_i=(\II-R^i)\HH^{\dagger/2}\ff
        -\sum_{j<i}R^{i-1-j}C_H\eta_j
        +\sum_{j<i}R^{i-1-j}\nu_j .
\]
Since $0\preceq R\preceq \II$, the source term has expected squared norm at most
one.
The martingale term has covariance at most $\zeta \II$, hence trace at most
$r\zeta=O(1/\log n)$, and the numerical term is uniformly small.
Therefore $\Ex\norm{\xx_i}_H^2=O(1)$ on the full reference law and
$\Ex\sum_e\rho_e=O(n\log n)$.
Before abort, executed steps agree with this reference; multiplying their
nonnegative charges by reach indicators only decreases expectation.
Guards cost $O(\nnz P_H+n)$ beyond shared header preparation, in the sparse row.
The dense continuation has no executed cost.

\subsection{Averaging the source moment before filtering}

Now average the raw moment over the mask and source fields, keeping the metric
and filter fixed.
The scalar $B$ below is the owner's per-frame budget $B_*$; the fields need not
have mean zero.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:ram27-averaged}
