Under \eqref{eq:ram27-state}, \eqref{eq:ram27-actual-coefficient-change}, and
\eqref{eq:ram27-raw}, let the filter and $M$ be fixed before the physical mask.
For analytical notation only, put $h=\sqrt\Gamma Y$.
Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ram27-averaged-bounds}
 \Ex\Gamma\le C\beta\sqrt r,\qquad
 \Ex h^T\MM h\le C B,\qquad
 \left\norm{\MM^{1/2}\Ex[hh^T\mid\mathcal F]\MM^{1/2}\right}_F
        \le C\beta R_0/s,\qquad B=\beta m/\sqrt r .
\end{equation}
No covariance bound or independence hypothesis on $b$ is needed.
\end{lemma} \begin{proof} Let $\cc_e=\Ex[|\ssigma_e|\mid\mathcal F]$, so $0\le \cc_e\le
Cd_e$.
The normalizer bound and weighted Cauchy give
\[
 \Ex\Gamma\le C\sum_e\llambda_ed_e
 \le C\norm{d}_{\ww}\left(\sum_e\llambda_e^2/\ww_e\right)^{1/2}
 \le C\beta\sqrt r .
\]
The projection identity implies $S\mathbf1=\ttau$, and $S$ is symmetric with
nonnegative entries.
Thus
\[
 \left\norm{\HH^{\dagger/2}\Ex\Delta \HH^{\dagger/2}\right}_F^2
   =\cc^TSc\le\sum_e\ttau_ec_e^2\le C\beta^2 .             \tag{O6}\label{eq:ram27-averaged-change-frobenius}
\]
The middle inequality is also the positivity of the weighted Laplacian
$\diag(\ttau)-S$.
Apply \eqref{eq:ram27-raw} and average over the mask before taking the Frobenius
norm.
No bound on $\Ex\norm{\HH^{\dagger/2}\Delta \HH^{\dagger/2}}_F$ is needed.
Writing $D=\MM^{1/2}\HH^{\dagger/2}$, we have $\norm{D}_{\rm op}^2\le CR_0/s$.
Equation \eqref{eq:ram27-averaged-change-frobenius} bounds the Frobenius norm of
the signal by $C\beta R_0/s$.
The background contributes at most
\[
 C\,\Ex\Gamma\,\zeta\sqrt r\,R_0/s
        \le C\beta R_0/s,
\]
because $r\zeta=O(1/\log n)$.

For the trace bound set $v_e=\ddeltabar_e^{-4}/\vvpbar_e$.
The signal is at most $C d^TSv$.
Weighted Cauchy and $(Sv)_f^2\le\ttau_f\sum_eS_{fe}v_e^2$ give
\[
 d^TSv\le\norm{d}_{\ww}
       \left(\sum_f(Sv)_f^2/w_f\right)^{1/2},
 \qquad
 \sum_f(Sv)_f^2/w_f
   \le C\sum_e\ttau_ev_e^2
   \le C\sum_e\ddelta_e^{-8}/\llambda_e
   \le Cm/s .
\]
Hence the signal is $CB$.
The background trace is at most
\[
 C\,\Ex\Gamma\,\zeta\,\tr(\MM\HH^\dagger)
 \le C\beta\sqrt r\,\zeta\sum_e\ddelta_e^{-4}
 \le CB/\log n .
\]
The trace calculation avoids the extra $R_0$ factor in the coarse comparison
$M\preceq C(R_0/s)\HH$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{A filter built from the maintained base}

Freeze the following proxies and filter before the mask.
It splits $Y=v_Y+r_Y$: residual-degree energy pays vertex clocks for $v_Y$, and
small expected $M$-energy pays marks for $r_Y$.

\paragraph{Choosing and accessing the top-list set.}
Choose $d_0= n^{64/p}\log^{D_0} n $.
Enumerate the union $C$ of the endpoint top-$d_0$ base-weight lists, using
dyadic bins; let $R_E=E\setminus C$.
This edge set is distinct from the owner's expander pieces.
Write $L_C$ for its weighted Laplacian and $D_{R_E},A_{R_E}$ for residual degree
and adjacency (not incidence).
Put
\[
 \QQ_\omega=L_C(\omega)+D_{R_E}(\omega),\qquad
 \QQ_\mu=L_C(\mu)+D_{R_E}(\mu).
\]
Thus $M=\QQ_\omega-A_{R_E}(\omega)$; replacing residual edges by their endpoint diagonals
permits the trace bound below.
Maintain full base degrees at genuine coefficient changes.
Subtract the enumerated top-list incidences to obtain $D_{R_E}(\mu)$ in
$O(nd_0)$ work, then add the explicit $L_C(\mu)$ to form $\QQ_\mu$.
Handle sparse components without residual edges directly; on the others $\QQ_\omega,\QQ_\mu$
are positive definite.

At each original vertex, maintain the integer index of
\Cref{lem:ram-deadline-index}, keyed by the negative dyadic exponent of $\mu_e$
and the original-label identifier.
Shift the exponent by its fixed range bound to obtain a nonnegative
polynomial-universe key.
Initial insertion costs $\O(m)$.
Each genuine base-bucket change updates two keys at $\O(1)$ cost each; private
owner migrations do not change these keys.

Repeatedly read and temporarily delete the minimum key until $d_0$ entries or
exhaustion, then reinsert those entries.
Only this auxiliary index changes; owners, coefficients, physical state and
output laws stay fixed.
These operations cost $\O(nd_0+n)$, without successor queries or dense scans.
Generation marks and direct incidence handles deduplicate the union and
reconcile roles within the same sparse query row.
Identifier ties make the set canonical at the frozen base.

\paragraph{The top-list trace bound.}
If $0<c_\star\le1$, selected weights at a vertex are at least $c_\star$ times every residual
weight there, and parallel-label multiplicity is at most $b_0$, then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ram27-top}
 \tr(\QQ_\omega^{-1}A_{R_E}\QQ_\omega^{-1}A_{R_E})
                       \le 32b_0 n/(c_\star d_0).
\end{equation}
To prove it, let $t_v$ be the largest residual weight at $v$, or zero if no
residual edge meets $v$.
Give a chosen edge $uv$ auxiliary weight
\[
 \tfrac{c_\star}{2}\bigl(t_u\mathbf1_{\{u\ {\rm chooses}\ uv\}}
                  +t_v\mathbf1_{\{v\ {\rm chooses}\ uv\}}\bigr).
\]
Let $\HH'$ be the graph of these auxiliary selected edges and $D$ the degree
matrix of $\HH'+R_E$.
Orient a selected edge from a choosing endpoint of largest $t$, and orient each
residual edge arbitrarily.
In $\HH'+R_E$, each oriented edge weight divided by its tail degree is at most
$2/(cd_0)$.
Its normalized adjacency therefore has squared Frobenius norm at most
$4b_0n/(cd_0)$.
Writing $B_0=D^{-1/2}A_{H'}D^{-1/2}$ and $C_0=D^{-1/2}A_{R_E}D^{-1/2}$,
nonnegative entries give the same bound on $\norm{B_0}_F^2+\norm{C_0}_F^2$.
The matrix
\[
 Z=(\II-B_0)^{-1/2}C_0(\II-B_0)^{-1/2}
\]
is a contraction since $L_{H'}+D_{R_E}\pm A_{R_E}\succeq0$.
The subspace spanned by eigenvectors of $B_0$ with eigenvalues above $1/2$ has
dimension at most $4\norm{B_0}_F^2$.
Since $Z$ is a contraction, the blocks meeting this subspace have total squared
Frobenius norm at most twice its dimension.
The block on its orthogonal complement has squared Frobenius norm at most
$4\norm{C_0}_F^2$, proving the auxiliary bound.
Finally $\QQ_\omega\succeq L_{H'}+D_{R_E}$.
For symmetric $\BB$, $\tr(YAYA)$ is increasing in PSD $Y$, since its directional
derivative is $2\tr(\Delta AYA)\ge0$.
Inverse order transfers the bound to $\QQ_\omega$.
Regularization handles isolated auxiliary rows annihilated by $A_{R_E}$.
Clamp any supplied comparison constant to $\min\{c,1\}$, since a selected
auxiliary edge has tail ratio at most $2/d_0$.
Here $c_\star=\min\{1,c_\star'/R_0\}$ gives $O(R_0n/d_0)$.

\paragraph{Sparse proxy construction.}
Prepare proxies $P_M=(1\pm\epsilon)M$ and $\widehat{\QQ}_\omega=(1\pm\epsilon)\QQ_\omega$, with
$\epsilon=c_\star d_0^{-1/32}$, before the physical mask.
The filter multiplies by $P_M$ and approximately inverts $\widehat{\QQ}_\omega$.
Both target actual $\omega$; the $\mu$-weighted base supplies sampling access.
Base leverage upper scores $\mathcal \BB_e$, distinct from incidence rows $\BB_e$,
select residual edges for $P_M$; coordinate scores $z_v\asymp(\QQ_\mu^{-1})_{vv}$
select residual endpoint atoms for $\widehat{\QQ}_\omega$.
Encode each set before its sampling tape.

The base scores come from the immediately preceding frame's cached proxy.
The same construction refreshes this cache to approximate the current $G_0$ for
the next frame.
\Cref{lem:ram27-upper-scores} supplies the first cache and its finite
categorical construction.
Discard or restore aborted caches consistently with the clean checkpoint.

\paragraph{Base-score fields from the previous cache.}
Write the immediately preceding base proxy as the stored sparse sum
\[
 P_{\rm old}=\sum_{\alpha}c_\alpha b_\alpha b_\alpha^T .
\]
Here $b_\alpha$ is the signed incidence vector of copy $\alpha$.
The supplied one-frame coefficient ratio makes this proxy constant-comparable to
the current $G_0$.
Freeze this proxy and a fixed symmetric inverse map $T_{\rm old}$ before the
field signs.
For a fixed small constant $\eta_s>0$, choose short dyadics
\[
 (1-\eta_s)\sqrt{c_\alpha}\le d_\alpha\le\sqrt{c_\alpha},
 \qquad
 \widetilde P_{\rm old}
       =\sum_\alpha d_\alpha^2b_\alpha b_\alpha^T .
\]
The coefficients $d_\alpha$ define the source exactly.
With fresh independent signs on the sparse atoms, define the analytical lanes
\[
 \xxrand_j=\sum_\alpha d_\alpha \sigma_{\alpha j}b_\alpha,
 \qquad h_j=T_{\rm old}\xxrand_j,
 \qquad J_0=2^{ \log_2(C_J\log n) }.
\]
Conditional on the frozen proxy and inverse,
\[
 \Ex h_jh_j^T
       =T_{\rm old}\widetilde P_{\rm old}T_{\rm old}
       \asymp G_0^\dagger.
\]
Indeed $\widetilde P_{\rm old}\asymp P_{\rm old}$, and if $C_{\rm old}=P_{\rm
old}^{1/2}T_{\rm old}P_{\rm old}^{1/2}$ satisfies $\norm{C_{\rm old}-\II}_{\rm
op}\le\eta_T<1$, then
\[
 (1-\eta_T)^2P_{\rm old}^\dagger
 \preceq T_{\rm old}P_{\rm old}T_{\rm old}
 \preceq(1+\eta_T)^2P_{\rm old}^\dagger .
\]

\paragraph{Stored base fields and canonical upper scores.}
Before the proxy-position tapes, store the finite lanes $\widehat h_j$ on a
predetermined dyadic grid.
For a predetermined dyadic score floor $\gamma>0$, choose inverse accuracy and
the grid so that, on the valid-proxy/inverse reference,
\[
 \left|
 \mu_e(\BB_e\widehat h_j)^2-\mu_e(A_eh_j)^2
 \right|\le\gamma
 \quad\hbox{for every original label, lane, and reference outcome}.
\tag{CF1}\label{eq:ram27-base-field-rounding}
\]
Here $\gamma$ is unrelated to the physical step ratio $\beta_s/\beta_f$.
Public polynomial bounds give $|A_eh_j|\le U$ and $\mu_e\le\mu_{\max}$.
A uniform coordinate error $\varepsilon_{\rm abs}$ changes the squared score by
at most $4\mu_{\max}\varepsilon_{\rm abs}U+ 4\mu_{\max}\varepsilon_{\rm abs}^2$.
Choose the inverse accuracy and the cache and scratch grids of
\Cref{sec:ram27-parameter-hierarchy} to make this at most $\gamma$.
Other factorization tapes obey the \hyperref[par:ram30-finite-arithmetic]{finite
arithmetic guards} or abort.

Let $\mathrm{up}_\gamma(x)=\gamma x/\gamma $.
Define the actual stored-label upper-score function to be
\[
 \mathcal \BB_e
 =C_A\mathrm{up}_\gamma
 \left(\frac{\mu_e}{J_0}
                 \sum_{j\le J_0}(\BB_e\widehat h_j)^2+\gamma\right),
 \tag{CF2}\label{eq:ram27-canonical-base-score}
\]
where $C_A$ is one sufficiently large fixed integer.
Frozen finite fields and stored $\mu_e$ fix this value independently of
proposals and reads.
The $\gamma$ term covers \eqref{eq:ram27-base-field-rounding}, even when the
stored squared distance is zero, and
\[
 \mathcal \BB_e\le C_A\left[
       \frac{\mu_e}{J_0}\sum_j(\BB_e\widehat h_j)^2+2\gamma\right].
 \tag{CF3}\label{eq:ram27-base-score-envelope}
\]
Choose $m\gamma\le1$.
Scalar Bernstein for averages of squared Rademacher linear forms
\cite[Theorem~6.2, scalar case]{T12:journal}, followed by the all-label union
bound, gives simultaneously
\[
 \ttau_e(G_0)\le\mathcal \BB_e,\qquad
                       \sum_e\mathcal \BB_e=O(n).
\]

\paragraph{Score envelopes and cache refresh.}
Mix the $J_0$ base-owner envelopes with weights $C_A/J_0$, and add a
uniform-label envelope of rate $2C_A\gamma$.
By \eqref{eq:ram27-base-score-envelope}, this is an upper envelope for the
actual $\mathcal \BB_e$.
Its mass is bounded by
\[
 \frac{C_AA_L}{J_0}\sum_j\norm{\widehat h_j}_{G_0}^2+2C_Am\gamma .
 \tag{CF4}\label{eq:ram27-base-envelope-mass}
\]
Prepared header masses give the normalizer without a dense score sum or
original-graph quadratic form.
Covariance and \eqref{eq:ram27-base-field-rounding} give unconditional
expectation $O(nA_L)$ for \eqref{eq:ram27-base-envelope-mass}, before any
field-work cap.
Thin each proposal to \eqref{eq:ram27-canonical-base-score} and use that actual
rate also in the packet denominator.
The refresh and residual rates are
\[
 \rho_e^{\rm cache}=C\log n\mathcal \BB_e,\qquad
 \rho_e^M=CR_0\epsilon^{-2}\log n\mathcal \BB_e
                   \mathbf1_{\{e\in R_E\}}.
\]
Retain the top-list edges $C$ exactly in $P_M$ and sample residual atoms at rate
$\rho_e^M$.
At rate $\rho_e^{\rm cache}$, use fresh positions to sample a constant-accuracy
proxy of the entire current $G_0$, with the prescribed positive common-grid
packet rounding.
Store it as the next frame's base cache.
This refresh occurs before the physical mask and adds $n^{1+o(1)}$ sparse atoms
and work after the shared $n(R+\log n)n^{o(1)}$ owner-header preparation, where
$R\asymp n^{1/16}$ is the counter radix.
Use the same \hyperref[par:ram30-categorical]{public-count kernel} with fresh
position tapes after fixing targets, without redrawing fields.
Each cache samples current $G_0$, so approximation factors do not multiply
across frames.

\paragraph{Diagonal scores for the second proxy.}
For the coordinate scores of $\QQ_\mu$, use its explicit top-list edge atoms and the
diagonal atoms of $D_{R_E}(\mu)$, with the latter regarded as edges to ground.
The same atom-sign construction has analytical covariance comparable to
$\QQ_\mu^{-1}$.
Compute all $nJ_0$ finite coordinates in the sparse row, and define $z_v$ by the
counterpart of \eqref{eq:ram27-canonical-base-score}, with no edge coefficient.
Choose its numerical mesh $\gamma_Q$ so that
\[
 \gamma_Q\sum_v(D_{R_E}(\mu))_{vv}\le1,\qquad
 \gamma_Q\le c_\star/\lambda_{\max}(\QQ_\mu).
\]
Fix these meshes from public polynomial bounds.
Since $(\QQ_\mu^{-1})_{vv}\ge1/\lambda_{\max}(\QQ_\mu)$, the resulting scores satisfy
the stated two-sided coordinate comparison and
\[
 \sum_vz_v(D_{R_E}(\mu))_{vv}
 \le C\tr(D_{R_E}(\mu)\QQ_\mu^{-1})+C
 \le Cn .
\]
Sample residual endpoint atoms at rates $C R_0\epsilon^{-2}\log n\mu_ez_v$.
Endpoint upper weights within a dyadic bucket differ by at most a factor two.
Excluded top-list positions split each compact array into gaps; sampling the
gaps avoids rejection from an array almost entirely in $C$.
Each proposal thins to its exact original-label target.
Endpoint packets use the literal stored $z_v$ in both their position
probabilities and their inverse-rate weights.

The $O(J_0)=O(\log n)$ point cost of \eqref{eq:ram27-canonical-base-score} and the
$J_0$ field/header preparations occur only in sparse proxy rows, where fixed
powers of $\log n$ are allowed.
Dense selected events and fine reads priced by \eqref{eq:ram27-packed-read-work}
never invoke this lane scan.

For either observer proxy, normalize by its target matrix.
A retained categorical-position PSD atom has norm at most $R_{\rm
pkt}=C\epsilon^2/\log n$; its centered version has norm at most $2R_{\rm pkt}$, and
the sum of its second moments is at most $R_{\rm pkt}\II$.
Independent matrix Bernstein \cite[Theorem~1.4]{T12:journal}, applied to both
signs, gives the desired two-sided error at polynomial confidence.
This use allows the exact retained top-list edges to have arbitrary atom size:
only random residual atoms enter the variance bound.

\paragraph{The filter's operator and Frobenius bounds.}
Let $T_Q$ be a fixed symmetric inverse of $\widehat{\QQ}_\omega$ refined to accuracy
$O(\epsilon)$, and set $F=\II-T_QP_M$, $k_{\rm it}=16$.
For an operator on the Laplacian quotient, use $X_M=\MM^{1/2}XM^{\dagger/2}$ for
its $M$-normalized form.
Since $P_M\mathbf1=0$, $F$ preserves constants and induces this quotient
operator.
With $K=\II-\MM^{1/2}\QQ_\omega^{-1}\MM^{1/2}$ on the quotient,
\[
 \norm{K}_{\rm op}\le1,\qquad
 \norm{K}_F^2\le CR_0 n/d_0,\qquad
 F_M=K+E,\quad \norm{E}_{\rm op}\le C\epsilon .
\]
Expand $(K+E)^{k_{\rm it}}$ in the original factor order.
Every term except $E^k$ contains a $K$; bound one such factor in Frobenius norm
and all remaining factors in operator norm.
The remaining term has squared Frobenius norm at most
$n(C\epsilon)^{2k}=O(n/d_0)$.
Hence the conservative bounds
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ram27-filter}
 \norm{(F^{k_{\rm it}})_M}_{\rm op}\le C,\qquad
 \norm{(F^{k_{\rm it}})_M}_F^2\le CR_0^2 n/d_0
\end{equation}
hold without any commutation assumption.

\paragraph{The complement, including finite evaluation.}
Since $D_{R_E}\preceq \QQ_\omega$, $\norm{\QQ_\omega^{1/2}FQ^{-1/2}}_{\rm op}=O(1)$, and
$P_MQ^{-1}P_M\preceq CM$, the identity
\[
 \II-F^{k_{\rm it}}=\sum_{j<k}F^jT_QP_M
\]
gives $\norm{(\II-F^{k_{\rm it}})h}_Q^2\le Ch^T\MM h$.
For finite evaluation use a fixed $k$-layer grid ladder.
Fix a positive dyadic $\nu_{\rm num}\le c_{\rm num}d_0^{-1/4}$, also below the ordinary
numerical reserve, before choosing that ladder.
Here $c_{\rm num}>0$ is a sufficiently small fixed constant.
Since $d_0$ has a fixed polynomial range, this requires only $O(\log n)$ bits
and does not change the fixed number of layers.
At layer $j$, form $P_Mz$ exactly on the product grid; if it is zero, use the
exact no-op.
Otherwise its grid and the polynomial upper eigenvalue bound on $\QQ_\omega$ provide a
positive lower bound on $\norm{P_Mz}_{Q^{-1}}$.
A sufficiently finer predetermined next grid gives action error at most
$\nu\norm{P_Mz}_{Q^{-1}}$ in $\QQ_\omega$-norm.
Because $k$ is fixed, all grid exponents remain $O(\log n)$.
For the stored output $r_Y$ and literal subtraction $v_Y=Y-r_Y$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ram27-complement}
 v_Y^TD_{R_E}(\omega)v_Y\le C Y^TMY,\qquad
 \norm{r_Y-F^kY}_Q\le C\nu\norm{Y}_M .
\end{equation}
The numerical error is controlled in the full-space norm.

\paragraph{The expected residual energy.}
Apply \Cref{lem:ram27-averaged} with the fixed filter, and use
$\norm{X^TX}_F\le\norm{X}_{\rm op}\norm{X}_F$.
For analytical notation $r_h=\sqrt\Gamma r_Y$, Schatten Cauchy and
\eqref{eq:ram27-filter} give
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ram27-residual-energy}
 \Ex\norm{r_h}_M^2
 \le C(\beta R_0/s)R_0\sqrt{n/d_0}
                   +C\nu^2\Ex h^T\MM h
 \le C B R_0^2/\sqrt{d_0}.
\end{equation}
The numerical term uses $\Ex h^T\MM h=O(B)$, $\nu^2\le c_{\rm num}^2/\sqrt{d_0}$,
and $R_0\ge1$.
Implement the filter at $Y$ and multiply its squares by $\Gamma$; this avoids
rounding $\sqrt\Gamma Y$ or assuming homogeneity of finite evaluation.

\subsection{From fields to read requests and lifetime payments}

A public score epoch runs between consecutive public fine reads of one label.
Its price and threshold stay fixed:
\[
 \pi_e=\ddeltabar_e^{-3},\qquad
 \Theta_e=c_\star\,\vvpbar_e\ddeltabar_e/\vvr_e^2,\qquad
 \pi_e/\Theta_e\asymp\omega_e .
\]
The \emph{physical debt} below is in squared-field units.
Multiplying by epoch-fixed $\vvr_e^2$ and the capture constants bounds score
variation; multiplying by $\pi_e/\Theta_e$ gives read cost.
\paragraph{Positive accounts.} For replica $Y_j$, write $r_j=r_{Y_j}$ and
$v_j=Y_j-r_j$.
Labels in the top-list set $C$ use a direct account; residual labels use the
split
\[
 (\BB_eY_j)^2\le
       4\bigl(v_j(u)^2+v_j(v)^2\bigr)+2(A_er_j)^2 .
\]
Vertex complement clocks pay the first term and marks the second.
The direct account receives $\Gamma J^{-1}\sum_j(\BB_eY_j)^2$, each endpoint
complement account receives $4\Gamma J^{-1}\sum_jv_j(v)^2$, and the global
background clock increments by $\Gamma\zeta$, with the epoch-fixed conversion
$\llambda_e/\vvr_e^2$.
Thus its account receives $\Gamma\zeta\llambda_e/\vvr_e^2$, all in physical-debt
units.
A label in $C$ has direct and background accounts; a residual label has two
endpoint accounts and background.
Give each account ceiling $\vartheta_e=\Theta_e/8$.
The timers below release a read only after $\Theta_e/16$ positive debt, so fix
$c_a=1/16$.

\paragraph{Residual marks.}
For each replica, use the target
\[
 t_{e,j}=\frac{16\Gamma}{J\Theta_e}
             (A_er_j)^2\mathbf1_{\{e\in R_E\}} .
\]
Accumulated target one corresponds to residual physical debt $\Theta_e/8$, the
tolerance used below.
Take fresh public-count categorical/null batches with multiplier $\lambda_{\rm
mon}=C_{\rm conf}\log n$.
A raw proposal reads only that replica's two stored coordinates, so its point
price has no factor $J$.
The product no-hit probability is at most $\exp(-\lambda_{\rm
mon}\sum_jt_{e,j})$.
The public count is fixed using the all-outcome polynomial range, not a
successful future source or work event.

\paragraph{Buffering the first request.}
At a label's first fine-score request, stamp and append it once, remove its
positive-account timers, and deactivate those accounts until its single final
read starts a new epoch.
For selected labels, do this before any observer clock increment.
Later marks only test the stamp; role and owner moves preserve inactivity.
Central-only requests do not suspend accounts.
Only deadline metadata changes: frozen coefficients, fields, sampling lists and
old read prices remain until the old batches finish.
Each first request costs a fixed number of handle operations, independent of the
replica count.

\paragraph{The no-hit guarantee.}
Stop a label's no-hit process at its first buffered public fine-score request;
central-only requests and inspections leave it unchanged.
Conditional on the generated fields and the history before the fresh mark coins,
the no-request indicator times the exponential of accumulated predictable
residual intensity is a nonnegative supermartingale.
Stopping when the target reaches one bounds failure by $\exp(-c_\star\lambda_{\rm
mon})$, conditional on a valid epoch birth at zero, uniformly over adaptive
later fields.
This bound is conditional on the birth, not on each intermediate no-hit history.
Union over the predeclared polynomial number of adaptive births, stopping at
earlier primitive failures.
A clean refresh restarts epochs at zero; other fine-score requests kill the
process and improve the bound.
The proof-only target survives all role and gauge changes until a public read.

\paragraph{Protecting the reference tube.}
By \eqref{eq:ram27-capture} and the inverse-order consequence
\[
 -(1-q)^{-1}\HH^\dagger\Delta \HH^\dagger
 \preceq (\HH')^\dagger-\HH^\dagger
 \preceq (1-q)^{-1}\HH^\dagger\Delta \HH^\dagger ,
\]
the four debt terms protect the tube until the first request.
The displayed inverse bounds follow from $\HH-\Delta\preceq \HH'\preceq \HH+\Delta$.
Choose debt tolerances as follows.
Let $K_{\rm obs}$ bound the fixed capture and inverse-order constants, and let
$C_t,C_\delta\ge1$ be fixed comparison constants with $\vvpbar_e\le
C_t(s+\ttau_e)$ and $\ddeltabar_e\le C_\delta\ddelta_e$ up to the first potential
reference exit.
A label that has not been requested is unselected, so its $\vvr_e,\llambda_e,\bar
\vvp_e,\ddeltabar_e$ stay fixed throughout this epoch.
If no positive account reports, its total positive debt is below $3\Theta_e/8$.
If the residual no-hit event has not failed, its accumulated residual physical
debt $2\sum_{t,j}\Gamma_t(A_er_{t,j})^2/J$, restricted to its residual roles, is
below $\Theta_e/8$.
Consequently its total captured score variation is at most $K_{\rm
obs}\vvr_e^2\Theta_e/2$, and
\[
 \frac{|\log(s+\ttau_e)-\log(s+\ttau_{e,\rm birth})|}{\kappa}
 \le \frac{K_{\rm obs}C_tc}{2\kappa}\ddeltabar_e
 \le \frac{\ddeltabar_e}{1024pC_\delta}
\]
when $c_\star\le\kappa/(512pC_\delta K_{\rm obs}C_t)$.
Choose the fresh fine-read and tracking-cell rounding constants to give the same
$\ddeltabar_e/(1024pC_\delta)$ birth error in normalized tracking coordinates.
Their sum is at most $\ddelta_e/(512p)$, leaving a strict reserve in the required
$\ddelta_e/(256p)$ observation window.
All clock quantities are upper enclosures of their physical contributions; the
prescribed source, capture, and fine-read numerical reserves are included in
these constants.
The argument uses the comparisons only before their first possible violation.
The physical one-frame margin reserve protects the wait until the final read.

\paragraph{Transferring debt when a role changes.}
At a top-list role change, materialize each touched label's direct/complement
debt and due flags, then transfer them to one canonical account without
duplication.
Preserve the background baseline and conversion.
Queue a read paid by old debt exactly when the new $\Theta_e/8$ ceiling or
early-report rule applies.
In either case, preserve debt and reference metadata until the final public read
starts the next epoch.
Touch a fixed number of accounts for each of $O(nd_0)$ labels.
Constant-unit transfers are exact on the common debt grid; upward conversions
may instead add total weighted numerical credit at most one under the fixed
horizon and precision.
Never revalue debt at a new $\pi_e$.

\paragraph{The lifetime payment identity.}
Here $D_e$ denotes accumulated physical debt, distinct from the owner's density
deficit.
Cap its proof-only active credit at the price of one read:
\[
 C_e=\pi_e\min\{1,D_e/(c_a\Theta_e)\}.
\]
A first positive-account request releases $\pi_e$.
A first selected or residual-mark request discards any old credit, because its
read has another payment.
The label then stops accruing credit until its final read starts a new epoch
with zero debt and zero credit.
Role changes preserve $C_e$, including immediately due ones.
Writing $A_t$ for new credit and $R_+$ for all released positive-account prices,
exact bookkeeping gives, at every stopped prefix,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ram27-ledger}
 R_+\le C_{\rm initial}+\sum_t A_t,\qquad
 \Ex[A_t\mid\mathcal F_t]\le C B_t .
\end{equation}
For the mean, an added debt increases credit by at most $C\omega_e\,\Delta D_e$.
Summing the direct and complement increments gives
\[
 \frac{C\Gamma}{J}\sum_j
       \left(Y_j^TL_C(\omega)Y_j+
                         v_j^TD_{R_E}(\omega)v_j\right).
\]
Equations \eqref{eq:ram27-complement} and \eqref{eq:ram27-averaged-bounds} bound
its conditional expectation by $CB_t$.
The background contributes at most
\[
 C\,\Ex\Gamma\,\zeta
            \sum_e\llambda_e/(\vvpbar_e\ddeltabar_e^4)
                         \le CB_t/\log n .
\]
Suspension, clipping, and discarding credit can only decrease it.
The credits are analytical; no dense clock update is performed.

\paragraph{Paying for marks and their raw proposals.}
The expected residual-mark price, counting even duplicate marks, is
\[
 \Ex\sum_e\pi_e\,\#\{\text{marks of }e\}
       \le C \log n\,\Ex\norm{r_h}_M^2 .
\]
Generate marks from the full base envelope \eqref{eq:ram27-owner-envelopes},
testing residual membership during thinning.
Since $\pi/\Theta$ is comparable to the actual encoded $\omega$, fix
$K_\omega\ge\sup_e\pi_e/(\Theta_e\omega_e)$.
For replica $r_j$, this envelope has per-label mass $E_e\ge\mu_e(A_er_j)^2$ and
total mass at most $A_L r_j^TG_0r_j$.
Give it raw rate $(16\lambda_{\rm mon}\Gamma/J)K_\omega R_0E_e$ in the
public-count categorical construction, and thin each proposed label by
\[
 \frac{\mu_e(A_er_j)^2}{E_e}\,
 \frac{\omega_e}{R_0\mu_e}\,
 \frac1{\pi_e}\,
 \frac{\pi_e}{K_\omega\Theta_e\omega_e}\,
                 \mathbf1_{\{e\in R_E\}}.
\]
Each factor lies in $[0,1]$, and a zero envelope returns zero, giving exactly
rate $\lambda_{\rm mon}t_{e,j}$.
Comparability of encoded weights therefore affects domination and work only.
Enlarge the public count by $K_\omega$ before the run; its point correction is
fixed-depth rational arithmetic without a fine read.
Thus no spectral certificate is restricted to the adaptive residual.
The raw-proposal expectation is at most $C \log n R_0 A_L\,\Ex\norm{r_h}_M^2$.
Consequently the sum of new credit, marked-read price, and raw proposals has
expectation
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ram27-observer-work}
 C B_t\left(1+\frac{\log n A_LR_0^3}{\sqrt{d_0}}\right)=O(B_t).
\end{equation}
The ratio has polynomial factor $n^{-8/p}$; choose $D_0$ after the fixed
logarithmic degree of the envelope.
The normalization $1/J$ pays summing all $J$ replicas.
Selected reads are charged separately through
\eqref{eq:ram27-observer-input-budgets}.

\paragraph{Implementing the positive clocks.}
Use dyadic FIFO timers.
For an account, let $\vartheta$ be its physical-debt ceiling, $D$ its current
physical debt, and $k>0$ its epoch-fixed conversion from clock increments to
physical debt.
In particular, the background account has $k=\llambda_e/\vvr_e^2$.
Use a common clock mesh $\Delta_C$ so fine that $k\Delta_C\le\vartheta/2$ for
every permitted account.
If $D\ge\vartheta$, or if $0<\vartheta-D<k\Delta_C$, queue a request
immediately.
Otherwise install a timer at the current clock plus
\[
 R=\Delta_C\,2^{\left\lfloor
       \log_2\frac{\vartheta-D}{k\Delta_C}\right\rfloor}.
\]
Each radius $R$ has a FIFO: the clock and new equal-radius deadlines are
nondecreasing.
Direct handles remove a requested label's timers; an update scans $O(\log n)$ radius
headers and reports due prefixes.
A timed report has accumulated physical debt at least $D+kR\ge\vartheta/2$; the
immediate submesh branch also has $D\ge\vartheta-k\Delta_C\ge\vartheta/2$.
Absence of a report implies physical debt below $\vartheta$.
These half-ceilings are at least $c_a\Theta_e$ for the choice $c_a=1/16$ above.
Role changes apply the debt-transfer rule and reinstall timers, preserving due
flags, background baseline and conversion, and single payment.
Insertion costs $\O(1)$; scanning $O(\log n)$ headers per updated clock belongs to
the $O(nd_0+n)$ sparse clock row.

\subsection{Packed fine reads}

After committing selected coefficients and before new metadata transactions,
prepare final-graph fine fields from the actual-$\llambda$ index.
Rates $C\log n\epsilon_{\min}^{-2}\llambda_e$, with $\epsilon_{\min}\asymp n^{-2/p}$,
give a fine proxy without fine-score queries.
Freeze graph, proxy and inverse before drawing the
$J_{\max}=\Theta(\log n\epsilon_{\min}^{-2})$ sign lanes; retain this bank through
all adaptive metadata requests and prefix choices.

\paragraph{Covariance and canonical values in the fine bank.}
For the frozen final graph write its fine sparse proxy as
\[
 P_f=\sum_\alpha c_\alpha b_\alpha b_\alpha^T
             =(1\pm\epsilon_f)H_{\rm final},
 \qquad \epsilon_f\le c_f\delta_{\min}.
\]
Take a fixed symmetric inverse $T_f$ of $P_f$ with relative error at most
$c_f\delta_{\min}$.
Choose source dyadics $d_\alpha$ with relative error at most $c_f\delta_{\min}$
from $\sqrt{c_\alpha}$, and define, before drawing the sign lanes,
\[
 \widetilde P_f=\sum_\alpha d_\alpha^2b_\alpha b_\alpha^T,
 \quad
 \xxrand_j=\sum_\alpha d_\alpha\sigma_{\alpha j}b_\alpha,
 \quad h_j=T_ff_j .
\]
The signs are independent over sparse atoms and lanes.
The exact analytical covariance is
\[
 \Ex[h_jh_j^T\mid P_f,T_f,(d_\alpha)]
       =T_f\widetilde P_fT_f
       =(1\pm Cc_f\delta_{\min})H_{\rm final}^\dagger .
 \tag{CF5}\label{eq:ram27-fine-bank-covariance}
\]
Store $\widehat h_j$ on a predetermined common grid.
As in \eqref{eq:ram27-base-field-rounding}, polynomial ranges give deterministic
squared-score error small compared with $\delta_{\min}\lambda_{\min}$ on every
outcome of the frozen valid-proxy/inverse reference.

The certified old margin $\delta$ determines how many lanes of the fixed bank to
read.
Its permitted prefix is the canonical integer
\[
 J(\delta)=2^{ \log_2(C_{\rm fine}\log n\delta^{-2}) },
 \qquad J_{\max}\ge\max_{\delta\ge\delta_{\min}}J(\delta).
\]
Conditional on the frozen proxy, inverse, and source coefficients, scalar
Bernstein for the squared Rademacher linear forms in
\eqref{eq:ram27-fine-bank-covariance} and a union bound over the polynomial
family of labels and prefixes give additive score error $c_\star\delta\llambda_e$ and
empirical energy $O(\llambda_e)$, at polynomial confidence after increasing
$C_{\rm fine}$.
On the same event the Rademacher moment bound gives
\[
 |a_eA_eh_j|\le C\sqrt{\log n\llambda_e}.
                                                               \tag{O7}\label{eq:ram27-fine-lane-bound}
\]
This uses $\ttau_e\le C\llambda_e$; fields and prefixes require no current
fine-score query.

The old stored margin and current stored $\vvr_e^2,\llambda_e$ determine scale,
prefix and rounding.
Compute the quantized squared distance by \eqref{eq:ram27-packed-read-work}, add
predetermined positive reserves for proxy, sampling, quantization and evaluation
errors, and round upward on a score grid finer than a fixed fraction of
$\delta_{\min}\lambda_{\min}$.
This canonical upper score may be capped by the certified upper bound
$C\llambda_e$.
For the prefix length $J=J(\delta)$, the squared-distance perturbation from
quantization is bounded by
\[
 2q\left(J^{-1}\sum_j(\BB_e\widehat h_j)^2\right)^{1/2}+q^2,
\]
so $q=c_{\rm fine}\delta\sqrt{\llambda_e}/\vvr_e$ incurs additive $O(c_{\rm
fine}\delta\llambda_e)$ score error on the simultaneous empirical-energy event,
without an extra $\sqrt{\log n}$ loss.

Simultaneous prefix accuracy permits adaptive requests without selective redraw.
Sparse sources, inverse lanes and bit planes cost
\[
 n^{1+8/p+o(1)}
\]
per frame, as in the fine-inverse preparation row.

\paragraph{The packed query.}
At a requested label use its old certified $\delta=\ddeltabar_e$, using the
canonical prefix $J_e=J(\delta)$ fixed above, before inspecting the score.
Pack $\Theta(\log n)$ lanes into each word and store coordinate bits in planes.
Choose a dyadic scale satisfying
\[
 \frac{c_{\rm fine}\delta\sqrt{\llambda_e}}{2\vvr_e}
 \le q\le
 \frac{c_{\rm fine}\delta\sqrt{\llambda_e}}{\vvr_e},
\]
with a fixed sufficiently small $c_{\rm fine}>0$.
At this scale, bound \eqref{eq:ram27-fine-lane-bound} requires only
$b=\O(\log(1/\delta))$ planes.
Modular bit-sliced subtraction recovers the unique signed difference; high
common offsets cancel, with sign extension for negative integers.
Bit-sliced squaring and masked pair additions sum its squares in
\[
 O\!\left(\delta^{-2}
              (\log\log n+\log(1/\delta))^2\right)
                      \le \O(\delta^{-3})
                                                               \tag{O8}\label{eq:ram27-packed-read-work}
\]
word operations.
No unit-cost population-count instruction is used.
The reverse comparison $\llambda_e\le C(s+\ttau_e)$ converts the additive
$O(\delta\llambda_e)$ score error into $O(\delta)$ relative error in $s+\ttau_e$.
After the comparison constants and before the bank, fix $c_{\rm fine}$, proxy
accuracy and lane count so the combined log-score error divided by $\kappa$ is
at most $\delta/(256p)$.
Fixed $p=512$ and $\kappa$ leave logarithmic exponents unchanged.

The one-frame margin comparison validates this old precision on the final graph.
Choose the accuracy and tracking-cell placement constants with that comparison
reserve so the new epoch's birth error is at most $\bar\delta_{e,\rm
new}/(1024pC_\delta)$ in normalized tracking coordinates, as required by the
debt tolerances above.
Direct dyadic lower-margin selection supplies the interior reserve without
repeated precision-discovery reads.

\subsection{Packed conserved-source assembly}

Prepare each $\O(1)$-bit $\widehat \cc_e$ once per selected label.
One independent sign word per label and lane block suffices for $J=C_J\log n$, a
fixed number of blocks.
Emit identical opposite endpoint packets without materializing $J$ scalar
copies.

\paragraph{Exact modular accumulation.}
Encode every short dyadic in one fixed dyadic source unit.
Its integer encoding is an $\O(1)$-bit mantissa shifted by a known nonnegative
number of planes.
Polynomial coefficient ranges give an $O(\log n)$-bit bound on the absolute packet
sum.
Choose a $B_0=\Theta(\log n)$-bit modular range greater than twice this deterministic
bound.
At frame start, pad each destination accumulator independently by a uniform
$B_0$-bit integer in each lane.

Fix packet order from selected labels and mathematical data; neither order nor
mathematical stop tests inspect pads or carry lengths.
Skip planes below the first nonzero bit.
Apply Boolean full-adders and full-subtractors on the $\O(1)$ mantissa planes to
positive and negative masks separately, then continue until the carry/borrow
mask vanishes or reaches the modular boundary.
Negative packets subtract magnitudes, avoiding sign-extension work.
Only Boolean operations and mask zero tests are used; modular wrap triggers
neither operand failure nor a pad-dependent stop.

After the final packet has reached both endpoints, remove destination pads
exactly on a fixed full-width schedule.
Decode and transpose the unique signed sums before any inverse, Richardson
product or source-dependent header query.
The range bound ensures exact endpoint conservation without intermediate
flushes.
A work-cap interruption aborts the whole attempt before any incomplete-source
query.

\paragraph{Expected carry cost.}
Condition on the checkpoint, preceding attempts, and the current attempt's
entire mathematical history with actual-work and padding-cost caps ignored.
Exact unpadding leaves fields unchanged; neither fields nor mathematical stops
inspect pads or carry costs.
Each lane before addition is its uniform pad plus a fixed prefix, hence uniform
modulo $2^{B_0}$.
Its upper bits remain independent unbiased bits conditional on lower bits,
including whether they initiate a carry or borrow.
Survival through $t$ extra planes has probability at most $2^{-t}$ per lane, and
at most $\min\{1,C \log n2^{-t}\}$ after the lane union bound.
Thus each packet has expected $\O(1)$ work, even though different packets' carry
costs need not be independent.
Initialization and removal cost $O(\log n)$ per destination and frame.
It follows that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ram27-pad-work}
 \Ex[W_{\rm source}\mid
                  \text{one attempt's mathematical history}]
                \le C(M_{\rm pkt}\log\log n+nT\log n).
\end{equation}
The final transpose to scalar field vectors costs $\O(nT\log n)$, included in
$S_{\rm sparse}$.
Fresh pads are used for each restarted attempt.
Conditioning on the actual cost-aborted history, or on a whole restarted
trajectory influenced by earlier pad costs, would not justify
\eqref{eq:ram27-pad-work}.

\subsection{Clean checkpoints and the stopped expectation}

Coalesce selections, account requests and residual marks in one public queue,
without resampling physical masks.
Finish old source and replica batches, commit selected coefficients, update
owners and actual-$\llambda$ bins, prepare final fields, then read each queued
label once at its old price.
Close its old epoch before replacing reference $\vvr,\llambda$, $\bar
t,\ddeltabar,\pi,\Theta$ or conversions; the reference $\vvr,\llambda$ differ from
their committed physical values.
Central-only visits and private moves never close epochs.

A clean checkpoint is an actual full public refresh.
One read per original label costs
\[
 C(\log\log n)^2\sum_e\ddeltabar_e^{-3}=\O(m).
\]
Shared field preparation is subquadratic.
Finish pending old-view source and clock service, then scan all labels at frozen
old prices before resetting accounts.
Each read also performs the central inspection of
\Cref{lem:ram-central-scheduler}: retain its center only on the inner-window
test, otherwise install an inner-quarter center.
After every label receives both services, zero positive debts, residual targets
and due flags, and save baselines for signed endpoint and common positive
central clocks.
After abort, restore saved physical arrays, certified references, point
enclosures and certificates literally, without a new randomized read or central
decision; discard or rebuild abandoned caches, transactions and tapes.
Thus $C_{\rm initial}=0$ in \eqref{eq:ram27-ledger}.

Since $J_t$ is measurable before frame $t$'s fresh coins, the tower rule,
\eqref{eq:ram27-ledger}, and \eqref{eq:ram27-observer-work} bound total
positive-account reads, marked reads, and proposals by $C\Ex\sum_tJ_t B_t$,
times their $\O(1)$ point and packed-read factors.
Equation \eqref{eq:ram27-observer-input-budgets} pays the selected reads and,
since $\pi_e\ge1$, the source packet count.
Equation \eqref{eq:ram27-pad-work} pays source assembly.
The end refresh pays its own dense scan.
For the sparse implementation used here,
\[
 T\,n d_0\,n^{1/16}\,n^{16/p+o(1)}
       =n^{1011/512+o(1)}=o(n^2),
\]
and the fine-field, transpose, and source-inverse rows have smaller fixed
exponents.
This proves the work bound \eqref{eq:ram27-observer-attempt-work} under the
stated sparse-preparation assumptions.

Charge cap, numerical, proxy, inverse and capture failures at their first failed
premise.
Uniform source references and all-outcome grids justify unconditioned
expectations before coupling to the actual prefix; future cap success never
conditions signs, masks or no-hit processes.
Couple the deterministic attempt-work cap only until its first crossing.
Markov's inequality and \eqref{eq:ram27-observer-attempt-work} bound that
probability after choosing its constant, without a separate $m\log n$ confidence
allowance per attempt.

\section{Initialization and exact output}
\label{sec:ram27-endpoints}

A hub supplies a feasible interior midpoint and an infeasibility alternative.
We initialize tracking, prove polynomial numerical ranges, and convert a
terminal state into an integral flow and integer dual.
Cold initialization and exact output are each paid once.

Use the input and word model of \Cref{sec:ram27-objective}.
Here $n,m$ are input dimensions, and $N,M$ will denote the augmented working
dimensions.
An incidence row has $-1$ at its tail and $+1$ at its head.
On the augmented graph, use the core-score proxy construction and fixed sparse
inverse of \Cref{sec:ram27-linear}: their sparse work is $N^{19/16+o(1)}$, and initial dense construction costs $\O(M)$.

\subsection{Preprocessing and a feasible midpoint}

\paragraph{Removing lower bounds and trivial arcs.}
If some $l_e>\vvu_e$, that pair certifies infeasibility.
Otherwise set
\[
 x=\ff-l,\qquad u^{\rm new}=u-l,\qquad
 b^{\rm new}=b-\BB^Tl
\]
and record the cost offset $\cc^Tl$.
Delete zero-width arcs.
Self-loops have zero incidence and are optimized independently at the
cost-minimizing endpoint.
Check $\sum_v b_v=0$; a nonzero sum is an exact infeasibility certificate.
All transformations take $O(m+n)$ word work, and the transformed data retain a
fixed polynomial bound.
For the remainder of this subsection, $0\le x\le u$ denotes the transformed
problem and every capacity is a positive integer.

\paragraph{The hub augmentation.}
In this augmentation calculation, $r$ denotes the midpoint imbalance.
Put
\[
 U_{\rm tot}=\sum_eu_e,\qquad C_c=\max\{1,\norm{c}_\infty\},
 \qquad M_{\rm art}=2C_c(U_{\rm tot}+1)+1,\qquad
 r=b-\BB^T(u/2).
\]
Add a hub $h$ with demand zero.
For each original vertex $v$ add the two arcs
\[
 h\longrightarrow v:
     \quad u_{hv}=2(1+(r_v)_+),\quad c_{hv}=M_{\rm art},
 \qquad
 v\longrightarrow h:
     \quad u_{vh}=2(1+(-r_v)_+),\quad c_{vh}=M_{\rm art}.
\]
Since $r$ is half-integral and sums to zero, these capacities are positive
integers and the augmented midpoint is exactly feasible.
It has half-integer coordinates and both box margins at least $1/2$.
One incidence pass constructs the $n+1$-vertex, $m+2n$-arc instance with
polynomial data.

\paragraph{A dense working graph.}
Add $n$ zero-demand dummy vertices, all directed arcs between distinct dummy
vertices, and the two opposite arcs joining one dummy to the hub.
Each has capacity two and cost $M_{\rm art}$.
Their midpoint circulation is one on each arc.
The resulting graph has $N\le2n+1$ vertices, $M=\Theta(n^2)$ arcs, is connected,
and has at most one arc for each ordered pair.
It adds no route between original vertices.
The dummy subgraph, including its hub attachment, carries a circulation in every
feasible flow; replacing it by zero preserves all demands and strictly lowers
cost unless it was zero.
Thus optimum dummy flow is zero.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:ram27-hub}
An integral optimum of the augmented problem either has zero hub flow and
restricts to an original optimum, or yields in $O(M+N)$ work an original set $S$
with
\[
              b(S)<-u(\delta^+(S)).
\]
Before lower-bound removal this is
\[
 b^{\rm orig}(S)
       <l(\delta^-(S))-u^{\rm orig}(\delta^+(S)).
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The cycle argument in \Cref{lem:ram27-round} proves that every nonempty
integer-demand box-flow polytope has an integral optimum.
An original feasible integral flow has cost at most $C_cU_{\rm tot}$.
Every augmented integral flow with positive hub flow costs at least $M_{\rm
art}-C_cU_{\rm tot}>C_cU_{\rm tot}$.
Hence original feasibility forces every integral augmented optimum to have zero
hub flow.

For the other case, let $A_-$ be the vertices with $\ff_{vh}>0$ and let $A_+$ be
those with $\ff_{hv}>0$.
Conservation at the hub and absence of dummy flow make both nonempty.
Let $S$ be the set reachable from $A_-$ using residual arcs of the original
graph only.
It cannot meet $A_+$: a simple such path, together with the reverses of its two
positive hub arcs, would be an augmented residual cycle of cost at most
\[
 C_c m-2M_{\rm art}\le C_cU_{\rm tot}-2M_{\rm art}<0.
\]
The zero-length path handles a vertex in both sets.
This contradicts optimality.
No original residual arc leaves $S$, so all original arcs leaving $S$ are
saturated and all entering arcs carry zero.
Summing balance gives
\[
 b(S)=-u(\delta^+(S))-\sum_{v\in S}\ff_{vh}
                       <-u(\delta^+(S)).
\]
Reachability and checking this strict integer inequality are linear.
Substituting $b^{\rm new}=b^{\rm orig}-\BB^Tl$ gives the lower-bound form.
The cut is an independently checkable certificate.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Cold tracking initialization}

\paragraph{A contracting fixed-point map.}
Use the augmented dimensions and write $r=N-1$, $s=r/M$.
At the midpoint, $\zz_e=\vvu_e/(2\sqrt2)$, so $\zz_e^2=\vvu_e^2/8$ is rational.
For positive weights $w$, let
\[
 \HH^\star(w)=\BB^T\diag(\zz^2/\ww^2)\BB,\qquad
 \ttau(w)=\diag\!\left(
 \diag(\zz/\ww)\BB \HH^\star(w)^\dagger
 \BB^T\diag(\zz/\ww)\right).
\]
The desired regularized equation is
\[
              \ww=3s+\ttau(w),\qquad \llambda=w-2s .
\]
Define the logarithmic map
\[
 \Phi(v)=\tfrac23v+\tfrac13\log(3s+\ttau(e^v)).
\]
If $\PPi$ is the corresponding edge projection, differentiation gives
\[
 \frac{\partial\ttau_i}{\partial v_j}
              =-2\mathbf1_{\{i=j\}}\ttau_i+2\PPi_{ij}^2 .
\]
Consequently $D\Phi$ has nonnegative entries and every row sums to $2/3$; its
diagonal lies in $[0,2/3]$.
Thus $\Phi$ is an $\ell_\infty$ contraction with factor $2/3$.
The box $3s\le w\le3s+1$ is invariant.
Its unique fixed point is at logarithmic distance $O(\log N)$ from $\ww=3s$.
Reaching the required fixed multiple of $\kappa/p$ accuracy takes $O(\log\log
N)$ iterations.

\paragraph{Estimating one iteration.}
At each iteration build a fresh static proxy $P=\sum_\alpha c_\alpha b_\alpha
b_\alpha^T$ of the actual rational graph and use its fixed symmetric inverse
$T_P$.
Choose dyadics $d_\alpha$ approximating $\sqrt{c_\alpha}$ to the reserved
relative accuracy, and prepare $J=\Theta(\log N)$ fields $h_j=T_P\sum_\alpha
d_\alpha\sigma_{\alpha j}b_\alpha$, with signs independent across sparse atoms and
lanes.
Their analytical covariance is $T_P(\sum_\alpha d_\alpha^2b_\alpha
b_\alpha^T)T_P$, which approximates $\HH^\star(w)^\dagger$ to the allocated
relative accuracy.
Finite evaluations and canonical score rounding use the same numerical reserves
as the fine-bank construction in \Cref{sec:ram27-observer}.
An all-label squared-distance scan gives relative score accuracy
$\eta=c_\star\kappa/p$, with any prescribed polynomial confidence, after allocating a
fixed fraction of $\eta$ to each of the proxy, inverse, and sampling biases.
The static core construction gives these first estimates without a prior
tracking-weight bound.

\paragraph{The finite update and installed reference.}
Implement the scalar map with error at most $\eta$ in log coordinates and clamp
the result to $3s\le w\le3s+2$.
The unrounded update is the cube root of $\ww_e^2(3s+\widehat\ttau_e)$.
Locate its exponent by comparisons to powers of two, then use
fixed-relative-accuracy mantissa comparisons of cubes.
Since $\eta$ is fixed, this costs $O(\log\log N)$ word operations, with
fixed-depth rational comparisons.
The clamp cannot increase distance to the exact fixed point.
For example, store $\ww=3s+q2^{-B}$, so $\llambda=s+q2^{-B}$ and the floor is exact
even when $s=r/M$ is not dyadic.
These all share the fixed denominator $M2^B$.
The ordinary squared coefficients are exactly $\zz^2/\ww^2$, evaluated as
fixed-depth word-sized rationals from the stored state.
Sparse packets and accumulated vectors use separate common grids, with errors in
the proxy/inverse reserves.
Cold ranges follow from midpoint capacities and $3s\le w\le3s+2$, without later
tube bounds.

After $O(\log\log N)$ iterations the distance to the fixed point is as small as
any prescribed constant multiple of $\kappa/p$.
The map $g(v)=\log(3s+\ttau(e^v))$ has $\ell_\infty$ Lipschitz constant at most
four, so
\[
 \norm{v-g(v)}_\infty\le5\norm{v-v^\star}_\infty .
\]
Moreover $(3s+\ttau)/(s+\ttau)\le3$, so converting $w$ to $\llambda=w-2s$
multiplies the relative error by only a fixed constant.
Fixing all accuracy constants therefore gives
\[
 \left|\kappa^{-1}\log
       \frac{\llambda_e}{s+\ttau_e}\right|
                      \le\frac1{600p}.
\]
In particular the initial tracking potential is at most $2.01M$.
Prepare fresh fields and scan all initial references on the final installed
graph; the previous iteration's fields do not apply.
The dense cost is
\[
 \O(M\log N),
\]
plus a strictly subquadratic total of sparse preparations.
Scalar fixed-accuracy roots and logarithms fit this budget.

\paragraph{The initial central state.}
For centrality set $\yy_0=0$, keep $\ff_0=u/2$, and put
\[
                         M_c=\max_eu_e|\cc_e|.
\]
Use the same actual central exponent $P\ge512$, $P=O(\log\log(2N))$, as the
phase algorithm.
Let $\mu_0$ be the least power of two at least
\[
                 \max\{1,100P(1+M_c)/(\varepsilon s)\}.
\]
At the midpoint the barrier first derivative vanishes, and
\[
 |\vvs_{0,e}|=\frac{\zz_e|\cc_e|}{\varepsilon\mu_0\ww_e}
           \le\frac{M_c}{6\varepsilon\mu_0s}
           \le\frac1{600P}.
\]
Thus the initial central potential is also at most $2.01M$.
The residual is exactly zero.
Place the central representatives and score references in their interior windows
in the paid scan, with all observer debts zero.
The factor $P$ in the polynomially bounded $\mu_0$ keeps this potential bound
uniform as $P$ grows.

\subsection{Polynomial numerical ranges}
\label{sec:ram30-polynomial-ranges}

Bound reduced costs from the feasible midpoint, then capacity margins and
condition numbers, all before choosing arithmetic meshes.

Suppose a later state has $0<\ff<\vvu$, $\llambda\ge s$, and tracking for the actual
rational graph:
\[
 \left|\log\frac{\llambda_e}{s+\ttau_e}\right|\le\kappa,
 \qquad
 \mu_{\rm low}\le\mu\le\mu_0 ,
\]
where $\mu_{\rm low}=N^{-O(1)}$ includes terminal-phase overshoot.
Use the barrier quantities of \Cref{sec:ram-local}, with $A_{\rm st},u$ in place of
$B,\vvu^{\max}$, and write $\DD_0=\DD$ and $\XXi_0=\zz\cc^y/(\mu\ww)=\XXibar+\varepsilon\vvs$, where
$\cc^y=\cc-\BB\yy$, $|\vvs_e|\le1$, and $\ww=2s+\llambda$.
Define
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \kk=\DD_0\XXi_0+(3\llambda/\ww)(1-\DD_0\XXibar),\qquad
 W_e=\frac{3\llambda_e \zz_e^2}{\mu \ww_e^2\kk_e},\\
 \WW=\diag(W_e),\qquad
 \LL=\BB^TW\BB,\qquad \rho=\BB^T\ff-b .
 \end{gathered}
\]
Assume $\norm{\rho}_{\LL^\dagger}\le\beta\sqrt\mu$ with $\beta\le1$.
Since $|\DD_0|,|\XXibar|\le1$, $0\le \DD_0\XXibar\le1$, and $1\le3\llambda/\ww\le3$, we have
$1-\varepsilon\le \kk\le3+\varepsilon$.
Tracking gives $\llambda_e\le C$, $\sum_e\llambda_e=O(r)$, $3s\le \ww_e\le C$, and
$\sum_ew_e=O(r)$.

\paragraph{Reduced costs.}
Define the nonnegative box expression
\[
 G(\ff,\yy)=\sum_e\left[\ff_e(\cc_e^y)_+
                      +(\vvu_e-\ff_e)(-\cc_e^y)_+\right].
\]
Centrality alone gives $G(\ff,\yy)\le C\mu r$, without feasibility.
For instance, when $\cc_e^y\ge0$, writing $t=\ff_e/(\vvu_e-\ff_e)$,
\[
 \frac{\ff_ec_e^y}{\mu \ww_e}
   =1-\vvp+\varepsilon \vvs_e\sqrt{1+\vvp^2}\le1+\varepsilon .
\]
Set $S_c=\sum_e\vvu_e|\cc_e|$.
The estimates $\zz_e\le \vvu_e/2$ and $\ww_e\ge3s$ give
\[
 W_e\le C \vvu_e^2/(\mu s),\qquad
 W_e(\cc_e^y)^2=3\mu\llambda_e \XXi_{0,e}^2/\kk_e .
\]
Therefore, using quotient Cauchy--Schwarz before any lower eigenvalue bound,
\[
 \norm{\yy}_{\LL}
 \le C\left(S_c/\sqrt{\mu s}+\sqrt{\mu r}\right),\qquad
 |\yy^T\rho|\le C\beta(S_c/\sqrt s+\mu\sqrt r).
\]
The exactly feasible midpoint gives the identity
\[
 \frac12\sum_e\vvu_e|\cc_e^y|
   =G(\vvu/2,\yy)
   =G(\ff,\yy)+\cc^T(\vvu/2-\ff)+\yy^T\rho .
\]
For a fixed sufficiently large $C_B$, put
\[
 B_c=1+S_c+C_B\mu r+
                  C_B\beta(s^{-1}S_c+\mu r).
\]
Then $\norm{\cc^y}_\infty\le2B_c$, with a fixed polynomial upper bound independent
of subsequent arithmetic meshes.
\paragraph{Capacity margins and condition numbers.} If $\ff_e\le1/4$, then
$\vvu_e\ge1$ implies $\XXibar_e\ge2/\sqrt{10}$, hence $|\XXi_{0,e}|\ge1/2$.
Using $\zz_e\le \ff_e$ gives
\[
 \ff_e\ge\mu \ww_e/(2|\cc_e^y|)\ge3\mu s/(4B_c).
\]
The upper endpoint is symmetric, so
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ram27-margin-range}
 \min\{\ff_e,\vvu_e-\ff_e\}
       \ge\min\{1/4,\mu s/(4B_c)\}=N^{-O(1)} .
\end{equation}

Ground the hub.
Paths and $|A_ey|\le|\cc_e|+|\cc_e^y|$ bound every stored potential coordinate by a
polynomial.
All positive barrier and structural coefficients now have polynomial lower and
upper bounds.
For a connected positive coefficient graph with minimum weight $a_-$, path
Cauchy--Schwarz gives the grounded eigenvalue lower bound $a_-/N^2$; twice the
total coefficient mass is an upper bound.
Thus all required grounded condition numbers are polynomial.
These bounds determine grids for rational coefficients, sparse packets, and
accumulated vectors without assuming their accuracy in advance.

Finally the potential caps imply $\delta^{-p},(1-|\vvs|)^{-P}\le N^{O(1)}$.
Any fixed number of differentiations adds only fixed powers of inverse margins,
$p$, and $P=O(\log\log N)$.
The resulting polynomial derivative bounds fix word and grid exponents before
implementation.

\subsection{An exactly feasible fractional endpoint}

The interior algorithm permits a small incidence residual.
We remove it while preserving the box constraints and a small duality gap: first
bound the ideal feasible projection, then approximate it and correct the
remaining residual exactly on a tree.

For any $\yy$, the quantity
\[
 \mathcal D(\yy)=\bb^T\yy+\sum_e\vvu_e\min\{0,\cc_e-(\BB\yy)_e\}
\]
is a dual lower bound.
For exactly feasible $x$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ram27-gap}
 \cc^Tx-\mathcal D(\yy)=\sum_e
       \left[x_e(\cc_e^y)_++(\vvu_e-x_e)(-\cc_e^y)_+\right].
\end{equation}

\paragraph{The analytical feasible projection.}
Consider the ideal projection
\[
                  \widehat \ff=\ff-\WW\BB\LL^\dagger\rho .
\]

Let $\ell_e^{\rm phys}=W_eA_e\LL^\dagger \BB_e^T$.
The ordinary graph is exactly $\HH^\star=\BB^T\diag(\zz^2/\ww^2)\BB$, so the tracking
bound applies directly in this metric.
Since $\mu\LL\succeq csH^\star$, inverse order and the trivial bound
$\ell_e^{\rm phys}\le1$ imply
\[
 \ell_e^{\rm phys}/\llambda_e
      \le C\min\{1/\llambda_e,\llambda_e/s\}\le Cs^{-1/2}.
\]
Row Cauchy--Schwarz against the residual tube then gives
\[
 \norm{(\widehat \ff-\ff)/\zz}_\infty\le C\beta s^{-1/4},\qquad
 \norm{(\widehat \ff-\ff)/\zz}_{\ww}\le C\beta .
\]
At the terminal trajectory state the residual invariant of
\Cref{lem:ram-residual} allows $\beta=\beta_s$.
The physical step scale then makes the first bound at most $1/10$.
Consequently $\widehat \ff$ is in the box with a known inverse-polynomial margin.
Since $\zz\cc^y=\mu\ww C_0$,
\[
 0\le \cc^T\widehat \ff-\mathcal D(\yy)
 \le G(\ff,\yy)+C\mu\beta\sqrt r\le C_{\rm gap}\mu r.
\]
Choose $\mu_{\rm end}$ so that $C_{\rm gap}\mu_{\rm end}r\le1/(64M)$.
It is inverse polynomial and $\log(\mu_0/\mu_{\rm end})=O(\log N)$.

\paragraph{Computing the projection.}
Freeze the exact rational weights $W_e$ at the terminal stored state, using the
square-root cancellations in the proof of
\Cref{lem:ram-algebraic-rate-enclosure}.
The physical owner gives a sparse constant proxy of $\LL$.
After grounding the hub, its fixed sparse inverse is scaled to obey
\[
          a\LL_g^{-1}\preceq T\preceq\LL_g^{-1}
\]
for an absolute $a>0$.
Starting from zero, use
\[
             v_{j+1}=v_j+T(\rho_g-\LL_gv_j).
\]
This fixed-metric iteration contracts exact energy error by $1-a$.
Each true product is one $O(M)$ original-label pass; each inverse application
has the separately supplied sparse price.

\paragraph{Finite arithmetic and a residual certificate.}
Use the preceding range and projection bounds to choose known polynomial bounds
$Q\ge\max\{1,\max_eW_e\}$, $w_-\le\min_eW_e$, and a known margin
$\sigma\le\min_e\{\widehat \ff_e,\vvu_e-\widehat \ff_e\}$.
Put $\KK_0=2MQ$, $C_1=1+\sum_e|\cc_e|$.
Choose a positive dyadic $\eta=N^{-O(1)}$ satisfying
\[
 (1+2M)\sqrt Q\,\eta
              \le\min\{\sigma/4,1/(64MC_1)\}.
                                                               \tag{E1}\label{eq:ram27-endpoint-error-reserve}
\]
These bounds reserve the box and duality-gap margins.
Choose the dyadic scales by squared rational comparisons or predetermined
polynomial bounds, and the smaller iteration tolerance $\nnu$ so that
\[
 0<\nu_{\rm it}\le\min\{\eta/8,\eta\sqrt{w_-}/(16N\sqrt{\KK_0})\}.
\]
For each product round the fixed-depth rational $W_e(v_{\rm head}-v_{\rm tail})$
once to a common grid of mesh $\delta_{\rm mult}$, then add opposite endpoint
integers.
If $d_v$ is the incidence count, the error at $v$ is at most
$\epsilon_v=d_v\delta_{\rm mult}/2$; the full error vector has Euclidean norm at
most $M\delta_{\rm mult}$.
Choose
\[
 NM\delta_{\rm mult}/\sqrt{w_-}\le a\nu_{\rm it}/32,\qquad
 M\delta_{\rm mult}\le\eta\sqrt{w_-}/(32N).
\]
Round the sparse inverse application and stored addition with another local
energy error at most $a\nu_{\rm it}/32$.
The actual iteration has
\[
 E_{j+1}\le(1-a)E_j+a\nu_{\rm it}/16,\qquad
 E_j=\norm{v_j-\LL_g^{-1}\rho_g}_{\LL_g}.
\]
The polynomial initial energy and inverse-polynomial $\nnu$ imply $E_j\le\nnu$
after a predetermined $O(\log N)$ steps.
Every dense edge uses a fixed number of word operations and one rounding;
unrelated rational denominators are never summed.

One final dense pass computes a rounded value $\widetilde r$ of $\rho_g-\mathcal
L_gv$ and the exact common-grid sum
\[
             R_2=\sum_{v\ne h}(|\widetilde r_v|+\epsilon_v)^2 .
\]
Accept the solve only if
\[
                       N^2R_2\le w_-\eta^2/4.
\]
Because $\LL_g\succeq w_-N^{-2}I$, this certifies energy error at most
$\eta/2$, independent of whether the sparse primitive was successful.
On its success event,
\[
 \sqrt{R_2}\le\sqrt{\KK_0}\nnu+2M\delta_{\rm mult}
                   \le\eta\sqrt{w_-}/(8N),
\]
so the check passes with reserve.
If the check fails, report terminal failure; do not repeat the dense solve.

\paragraph{Exact correction on a tree.}
Form $\ff-WAv$ by one dense pass and round to a common terminal primal grid with
coordinate error at most $\sqrt Q\,\eta/2$.
The result $\widetilde \ff$ satisfies
\[
 \norm{\widetilde \ff-\widehat \ff}_\infty\le\sqrt Q\,\eta,\qquad
 \norm{\BB^T\widetilde \ff-b}_1\le2M\sqrt Q\,\eta.
\]
For $q=b-\BB^T\widetilde \ff$, take any spanning tree and send each rooted subtree
total $q(T_v)$ through its parent edge, with the appropriate sign.
This constructs $d$ with
\[
 \BB^Td=q,\qquad \norm{d}_\infty\le\norm{q}_1/2
\]
in $O(M+N)$ exact common-grid work.
Set $x=\widetilde \ff+d$.
Equation \eqref{eq:ram27-endpoint-error-reserve} preserves the box and gives
\[
 \norm{x-\widehat \ff}_\infty
       \le(1+2M)\sqrt Q\,\eta,\qquad
                 0\le \cc^Tx-\mathcal D(\yy)<1/(8M).
\]
Balances, bounds, and this gap are checked exactly.
The complete terminal projection costs $O(M\log N)$ plus a strictly subquadratic
sparse row.

\subsection{Integral rounding and an integer dual}

Cost-nonincreasing rounding preserves the endpoint's gap bound: $\cc^T\ff-\mathcal D(\yy)\le
\cc^Tx-\mathcal D(\yy)<1/(8M)$.
This meets the $1/(4M)$ hypothesis of \Cref{lem:ram27-dual}, which supplies the
integer dual.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:ram27-round}
From an exactly feasible common-grid flow $x$ with integer demands and bounds,
an integral feasible flow $\ff$ with $\cc^T\ff\le \cc^Tx$ can be found in $O((M+N)\log
N)$ word operations.
Each coordinate remains between its initial floor and ceiling.
\end{lemma} \begin{proof} The fractional-edge subgraph cannot be a nonempty
forest: a leaf would have exactly one fractional incident coordinate,
contradicting its integer balance.
Along a fractional undirected cycle choose the circulation direction of
nonpositive cost, and move until a coordinate hits its initial floor or ceiling.
This preserves feasibility and cost nonincrease while permanently removing a
fractional coordinate.
The finite argument also proves integral optimum existence from any real optimum
in the compact box.

Insert fractional edges into a dynamic forest.
A within-tree insertion exposes a cycle; signed path-cost sums choose its
direction and directional path minima its step.
Store the signed cost and minimum distances to initial floors and ceilings.
Augmentation subtracts from one distance and adds to the other; reversal negates
cost and swaps the aggregates.
These are constant-size dynamic-tree lazy updates.
Cut every newly integral forest edge.
To handle ties, after a cut recurse on the two surviving endpoint subpaths,
discarding a subpath with positive minimum.
Each removal creates at most two further queries; every edge is removed
permanently.
If the inserted edge remains fractional, at least one forest edge has become
integral.
Cut all tied zero-distance edges before linking the inserted edge; these cuts
break the old path.
Each fractional edge is inserted at most once and each forest edge is cut at
most once, giving $O(M)$ dynamic-tree operations.
All flows stay on the initial common grid.
\end{proof}

The dynamic-tree operations above cost $O(\log N)$ amortized time \cite[Sections
2--4]{ST83:journal}; see also
\href{https://arxiv.org/pdf/1507.08139}{Kang--Payor, \emph{Flow Rounding}},
Sections 2--4.
This is one terminal $O(M\log N)$ call, storing each capacity once.

\begin{lemma}
\label{lem:ram27-dual}
If $\ff$ is integral feasible and $0\le \cc^T\ff-\mathcal D(\yy)<1/(4M)$, an integer potential
$p$ satisfying all residual reduced-cost inequalities can be found in $O(M+N\log
N)$ word operations.
\end{lemma} \begin{proof} Let $g_{\rm gap}=\cc^T\ff-\mathcal D(\yy)$.
Every residual arc $a\to b$ of integer cost $w_{ab}$ has positive integer residual
capacity, so \eqref{eq:ram27-gap} gives $\yy_b-\yy_a\le w_{ab}+g_{\rm gap}$.
For $0\le\alpha<1$, put $p_v(\alpha)=\lfloor \yy_v+\alpha\rfloor$.
If $\yy_b-\yy_a\le w_{ab}$, its residual inequality holds for every shift.
Otherwise the violating shifts occupy measure $\yy_b-\yy_a-w_{ab}\le g_{\rm gap}$.
There are at most $2M$ residual arcs, so their forbidden union has measure below
$1/2$.

Initialize $p=\lfloor \yy\rfloor$ and count violations in one scan.
If zero, return immediately.
Otherwise sort the distinct events $1-\{\yy_v\}$ strictly between zero and one.
At each event increment all tied vertices, updating the violation count through
their incident residual arcs.
Inspect only after the complete tie group.
These tests cover every interval between events, so the measure bound guarantees
a zero count.
Each residual arc is updated at most twice.

The returned inequalities are
\[
 \cc_e-(\BB p)_e\ge0\quad(\ff_e<\vvu_e),\qquad
 \cc_e-(\BB p)_e\le0\quad(\ff_e>0).
\]
They make every summand of \eqref{eq:ram27-gap} vanish, so $\cc^T\ff=\mathcal D(p)$.
This exactly certifies optimality.
\end{proof}

All fractional-part comparisons use represented grid integers.
The final primal and dual scans are exact.
If the augmented optimum has positive hub flow, use \Cref{lem:ram27-hub};
otherwise restrict the flow and potential and restore lower bounds, fixed arcs,
and optimized loops.
For the original lower-bounded instance the checked dual value is
\[
 \bb^Tp+\sum_e\left[
       l_e\max\{0,\cc_e-(\BB p)_e\}
          +\vvu_e\min\{0,\cc_e-(\BB p)_e\}\right].
\]
Thus either output has a certificate checkable in $O(m+n)$ work.

\subsection{Matching and the bounded-size branch}

The proof of \Cref{cor:ram27-matching} in \Cref{sec:ram27-main-theorem} gives
the matching reduction.
Below the fixed asymptotic cutoff, enumerate the augmented integer box and
choose a feasible minimum-cost assignment; the midpoint and cycle argument
guarantee existence.
Bellman--Ford from a zero-distance source in its optimal residual graph gives
integer potentials.
Apply the hub alternative or restrict and check the solution.
For fixed cutoff and input exponent this finite work is absorbed by the
constant.
Empty and one-vertex inputs are handled directly.

\subsection{Confidence and one-time work caps}

\begin{lemma}
\label{lem:ram27-endpoint-caps}
For any fixed input-range and confidence exponents, the cold and terminal
procedures can each be run once with a deterministic cap
\[
 \O(M\log N)
       +N^{19/16+o(1)},
\]
whose probability of firing on a valid endpoint input is at most any prescribed
inverse polynomial.
The degree in the dense iterated-logarithm factor is absolute.
A cap or failed certificate returns failure and does not restart either dense
endpoint.
\end{lemma} \begin{proof} Cold contraction uses a predetermined number $\O(1)$ of iterations, each with
$J=C \log n$ lanes and one all-label squared-distance scan.
Including the separate final installed-graph scan, these dense operations cost
$\O(M\log n)$ on every tape.
Terminal refinement uses a predetermined number $O(\log n)$ of true matrix products;
its final exact residual test passes or reports failure.
Tree correction, integral cycle rounding, integer dual extraction, and exact
output checks cost $O(M\log n+N\log n)$.
Sparse-inverse operand guards apply on every tape.

All random preparatory work lies in $S(N)=N^{19/16+o(1)}n^{o(1)}$, including the
cold calls, amplitude buckets, and terminal factor applications.
Make the stated child, replay, residual-phase, sparse-copy, factor-entry, and
sample-count caps literal.
For a Bernoulli or categorical-position batch in the sampling reference with
public conditional mean count at most $V_*$, scalar Chernoff gives a cap
$C(V_*+(c_0+A_{\rm st}+10)\log n)$ with failure probability at most $N^{-c_0-A_{\rm st}-10}$, where
$N^{A_{\rm st}}$ is the fixed call horizon.
This also counts selected root positions that are subsequently discarded.
The structural finite-geometric primitive of \Cref{struct27:sec:skipping} has
its separately allocated coupling failure and $O(\log^2 n)$ work per selected or
exhausted position; stream starts are included.
Apply structural work bounds only before the first failed structural premise.
On their simultaneous correctness event the stated scan caps cannot be reached,
so no conditioning on future success is needed.

After these caps there are at most a deterministic $K_*=O(S(N))$ logical
uniform-integer and eligible-pivot choices.
Integer rejection succeeds with conditional probability at least $1/2$;
combining it with the low-degree eligibility test gives at least $1/4$.
Their conditional geometric moment bound implies total elementary trials
$O(K_*+(c_0+10)\log n)$ except with probability $N^{-c_0-10}$.
The conditional moment bound applies to adaptive histories.

For geometric gaps, use the \hyperref[par:ram-stream-endpoint]{finite endpoint
construction}, with prefix constant $\BB$, power mesh $\zeta$, and remaining
length $d$.
Each completed gap has its ideal value on the stated coupling, costs $O(\log^2 n)$
deterministically, and has conditional failure probability at most
$C(d2^{-B\log n}+d^2\zeta)$ for $d\le N^{A_{\rm st}}$.
The chosen precision makes the union over at most $N^{A_{\rm st}}$ calls smaller than the
allocated inverse-polynomial reserve.
Report failure at the first ambiguous comparison, without redrawing a gap or
restarting the dense endpoint.

Every gap belongs to sparse proxy or preparation work.
With the preceding stream-start and sample-count caps, there are $O(S(N))$ gaps:
each stream uses at most its raw activation count plus one.
Their $O(\log^2 n)$ price is therefore absorbed in $S(N)$'s unspecified logarithmic
factor.
Dense label scans use no geometric gaps.
The scalar table of \Cref{lem:ram-stream-scalar} has one-time deterministic
$N^{1/8}n^{o(1)}$ work.

Union conditional failures over the public horizon, fixing each call at its
valid input before fresh coins, including the adaptive terminal checkpoint.
Confidence changes constants, not dense iteration counts.
The sparse row is $o(N^2)$; the bounded-input branch covers the exceptions.
\end{proof}

\section{Clean phases and restarts}
\label{sec:ram-phases}

Here $n,m,r$ again denote the working graph's vertex count, label count, and
rank, written $N,M,N-1$ in \Cref{sec:ram27-endpoints}.

A \emph{frame} comprises a physical update and its reference/metadata service.
A \emph{clean checkpoint} saves the physical state and refreshed references,
with zero new observation debt.
An \emph{attempt} tries to complete $T$ frames from that checkpoint.
Success installs a new checkpoint and completes a \emph{phase}; failure restores
the old one.

Each frame draws one independent original-label mask.
A failed residual, source-normalizer, or work test aborts the attempt without
redraw, so the reference laws of \Cref{sec:ram-local}
and~\ref{sec:ram-finite-direction} apply at every valid pre-mask state.

\subsection{The four implementation contracts}

In addition to local drift, use the following contracts, verified for this
implementation by \Cref{prop:ram27-contract-closure}.

\begin{enumerate}
\item \emph{Potential certificates.}
At a returned valid state, representatives and class counts give
computable nonnegative certificates $U_R,U_Z$ satisfying
\[
 \Psi_R\le U_R\le2\Psi_R,\qquad
 \Psi_Z\le U_Z\le2\Psi_Z.
\]
Charge their preparation without an all-edge leverage calculation.

\item \emph{Clean checkpoints.}
At a returned state in the declared outer ranges, public refresh and rebuild
create a clean checkpoint for $\O(m)+S_{\rm cp}(n)$ work, where $S_{\rm
cp}(n)=o(m)$.
Refresh every original-label observer reference before zeroing new debt
accounts, and save the physical state and certified references.
Restore all mutable state consistently at this same price.

\item \emph{Expected work.}
For an attempt stopped at its first invalid primitive premise, valid completed
outer exit, or prospective genuine-work ledger crossing, its uncapped reference
obeys the nonnegative accounting bound
\begin{equation}
 \Ex W\le C\O(1)
 \left[m+\Ex\sum_tJ_tB_{*,t}\right]+S(n),
 \label{eq:ram-phase-work-contract}
\end{equation}
where $J_t$ says that frame $t$ begins, and $S(n)\le n^{2-\epsilon+o(1)}$
for fixed $\epsilon>0$.
Include original-label proposal evaluations, selected updates, observer credits
and payments, scalar arithmetic, inverse/field preparations, list starts, and
rejected-endpoint refreshes.
Freeze old-owner prices before generating each batch.
Observer payments may use earlier frames' credits.
The prospective genuine ledger has the same expected bound.

\item \emph{Finite execution and controlled failures.}
A fresh construction has inverse-polynomial failure probability given the valid
history just before its tapes.
A public observer epoch has an inverse-polynomial probability of unreported
residual exit given its valid zero-target birth, for adaptive later fields and
stopped at an earlier primitive failure.
This bound is not conditional on intermediate misses.
Union at call starts and epoch births under the predeclared polynomial horizon,
from each clean checkpoint's full epoch reset.
Domain/operand guards, iterative caps, and actual work counting ensure finite
execution on every outcome.
Hidden matrix or projection failures need not be detectable; couple to the valid
reference only until its first failure.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Stopped potential and work budgets}

Recall from \Cref{sec:ram-local} that $\beta_s=\gamma\beta_f$ and
$B_*=\beta_fm/\sqrt r$.
Let $\epsilon_{\rm tot}$ bound the sum of the local recurrences' additive
$\epsilon_{\rm num}$ terms over one attempt; the parameter hierarchy makes this
reserve inverse polynomial.

Fix inner constants $C_{R,\rm in},C_{Z,\rm in}$ and start with
\[
 U_R\le C_{R,\rm in}m,
 \qquad U_Z\le C_{Z,\rm in}P^2m.
\]
Choose a fixed phase constant $C_T$ and length
\[
 T=C_T\sqrt r/\beta_s
   =O\left(n^{3/4+3/p}P^{16}\right).
\]
Constants may depend on the fixed $\gamma$.
The attempt length is fixed independently of the potential.

We use two proof processes.
The \emph{killed process} assigns zero potential after an abort or primitive
failure and controls endpoint potentials.
The \emph{outer-exit process} instead retains the value at a valid outer
crossing, so the crossing remains visible in its expectation.
These processes are analytical devices; neither changes the algorithm.

If a stop occurs inside a begun frame, complete that frame only in the uncapped
mathematical reference before killing its potential, or assign zero if a
primitive failure occurs first.
Start from the nonnegative matrix-killed tracking candidate and all-mask central
candidate; later observer, epoch, or operational failures only decrease them.
The local tracking bound already includes its same-frame signed-error reserve.
A pre-mask preparation abort also sets the candidate to zero.
The fixed small-step choices give $a_R,a_Z\in[0,1]$, so the affine drift bounds
have nonnegative right-hand sides and remain valid at that abort.
An abort during source preparation still charges its header work to that frame's
$B_*$ and sparse row.

\begin{lemma}
\label{lem:ram-attempt-budgets}
For any attempt stopped by the preceding rules, before imposing its actual-work
cap,
\[
 \sum_tJ_t B_{*,t}\le C_\gamma C_Tm.
\]
Thus the work contract gives $\Ex W=\O(m)+S(n)$.
\end{lemma} \begin{proof} The frame-start indicator $J_t$ is predictable.
The first bound is deterministic: $TB_*=O(C_T\sqrt r/(\gamma\beta_f))
(\beta_fm/\sqrt r)=O_\gamma(C_Tm)$.
Apply \eqref{eq:ram-phase-work-contract}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{An attempt and its probability of success}

Use larger fixed outer constants $C_{R,\rm out},C_{Z,\rm out}$.
After each completed valid frame, abort if
\[
 U_R>C_{R,\rm out}m
 \quad\hbox{or}\quad
 U_Z>C_{Z,\rm out}P^2m.
\]
Also abort at a failed computable residual or $\Gamma$ test, a numerical/domain
guard, a prospective genuine-ledger cap, or the actual work cap.
At a surviving length-$T$ endpoint, perform the paid public refresh before
testing the inner certificates.
Accept the checkpoint only if both inner tests pass.
A failed endpoint still pays for its refresh.

\begin{lemma}
\label{lem:ram-outer-exit}
The outer constants can be chosen so that an outer exit has probability at most
$1/20$, in addition to the separately budgeted primitive first-failure
probability.
\end{lemma} \begin{proof} Set $V_R=\Psi_R/m$, $V_Z=\Psi_Z/(P^2m)$, and
$V=V_R+V_Z$.
The two local drifts bound its conditional expected increase by
$C_\gamma\beta_f/\sqrt r$ per valid frame.
Summing through $T$ gives a fixed constant depending on $\gamma,C_T$.

Kill the outer-exit process at a primitive first failure before any undefined
barrier evaluation.
Freeze its first \emph{valid completed outer crossing} at the crossing value.
An earlier operational abort also freezes it, but receives no exit witness.
For a midframe abort, complete the mathematical frame before freezing, or assign
zero if a primitive failure occurs first.
Freezing adds no drift.
Once a valid crossing is frozen, a later virtual-continuation failure cannot
erase its witness.
Killing a nonnegative candidate adds no positive drift, with the local
same-frame signed-error reserve included.
This remains true when a public-epoch failure has large conditional probability
at the current prefix.

A crossing $U_R>C_{R,\rm out}m$ retains the witness $V\ge V_R>C_{R,\rm out}/2$;
the central crossing is analogous.
The expected final outer-exit value is bounded by $V(0)+C_\gamma T\beta_f/\sqrt
r+\epsilon_{\rm tot}$.
Choose both outer constants sufficiently large and apply Markov to this retained
crossing value.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}
\label{lem:ram-inner-return}
The inner constants and $C_T$ can be chosen so that the probability of a
surviving endpoint failing either inner test is at most $1/20$.
\end{lemma} \begin{proof} Use the killed process at every earlier abort.
Its nonnegative potentials obey the affine recurrences, since killing only
decreases them.
For tracking,
\[
 \Ex\Psi_R(T)
 \le e^{-a_RT}\Psi_R(0)+Cm+\epsilon_{\rm tot},
 \qquad a_RT=\Theta(C_T/\gamma).
\]
For centrality,
\[
 \Ex\Psi_Z(T)
 \le e^{-a_ZT}\Psi_Z(0)+C_\gamma Pm+\epsilon_{\rm tot},
 \qquad a_ZT=\Theta(C_TP).
\]
Choose $C_{R,\rm in}$ large relative to the first equilibrium constant, and
$C_{Z,\rm in}$ large relative to the second constant divided by the uniform
lower bound $P\ge512$.
Then choose $C_T$ so both contraction factors are small enough.
For example both expected endpoint potentials can be made less than one
hundredth of their respective inner thresholds.

The refresh preserves physical state and true potentials.
At a surviving endpoint, a rejected upper certificate puts the true potential
above half its inner threshold.
Markov at that threshold proves the claim without conditioning on survival or
changing the within-attempt law.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Last-frame service.}
Prepare scalar ranges, field enclosures, and final metadata service on a fixed
one-step expansion of the outer range.
The tracking estimate gives $\delta'_e\ge(1-\epsilon_0)\ddelta_e$ on a good
frame, where $\epsilon_0$ is a sufficiently small fixed constant; therefore an
expanded tracking cap is $(1-\epsilon_0)^{-p}C_{R,\rm out}m$.
For centrality, the wall and source caps give, with a fixed small movement
constant $c_{\rm mov}$,
\[
 \frac{|\vvs'_e-\vvs_e|}{1-|\vvs_e|}\le c_{\rm mov}/P^2,
 \qquad
 \psi_P(\vvs'_e)\le(1-c_{\rm mov}/P^2)^{-P}\psi_P(\vvs_e)
                              \le e^{2c_{\rm mov}/P}\psi_P(\vvs_e).
\]
This expansion is uniform in growing $P$.
A frame that crosses an outer certificate is serviced within these expanded
ranges and is never used to begin another valid frame.

\subsection{Operational caps and their reference runs}
