\paragraph{Prospective ledger and token caps.}
\Cref{lem:ram-attempt-budgets} and the work contract bound the expected genuine
ledger by $\O(m)$ per attempt.
Use the changed batch's prices frozen before the mask and test the prospective
cumulative ledger before pruning, retirement, or carrying.
Include the crossing batch in the stopped expectation; Markov then gives
crossing probability at most $1/100$ for a sufficiently large fixed cap.

Choose a separate sharp token cap $N_{\rm sharp}=\O(m)$ after
proving the weighted-rate expectation under the coarse admission cap.
The current moments proved under that coarse cap price the batch.
Here $N$ counts admission tokens and $Z$ is the genuine-event ledger at
normalized frozen old prices.
\Cref{prop:ram27-owner-attempt} gives $N\le N_{\rm init}+81Z$.
Before commit and copying, test $N_{\rm init}+81Z_{\rm pros}\le N_{\rm sharp}$,
retaining the per-token guard without needing future export counts.
The coarse reference includes the whole proposed crossing batch.
Choose the cap constant after bounding $\Ex(N_{\rm init}+81Z)$; Markov makes a
failed test a small constant-probability attempt failure.

With the owner's threshold $T_0\asymp m/\log^{1024} n$, only after imposing this
sharp cap use the deterministic bound
\[
 1+\log\left(1+\frac{N_{\rm sharp}}{T_0}\right)
          =\O(1),
\]
since $P=O(\log\log n)$.
This pathwise depth pays copying; $\Ex N=\O(m)$ alone would not bound
$\Ex[N\log(1+N/T_0)]$.

\paragraph{The actual-work cap.}
Fix the mean-work constants before the cap $\O(m)$.
The following coupling includes exact random sampling and bounds exhaustion by
$1/100$.
A cap may interrupt a call but never redraws an individual query.

For the coupling proof, use a coarse reference $\mathcal R_0$ with only its
predeclared polynomial horizon and ordinary mathematical stops, and a reference
$\mathcal R_1$ that additionally applies the sharp ledger and token guards but
omits the actual-work cap.
Their primitive-failure event $\mathcal B$ includes a first failure in the
same-tape virtual continuation of $\mathcal R_0$, even after the real execution
has stopped.
Its probability is bounded at valid call starts and epoch births, not
conditional on an expensive partial stream.
If $\mathcal A_W$ is real work-cap exhaustion at $w_*$,
instruction-by-instruction coupling gives
\[
 \mathcal A_W\cap\mathcal B^c
       \subseteq\{W_{\mathcal R_1}\ge w_*\},\qquad
 \Pr(\mathcal A_W)\le
       \Ex W_{\mathcal R_1}/w_*+\Pr(\mathcal B).
\]
This includes a stop during an exact gap call; the reference continues the
operation on the same logical tape.

\paragraph{Independent virtual tails.}
Give attempts and calls independent logical tapes, revealed as used.
After a midcall stop, complete only the virtual history with an independent
auxiliary suffix, reserving the unread physical stream for the next real
attempt.
This preserves the reference law and the next attempt's independence from that
history.
The suffix never controls the algorithm and requires no executed computation or
random access: the machine requests fresh independent words and discards
abandoned cursors.

\paragraph{Random source pads across restarts.}
Draw exactly cancelling padding independently of this attempt's uncapped
mathematical history.
In the reference without the actual-work cap, mathematical stop tests and the
prescribed source-finalization schedule do not inspect padding cost.
Integrate the conditional carry bound over that history.
Each new attempt conditions on its checkpoint and draws fresh padding;
independence from the entire restarted trajectory is unnecessary.

\subsection{Amplification and running time}

\begin{theorem}
\label{thm:ram-phase-composition}
Assume the local drift bounds and the four contracts above.
Assume a clean inner initialization and a terminal extraction at $\mu\le\mu_{\rm
end}$ with polynomial scale ratio $\mu_0/\mu_{\rm end}$.
Then the clean-phase algorithm makes the required progress with any prescribed
polynomial confidence in
\[
 O\bigl(m\log n\O(1)\bigr)
       +n^{2-\epsilon+o(1)}
 =O\bigl(n^2\log n\O(1)\bigr)
\]
work, for fixed positive $\epsilon$ and $m=\Theta(n^2)$.
\end{theorem} \begin{proof} Allocate a small fixed probability to the two
potential failures of \Cref{lem:ram-outer-exit,lem:ram-inner-return}, the
genuine-ledger, sharp-token and actual-work caps, and all primitive failures.
For the last group, condition on the clean checkpoint and sum fresh-call failure
bounds at their call times and no-hit failure bounds at their adaptively chosen
epoch births.
Each valid birth starts at zero residual target, and there are only polynomially
many births.
The epoch supermartingale bound already covers its entire adaptive future, so no
per-frame conditional no-hit bound is used.
By the preceding lemmas, each attempt has success probability at least a fixed
$q_0>0$, conditional on any valid clean checkpoint and the entire preceding
history.
Every successful attempt makes between two fixed positive constants of progress
in $-\log\mu$, because it contains $T$ frames and each actual ratio obeys
$1-\theta=\Theta(\beta_s/\sqrt r)$.

Restore physical state exactly with its saved certified references.
Rebuild owners and mutable metadata, discarding failed queues, due flags,
private caches, buffered transactions, and tape cursors.
Each restored observer epoch has its refreshed reference and zero new debt; the
next attempt uses fresh mask and source tapes.
The checkpoint contract pays this restoration.

A polynomial initial-to-terminal scale ratio requires $O(\log n)$ successful
attempts.
Conditional success probability at least $q_0$ gives geometric domination; a
Chernoff or elementary exponential-supermartingale calculation bounds the number
of attempts by $O_{c,q_0}(\log n)$ except with probability $n^{-c}$.
Apply this domination to indicators of actual accepted valid progress before the
first primitive failure, set to one thereafter in the analytical continuation.
Before failure the checkpoint is valid; afterward the artificial success
probability is one.
The lower-tail bound therefore needs no conditioning on eventual absence of
failure.
On the no-failure coupling event the indicators agree with actual progress up to
termination; add the first-failure probability separately.
Set that number as a deterministic outer cap.
No independence between successive checkpoints is assumed.
Multiplying the per-attempt actual-work cap, restoration price, and sparse
budget by this attempt count proves the stated bound.
Fixed powers of $\log n,P$ in the sparse rows are absorbed by the strict polynomial
exponent gap; the dense rows retain only $\O(1)$ per attempt.

Contract 4 makes the capped execution finite on every tape.
The coupling event gives progress and the work bound; the terminal extraction
contract guarantees correctness of every released answer.

Run the terminal phase to its full endpoint.
Choose the polynomial-range lower reserve only after $C_T$ is fixed:
\[
 \mu_{\rm low}=e^{-C_\mu C_T-1}\mu_{\rm end},
\]
where $C_\mu$ bounds a full phase's logarithmic decrement.
A last phase begins above $\mu_{\rm end}$ and remains above $\mu_{\rm low}$,
even if its endpoint lies well below $\mu_{\rm end}/2$.
The fixed-factor extension preserves all polynomial ranges.
At a successful endpoint, $\mu\le\mu_{\rm end}$ is the only terminal-scale
condition required by the stated extraction contract.
\end{proof}

\section{Choosing constants and finite precision}
\label{sec:ram27-parameter-hierarchy}

Choose margins and physical ranges first, confidence and arithmetic grids
second, and sharp work caps last.
We check this order, including the growing powers on occupied classes and the
cache grid reused each frame.

Write $N=2+n$.
The input magnitude exponent and the requested final failure exponent $c_0$ are
fixed constants.
In this section a polynomial range means $[N^{-A},N^A]$ for a fixed $A$; its
exponent may depend on the fixed inputs and algorithm, but not on $n$ or $P$.
Bit bounds describe stored operands rather than unused high bits of the machine
word.

\paragraph{Finite arithmetic convention.}\label{par:ram30-finite-arithmetic}
Fix polynomial operand and address ranges, common rounding grids, and iteration
and operation caps in the order below.
Fixed-depth rational expressions use a constant number of words.
Before each operation, check its domain and range without overflowing; a failed
guard or cap reports failure under that routine's prescribed rule.
These checks enforce finite representation on every tape, including after a
hidden primitive error.
Accuracy and work estimates are coupled to the reference only until its first
primitive failure.
Failure probabilities are allocated at valid call starts or epoch births as
specified in \Cref{sec:ram-phases}, without conditioning on eventual success.

\subsection{Scalar enclosures and parameter order}

\begin{lemma}
\label{lem:ram-stream-scalar}
For every fixed precision exponent $D$, a one-time $N^{1/8}n^{o(1)}$
preprocessing permits certified enclosures of logarithms of positive
polynomial-range rationals to absolute error $N^{-D}$ in $\O(1)$ word operations
per query.
The constants in the polynomial ranges and in $D$ are fixed first.
\end{lemma} \begin{proof}
Put $v=\max\{1,\lfloor \log n/8\rfloor\}$ and tabulate certified values of
$\log(1+j2^{-v})$, $0\le j\le2^v$, and $\log2$ on a common inverse-polynomial
grid.
On $[1,2]$ the expansion
\[
 \log x=2\sum_{i\ge0}\frac{((x-1)/(x+1))^{2i+1}}{2i+1}
\]
has ratio at most $1/9$.
Its explicit geometric tail and outward rounding give each table entry in
$n^{o(1)}$ work.
Reserve enough fixed powers of $N$ in the table accuracy for later arithmetic.

For a query, binary search among bit positions finds $t$ with
$z=2^{-t}x\in[1,2)$ in $\O(1)$ operations.
Exact quotient selects $j=\lfloor2^v(z-1)\rfloor$.
With $z_j=1+j2^{-v}$, write $z=z_j(1+u)$, where $0\le u\le2^{-v}$, and use
\[
 \log x=t\log2+\log z_j+
       \sum_{i=1}^{k}(-1)^{i+1}u^i/i.
\]
A fixed $k$, chosen after $D$, controls the alternating tail.
Outward rounding uses one grid and a fixed number of words.
For bounded $N$, use the original series.
Including interval endpoints and range reduction, the query costs $\O(1)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}
\label{lem:ram27-parameter-order}
Assume the valid-state inequalities, stopped expected-work bounds,
and finite sparse-inverse guarantees established by the preceding
constructions.  The constants and grids in their implementation can
be fixed in an acyclic order, with $P=O(\log\log n)$ and $P\ge512$, so that:
\begin{enumerate}
\item every ordinary stored operand uses $O(\log N)$ bits, and
all ordinary addresses and deadline keys have fixed polynomial ranges;
\item the direction and point-storage defects, source/product errors,
and clock debts have any prescribed inverse-polynomial total reserve;
\item the exact stream sampler has polynomial memory and its stated
unconditional expected cost, even though its reference running time
is not bounded on every random tape;
\item the dense work multipliers are fixed polynomials in $(P,\log\log n)$,
and the displayed sparse rows retain fixed exponent gaps below two.
\end{enumerate}
Empty headers must be skipped before scalar power evaluation, and
all invalid-state arithmetic must be guarded as specified below.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
First fix $p=512$, $\varepsilon,\kappa$, local response reserves, $\gamma,a_{\rm
jump},c_{\rm sol},c_Z,\eta,c_{\rm cen}$, and the sampling lower bounds from the
scalar and drift inequalities, without using an outer inverse-margin cap.
After the potential allowances below, choose dyadic $M,h,\gamma,\widehat B_*$
and the initial/terminal $\mu$ scales, including overshoot, before state or
source precision.
Subsequent paragraphs fix the coarse horizon, confidence, arithmetic grids, word
width, and sharp caps in that order.
Later sampling multipliers are enlarged only where earlier estimates hold
uniformly.

\paragraph{Constants and potential allowances.}
The tracking drift has equilibrium $C_Rm$, and the central drift has equilibrium
$C_ZPm$, with $C_R,C_Z$ independent of the outer potential allowances.
Choose the inner allowances to dominate these constants using only $P\ge512$,
then choose $C_T$ for the two contraction factors, and then the outer allowances
for the frozen-exit estimate.
The constant $C_*$ in
\[
 B_\nu=C_*P\widehat B_*
        \sqrt{1+\beta_f^2/(\vartheta\widehat B_*)}
\]
may now depend on the outer central allowance.
Increasing it lowers the curvature-variance bound without increasing the drift
error.
Its work multiplier is fixed before the sharp caps.
The same monotonicity permits choosing the final confidence multiplier $K$ after
the horizon and killed tracking linear-term bound: the local arguments hold for
every $K$ above their original threshold, so the outer allowances need not
change.

The outer cap gives $\delta_{\min}\ge cN^{-2/p}$ and $d_{Z,\min}\ge cN^{-2/p}$,
uniformly for $P\ge p$.
Consequently
\[
 h/\delta_{\min}\le Cc_hN^{-1/p}P^{-16},\qquad
 h/\rho_{\min}\le Cc_hN^{-1/p}P^{-7}.
\]
These inequalities permit one fixed sufficiently small $c_h$ after the outer
constants have been fixed.
Since a phase has $T=O(\sqrt r/\beta_s)$ frames, decreasing $c_h$ changes phase
length and primitive counts by constants and leaves $TB_*=O_\gamma(C_Tm)$
unchanged.
The terminal lower scale is a fixed factor $\exp(-C_\mu C_T-1)$ times $\mu_{\rm
end}$.
It remains inverse polynomial.

\paragraph{Physical bounds precede accuracy.}
\Cref{sec:ram30-polynomial-ranges}, with $\mu_{\rm low}\le\mu\le\mu_0$, gives
polynomial cost-slack, capacity-margin, grounded-potential, and condition-number
bounds on the expanded valid range.
The three owner coefficients and physical coefficients are fixed-depth positive
expressions in those quantities, $w\in[3s,C]$, and
$k\in[1-\varepsilon,3+\varepsilon]$.
Hence their inverse norms, directions, and required derivatives are bounded
before any mesh is chosen.
Contractive refinement and the substochastic triangular-factor bound use one
absolute grid for all $O(\log n)$ sparse inverse iterations.

Choose a sufficiently large fixed coarse admission exponent $A_N$.
Its operational stop bounds the records, lists, and headers polynomially.
The vector and coefficient caps also bound public source and mark position
counts by a polynomial, with exponent $A_{\rm pos}$.
For a coarse frame/attempt exponent $A_T$, full enumeration has a fixed exponent
$A_{\rm call}>A_N+A_{\rm pos}+A_T+10$.
This gives a combinatorial call horizon before bounding random sampling work.
For the same-frame matrix signed correction bounded by $N^{A_E}$, assigning
failure at most $N^{-c_0-A_E-A_{\rm call}-10}$ makes its entire sum negligible.
Enlarge the earlier concentration multipliers to achieve this exponent.
Apply the call-start and epoch-birth convention above.
Later observation failures kill nonnegative candidates and need no signed
correction.

\paragraph{Growing central powers.}\label{par:ram30-central-powers}
Put $B=CP^2m$ for the expanded central allowance.
For every occupied central header its representative comparisons give
$d_Z^{-P}\le CB$.
For every fixed derivative order $j$,
\[
 |\psi_P^{(j)}(b)|
 \le 2(P+j)^j(CB)^{1+j/P}
 \le \O(N^{2+2j/p}).
\]
For every fixed $0\le j\le p$, power means also give
\[
 \sum_e d_{Z,e}^{-j}
       \le m(CP^2)^{j/P}=O_j(m).
\]
The constants are uniform in $P\ge512$ because $P^{2j/P}$ is bounded.
No step calls for the moment order $j=P$ in a fixed-order density or
weighted-price estimate.

An unused center may have $d_Z=N^{-a}$ and potential $N^{aP}$.
Test multiplicity first: zero-mass headers skip powers, derivatives,
breakpoints, and reciprocals; occupied headers use the finite arithmetic guards.
Valid-range inputs never trigger them.

For an occupied header, first enclose the inverse bases $(1-b)^{-1}$ and
$(1+b)^{-1}$, and then use rounded repeated squaring on those bases.
Only exponents up to $2(P+j)$ are needed.
Its exact positive intermediates lie between $2^{-2(P+j)}$ and a fixed
polynomial in $N$.
Since $P=O(\log\log N)$, the reciprocal lower bound is a fixed power of $\log n$.
Relative enclosure error $u$ per operation accumulates to at most $\exp(CPu)-1$.
One absolute grid with $u\le N^{-D}/(CP)$, refined by a fixed polynomial factor,
therefore gives the required absolute accuracy.
This avoids the artificial $N^{C\log P}$ loss from separate Lipschitz bounds at
each squaring.
At a nonzero center, $|\psi_P'(b)|\ge2P(P+1)|\bb|$ and its dyadic description has
a polynomial nonzero gap; thus relative $G^c,J^c$ enclosures are also available.
No exact denominator of length $P \log n$ is stored.

\paragraph{A fixed-depth precision ladder.}
If the direction perturbation bounds are $N^{A_E}$ over at most $N^{A_T}$
frames, take $\Delta_D\le N^{-A_E-A_T-c_0-20}$, also refining it for the stated
minimum central radius and source norm.
This uses only physical magnitudes.
Next choose the point/state mesh so its polynomial sensitivity times its mesh is
at most a fixed small multiple of $\Delta_D$, and so its accumulated residual
and potential error fits the numerical reserve.
The shifted weight storage $\llambda=s+q_{\rm st}\Delta_{\rm state}$ preserves the floor
exactly.
Store $\mu$ on a mesh much smaller than $\mu_{\rm low}\beta_s/\sqrt r$ and use
the actual ratio $\theta=\mu'/\mu$.
No product of old ratio denominators is retained.

The logical state bit bound $W_s=A_s\log n$ is now fixed.
Coefficients and old--new differences are fixed-depth rational expressions, so
every nonzero $\llambda|\bb|$ is at least $2^{-C W_s}$.
Use this separation to choose the upward normalizer grid.
The central rate uses \Cref{lem:ram-algebraic-rate-enclosure}: its exact zero
test and outward rational enclosure are chosen after these logical operand bits,
without altering the analytical noise.
Neither enclosure changes the direction grid or requires resolving state
cancellations below this separation.
The exact-zero branch and fixed $d_c\ge\Delta_D$ absorb point rounding in
$|b^{\rm sel}|\le Cd_c/p$.
The ordinary metric remains exact.

Source and packet grids follow this separation bound and the public position
counts.
Source Richardson iterations reuse one absolute grid because their error
convolution is contractive.
The observer's relative filter evaluation has only $k=16$ layers.
First fix its positive dyadic action-error tolerance $\nu\le c_{\rm
num}d_0^{-1/4}$, also below the ordinary numerical reserve.
Since $R_0\ge1$, this gives $\nu^2\le C R_0^2/\sqrt{d_0}$; the choice uses only
the already fixed polynomial-range filter parameter $d_0$.
At each layer the previous product grid provides a nonzero input lower bound;
choose the next grid from it and the fixed spectral bounds.
A recursion $A_{j+1}\le C A_j+C'$ for $16$ stages has a fixed final exponent.
Such a recursion over $\Theta(P)$ or $\Theta(\log n)$ layers would not justify the
same claim and is not used here.

Choose the signed clock mesh $\Delta_C$ after the horizon so that
\[
 2T_{\max}a_{\max}\Delta_C\le\rho_{\min}/1000,
 \qquad
 CmT_{\max}a_{\max}\Delta_C/\rho_{\min}\le N^{-c_0}.
\]
Round signed vertex increments once to this mesh and the common positive
increment upward.
Every debt translation is then exact integer arithmetic.
With magnitude bound $N^{A_C}$ and mesh $N^{-D_C}$, a shifted key needs at most
$b= (A_C+D_C+2)\log_2N $ bits.
Fix that key width first.
A reserved deadline block has size $2^{O(b)}$; polynomially many fresh instances
still require only polynomial addresses.
The arithmetic word may be enlarged afterward to hold those addresses.
Its extra bits do not enlarge the deadline universe or change a scalar grid.

\paragraph{Proxy caches on a fixed grid.}
First fix $a$ to bound the physical base and observer coefficients, their
relevant positive eigenvalues, and rate multipliers by $[N^{-a},N^a]$.
The observer coefficient has a fixed positive metadata grid chosen from these
bounds; more accurate fine reads leave that grid unchanged.
Also fix the coarse proxy copy-count guard $N^q$ from the sparse count bounds,
before proxy precision.
On a valid old proxy, spectral comparison with the current base gives
$c_\alpha\le N^a$ and $\norm{T_{\rm old}}\le N^a$.
Thus for every assignment of the atom signs,
\[
 \norm{\xxrand_j}_2\le N^{q+a+1},\qquad
 \norm{T_{\rm old}\xxrand_j}_\infty\le N^{q+2a+2}.
\]
Use the coordinate guard $N^{H_c}$, where $H_c=q_{\rm exp}+2a+4$, with the numerical
reserve.
Choose the observer score floors $\gamma,\gamma_Q$ from the physical bounds in
\eqref{eq:ram27-base-field-rounding}--\eqref{eq:ram27-base-envelope-mass}, with
$N^{-d_c}\le\gamma,\gamma_Q\le1$ for a fixed $d_c$.
Every non-aborted score evaluation then satisfies
\[
 N^{-d_c}\le\mathcal A_e,z_v\le N^{K_c},
 \qquad K_c=a+2H_c+6.
\]
The field guard and positive score floor enforce this representation bound even
on a bad spectral tape.

The cache, residual-$M$, and residual-$Q$ packet weights are respectively
\[
 \frac{\mu_e}{C\log n\mathcal A_e},\qquad
 \frac{\omega_e}{CR_0\epsilon^{-2}\log n\mathcal A_e},\qquad
 \frac{\omega_e}{CR_0\epsilon^{-2}\log n\mu_ez_v}.
\]
Consequently each positive packet lies in $[N^{-k_c},N^{a+d_c+2}]$ for
$k_c=2a+K_c+2$, before its output grid is chosen.
The lower bound uses the score \emph{upper} bound, independently of previous
inverse precision or score-floor denominator.
The score floor bounds packets above even at zero empirical distance.
Choose once $\sigma=2^{-g \log_2N }$ with $g_{\rm key}\ge k_c+a+10$, also fine
enough to divide the fixed deterministic core grids.
It preserves positivity and gives the required relative rounding reserve.
Every new cache returns to this same packet mesh.
The cold static cache and the exact core atoms fit these bounds after one fixed
enlargement of $a,q,k_c$.

Only afterward choose the field accuracy, for example coordinate error at most
$N^{-d_c-a-H_c-8}$ for \eqref{eq:ram27-base-field-rounding}, and the sparse
inverse's pivot and scratch grids from that accuracy and input mesh $\sigma$.
The next cache returns scores to $\gamma$ and packets to $\sigma$ without
inheriting the scratch grids.
The $Q_0$ coordinate fields use its explicit current base core and residual
diagonal.
A component with positive residual diagonal has a polynomial grounded eigenvalue
bound from its base edges and one edge to ground; zero-residual components use
their quotient inverse.
Thus the same choice applies with $\gamma_Q$.

In the observer filter, a nonzero layer product on grids $\sigma$ and $\Delta_j$
has $\norm{P_Mz}_{Q^{-1}}\ge\sigma\Delta_j/ \sqrt{\lambda_{\max}(Q)}$.
This determines the next action grid once for each of the fixed $16$ layers.
Each frame restarts this ladder; its output never becomes a cache coefficient.
Public categorical counts are fixed from these guarded magnitudes and envelope
bounds.
They may exceed the coarse copy-count guard: crossing that guard aborts, rather
than changing the position law.
Range or count failures abort without redrawing fields or packets.
Spectral and numerical claims stop at the first primitive failure;
representation bounds hold on every executing tape.
Expectations remain unconditional on eventual guard success.

\paragraph{Exact stream inputs and endpoint precision.}
After representing all actual rational probabilities, fix $A$ to bound their
numerator and denominator bit lengths by $A\log n$, their list lengths by $N^A$, and
the total number of calls by $N^A$.
The exact sampler of \Cref{sec:ram-exact-streams} then has polynomial memory and
uniform conditional expected cost.
For the finite endpoint sampler, choose $B>2A+c_0+20$ and $D>3A+c_0+20$ in its
prefix length $B\log n$ and mesh $2^{-D\log n}$.
Its total coupling failure is inverse polynomial.
Choose scalar-table accuracy from the required numerical reserves and then fix
the final arithmetic word.
This does not change the stream inputs or their exponent $A$.
Only the capped execution needs an actual-work counter; the exact reference may
continue on its original tape with bounded memory.

\paragraph{Final caps and work exponents.}
Use \Cref{sec:ram-phases}'s prospective token guard after its coarse-stop
expectation and before copying.
Its sharp cap is eventually below the coarse cap and, for $T_0\asymp
m/\log^{1024} n$, gives pathwise depth $\O(1)$.
Include copying, metadata, sources, and exact sampling in the complete
expectation before choosing the actual-work cap.
The coarse horizon bounds call starts; sampling work uses the uniform
conditional expectation proved above.

Every explicit growing-$P$ factor is fixed degree: $h^{-1}$ contributes
$P^{16}$, the central floor contributes $P^{12}$, the normalizer contributes
$P$, the central potential allowance contributes $P^2$, the central radius has
inverse at most $CP^9d_Z^{-1}$, and the center count is $\O(\log n)$.
Products of their fixed number of uses remain fixed polynomials.
In particular the principal sparse exponents are
\[
 \frac{1001}{512}<2,\qquad
 \frac{1011}{512}<2,\qquad
 \frac{15}{8}+o(1)<2.
\]
Any fixed powers of $\log n,P$ can be absorbed in these strict gaps.
The dense row retains only $\O(1)$, and $O(\log n)$ total attempts introduce
its one explicit factor $\log n$.
All constants can be increased to cover the finitely many inputs below the fixed
asymptotic cutoff, using the separately specified finite exact fallback for
bounded input size.
This completes the parameter-order implication.
\end{proof}

\section{The complete algorithm}
\label{sec:ram27-joint}

The following state and execution rules satisfy the four phase contracts;
\Cref{sec:ram27-contract-closure} verifies them.

\subsection{Persistent state and the three coefficient owners}

Store original-label arrays $\ff,\llambda,\cc,\vvu^{\max}$, vertex potential $\yy$, and
common parameter $\mu$, with $\ff,\yy,\mu$ on fixed common grids and
$\llambda=s+q\Delta_{\rm state}$ with an integer $q\ge0$.
Unselected labels retain their stored $\ff,\llambda$, so the structural metrics
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ram27-three-owners}
 \vvr_e^2=\frac{\zz_e^2}{\ww_e^2},\qquad
 \frac{\zz_e^2}{\ww_e},\qquad
 \frac{\vvr_e^2}{\llambda_e}
\end{equation}
change only on the selected list.
Their fixed-factor dyadic envelopes support, respectively, the ordinary inverse,
physical solve, and observer sampling.
The latter two graphs are maintained bases: the graph with coefficient $\zz^2/w$
is spectrally comparable to $\mu\LL$, while the observer's margin-dependent
coefficient $\omega$ remains metadata.
Thus changes to $\yy,\mu$ and reference metadata need no dense structural update.

Use the static construction and original-label pruner of
\Cref{struct27:thm:main} in each simple layer and amplitude bucket.
The hybrid counter of \Cref{sec:ram27-owner} uses $\tau_0\asymp n^{9/8}$,
$R\asymp n^{1/16}$, $T_0\asymp m/\log^{1024} n$.
A core's current cut certificate is in its immutable actual birth-degree
reference.
Its original labels occur in compact live arrays with direct handles; private
deleted ghosts are absent from scalar and sampling arrays.

Use a stored field coordinate as each fixed-reference weighted median, shared at
both endpoints.
The energy bound \eqref{eq:ram27-owner-gate} holds for every field, including
constant and past-dependent fields.
Start under the coarse polynomial token stop, charging lower-capacity records at
their full worst deficit price; impose the sharper cap below.

Use the joint lists, exact-count guards, and saved cache contributions of
\Cref{sec:ram30-joint-classes}, further refined by coefficient bucket and
required public precision bin.
Their $n^{o(1)}$ headers per core vertex belong to field preparation; touched
labels update only a fixed number of handles.
The physical metric needs the narrow actual-$\llambda$ bin to control $w^{-1/2}$
in addition to $\zz^2/w$.
\Cref{lem:ram-class-feedback} handles $\yy,\mu$ changes without member scans.
Changing header waterfills or feedback causes no label migration.

\subsection{One physical frame in execution order}
\label{sec:ram30-frame-order}

Begin with all previous requests served and tracking cells, central windows,
coefficients, lists, and owners valid.
Execute these steps in order.

\begin{enumerate}
\item \emph{Freeze the inputs.}\label{item:ram30-freeze}
Form the class masses and potential upper certificates.
Compute the rational represented source $\FF$ and class feedback
$\pphi$, including its lower-wall guard.  Choose the next stored
$\mu'$ on its fixed grid so that its actual ratio
$\theta=\mu'/\mu$ has the required decrease.  Freeze all source
branches, actual coefficients, and old read and deletion prices.

\item \emph{Prepare the sparse operators.}\label{item:ram30-operators}
Use the physical-base owner to construct the sparse physical proxy, sampled
right-hand side, and direction $\xxdir$ of \Cref{lem:ram27-physical-solve}.
Prepare or retain a fixed inverse of the old ordinary graph with the scaling
required for the source iterations.
Construct the base top-list observer filter using fresh proxy tapes.
This filter is fixed before the physical mask.

\item \emph{Fix the original-label rates.}\label{item:ram30-rates}
Use the fixed lazy direction $\UUps_c$ of \Cref{sec:ram-finite-direction}, evaluated
from the old data.
Use $\dd_c=|\UUps_c|+|\pphi|$ and the corresponding $\nnu_c$ in every physical,
quadratic, wall, and curvature rate.
Use certified rational upward enclosures as the actual component probabilities.
For the central rate, \Cref{lem:ram-algebraic-rate-enclosure} supplies the
exact-zero branch and a relative enclosure with no numerical background rate.
For the tracking wall, the literal target is $C_\delta \dd_c/(a_{\rm
jump}\ddeltabar)$; the certified comparison $\ddeltabar\le C_\delta\delta$ makes
it dominate $\dd_c/(a_{\rm jump}\delta)$ without reading the true current score.
The represented class source fixes the source-cap set.
All target probabilities depend only on this frozen data.

\item \emph{Draw one product mask on original labels.}\label{item:ram30-mask}
Homogeneous Bernoulli streams use \Cref{thm:ram-exact-streams}.
Thin endpoint unions only on each original label's first proposal, using the
actual incidence-union denominator.
Mark a label processed even when its thinning coin rejects it.
Distinct labels use independent coins.
Evaluate the full union probability $p_e$ on every selected label and use the
actual inverse probability $1/p_e$.

\item \emph{Test the candidate.}\label{item:ram30-test}
Compute and buffer the selected finite candidate points.
Unselected points are literal no-ops.
Form the exact candidate residual from the maintained $n$-entry residual vector
and the selected stored endpoint changes, using
\Cref{lem:ram27-candidate-tests}.
Its norm test applies the retained old physical inverse with a fixed gap that
reserves the new-metric comparison.
Form the upward common-grid source normalizer $\Gamma$ of \eqref{eq:ram27-gamma}
from actual rational coefficient changes.
A failed residual or normalizer test aborts the entire attempt.

\item \emph{Finish the old-graph work.}\label{item:ram30-old-work}
With the old graph and its owner still installed, construct all conserved change
sources, perform their original-product Richardson iterations, and complete the
observer filter evaluations and old-view clock increments.
Generate residual marks with their public categorical-position count.
Finish the signed central-clock increments too.
Selected labels, positive-account requests, residual marks, and central
deadlines are buffered and coalesced by original label.
Use the operators from Step~\ref{item:ram30-operators}.

\item \emph{Commit the batch.}\label{item:ram30-commit}
After the prospective ledger and token checks below pass, commit the actual
selected $\ff,\llambda$ and the common $\yy,\mu$.
Detach the whole genuine coefficient-change batch at its frozen old prices
before pruning, retirement, or carries.
Pruner exports and carries move live ownership and translate their stored debts
and due flags; they are not new external deletion sources.
Update actual-$\llambda$ bins before preparing final-graph fine fields.
No new physical query occurs during this commit.

\item \emph{Refresh the observations.}\label{item:ram30-refresh}
Read each queued fine label once from the shared final fields at its old frozen
margin price.
Close its old public observation epoch before replacing its reference metadata
and starting new accounts.
Serve central-only requests without a fine score read.
Install all final joint classes and guards and evaluate the returned state's
potential upper certificates.
Check phase/attempt caps before another frame begins.
\end{enumerate}

Deduplicate by generation stamps, then service each coalesced list in
original-label order; fix source-packet order before signs and pads.
For IDs in $\{0,\ldots,m-1\}$, $m=O(n^2)$, a fixed number of stable radix passes
with power-of-two radix in $[n,2n]$ costs $O(\zz+n)$ on $\zz$ labels.
The fixed number of lists charges $O(\zz)$ to enumerated labels and $O(n)$ array
initialization to sparse frame preparation.
Thus private proposal order does not control public commit order.

An abort discards the pending transaction, attempt-local caches, and tapes, then
restores the last clean checkpoint; a retained frame never rewinds a consumed
random cursor.

\subsection{Finite arithmetic}

Use the fixed direction mesh, bounded-denominator enclosures, and
coefficient-separation and clock grids of \Cref{sec:ram27-parameter-hierarchy}.
Paired packets share one rounded scalar for exact conservation.
Round signed fields before clock increments, positive clocks upward, and
transfer debts exactly in stored units with upward credit for unit conversion.
The parameter-order proof bounds total clock error and avoids cross-label
denominator products.

\subsection{Work ledger and operational caps}

Before each mask freeze $R_e=1+W_e+\bar\delta_{e,\rm old}^{-3}$, the price used
for raw proposals and selected updates.
\Cref{lem:ram27-weighted-rates} and the sparse-component bounds in
\Cref{sec:ram27-owner} cover every rate in Step~\ref{item:ram30-rates}, using
its implemented direction envelope.

After \Cref{prop:ram27-contract-closure} bounds the genuine old-price ledger $Z$
under the coarse stop, set $N_{\rm sharp}=\O(m)$.
Before each structural commit, test the cumulative certificate $N_{\rm
init}+81Z_{\rm pros}\le N_{\rm sharp}$ in the owner's normalized prices,
retaining a per-token insertion guard.
This uses the enumerated old-price batch before its exports are known and bounds
historical copy depth by $\O(1)$.

Central inspections, joint-list moves, feedback cache changes, and signed-index
operations cost $\O(1)$ per touched label.
An occurrence move unable to preserve its signed debts queues a cheap central
inspection, without a fine score read.
Header enumeration is sparse field preparation.

\Cref{prop:ram27-contract-closure} bounds stopped-reference expected work, and
\Cref{sec:ram27-explicit-ledger} gives its explicit dense factor.
Choose the actual work cap afterward as in \Cref{sec:ram-phases}; exhaustion
aborts the whole attempt, without redrawing an individual mask, source, or
proxy.

\subsection{Clean starts, amplification, and exact output}

Initialize once by \Cref{sec:ram27-endpoints}.
At each later clean checkpoint perform the observer's full public refresh using
current packed fields before zeroing debts, and save the physical arrays and
certified references.
A retry literally restores the saved checkpoint state and rebuilds owners and
caches as specified in the proof of \Cref{thm:ram27-clean-observer}.

Run complete clean phases under the total attempt cap of
\Cref{thm:ram-phase-composition}.
When a successful phase ends with $\mu\le\mu_{\rm end}$, perform the one-time
exact extraction of \Cref{sec:ram27-endpoints}.
The common-shift dual or hub cut certifies the original answer; matching returns
original edges and an original-cost integer dual.
External exact flow is used only in sparse structural subproblems.

\section{Why the phase theorem applies}
\label{sec:ram27-contract-closure}

We verify the four contracts of \Cref{sec:ram-phases} for the algorithm in
\Cref{sec:ram27-joint}, starting with its state invariant.

\begin{lemma}
\label{prop:ram27-contract-closure}
Fix the input exponent and a polynomial confidence exponent.
With the constants and grids ordered as in \Cref{lem:ram27-parameter-order}, the
implementation in \Cref{sec:ram27-joint} supplies all four contracts of
\Cref{sec:ram-phases}.
There is a polynomial $Q_{\rm led}(P,\log\log n)$ of absolute degree, independent of the
input-range and confidence exponents (which may change its multiplicative
constant), such that one clean attempt, before its actual-work cap and stopped
as specified there, has expected work at most
\[
 C mQ_{\rm led}(P,\log\log n)+n^{15/8+o(1)}
                         +n^{1011/512+o(1)}.
\]
The initialization and terminal procedures of \Cref{sec:ram27-endpoints} supply
the remaining endpoint premises of \Cref{thm:ram-phase-composition}.
\end{lemma} \begin{proof} Work on the reference stopped at its first primitive
failure, before imposing the actual-work cap, without conditioning on eventual
success.

\paragraph{State invariant and clean start.}
At each returned state, $\ff,\llambda,\yy,\mu$ satisfy both tubes and their exact
incidence residual meets the physical-metric bound.
Tracking cells and central representatives are valid, all preceding selected
labels are serviced, and no request or due flag is pending.
Each metric has one live owner per label, no public ghosts, and immutable
birth-reference core certificates.
Clocks retain debt exactly.
The preceding-frame sparse base cache is constant-comparable to the current base
by the one-frame coefficient bound.

The cold midpoint, deterministic core upper scores, contraction scans, and
final-graph reference scan establish this invariant.
Warm checkpoints refresh references before zeroing accounts.

\paragraph{Constructing a frame.}
The owner energy bound \eqref{eq:ram27-owner-gate} applies to every field,
including past-dependent fields.
\Cref{lem:ram-class-feedback} constructs tracking feedback, and the waterfill in
\Cref{sec:ram-scalar-metadata} constructs central sources, without member scans.
Center counts and outward enclosures certify potentials, skipping empty headers
as prescribed.

\Cref{lem:ram27-upper-scores} supplies the leverage bounds for the true-metric
direction of \Cref{lem:ram27-physical-solve}.
Step~\ref{item:ram30-operators} fixes the filter and old inverse before the
mask, without fine reads: physical upper scores use birth references and
observer coefficients use metadata.
The lazy direction is therefore fixed on every label.

The exact-stream multiplier covers all endpoint and direct occurrences.
Thus Steps~\ref{item:ram30-rates}--\ref{item:ram30-test} use independent
original-label bits and the actual union denominator, meeting the scalar
identities and \Cref{thm:ram-tracking,thm:ram-central}.
Candidate tests use the rounded point and abort without local retry.

\paragraph{Restoring valid observations.}
Step~\ref{item:ram30-old-work} supplies the raw-moment premises: source products
and capture use the old matrix and actual coefficient changes, with the filter
fixed before those changes are sampled.

An unrequested label is unselected, so $\vvr_e,\llambda_e$, its epoch price, and
threshold stay fixed.
Positive-account ceilings, residual no-hit bounds, capture, and inverse order
protect its window up to first exit.
The one-frame margin bound protects requested labels until their final shared
reads, using the same old certified references.

The tracking tube $|\log(\llambda/(s+\ttau))|<\kappa$ supplies both comparisons
required by the observer: $\ttau\le C\llambda$ and $\llambda\le C(s+\ttau)$.
Its reference $\vvpbar$ stays comparable to $s+\ttau$ throughout the epoch.
On the same-frame safety event the actual candidate has $\ttau'_e\le C\llambda'_e$
and $\llambda'_e\le C(s+\ttau'_e)$, and its true tracking margin is comparable to
the old one.
Hence the packed read's additive error $c_\star\ddeltabar\llambda$ is a relative error
$O(c_\star\ddeltabar)$ in $s+\ttau$.
Choosing $c_\star$ within the tracking-cell reserve gives the required logarithmic
accuracy.

The updated independent actual-$\llambda$ index supplies the final-graph fine
proxy and lanes before joint tracking classes change.
Step~\ref{item:ram30-refresh} then uses the simultaneous prefix guarantee for
adaptively requested labels and precisions at their old margin prices.
New interior windows and central-only service restore the invariant.

The commit rule in Step~\ref{item:ram30-commit} retains due flags and signed and
positive debt at frozen old prices; due positive debt uses old credit.
Representation changes pay any needed central inspection, without resetting
tracking epochs or creating genuine deletion sources.

\paragraph{Contract 1: potential certificates.}
The restored windows and occupied-header arithmetic give $\Psi_R\le
U_R\le2\Psi_R$ and $\Psi_Z\le U_Z\le2\Psi_Z$.

\paragraph{Contract 2: clean checkpoints.}
The packed all-label checkpoint read costs $\O(\sum\ddeltabar^{-3})=\O(m)$.
Rebuilding owners/caches and copying physical/reference arrays cost
$\O(m)$.
Retry discards failed mutable state, restores saved arrays, references, and
point enclosures literally, and rebuilds graph/proxy caches with fresh tapes.
The saved refresh, central inspections, and baselines justify zero new debt
without cold lane scans.

\paragraph{Contract 3: expected work before the actual-work cap.}
Under the provisional token stop, \Cref{lem:ram27-current-moments} gives
pathwise moments before any event-count estimate.
\Cref{lem:ram27-weighted-rates} prices all raw proposals, including rejects and
duplicates, at frozen old weight $1+W_e+\ddeltabar_e^{-3}$.
Its curvature estimate cancels $(1+\zeta_*)^{1/2}$ and $(1+\zeta_*)^{-1/2}$
pointwise.
Thus the dense conditional mean is $\O(B_*)$ without a cumulative
curvature assumption; other physical components retain their strict polynomial
reserves.

\Cref{lem:ram-attempt-budgets} gives $\sum J_tB_*\le Cm$ deterministically, so
genuine events cost $\O(m)$ in expectation.
Pathwise pruning gives $N\le C(m+Z)$ for admissions and the old-price genuine
ledger $Z$.
Impose \Cref{prop:ram27-owner-attempt}'s sharp token stop before using
historical depth $\O(1)$.
This pays copies, exports, and debt-preserving migrations; static births cost
$n^{15/8+o(1)}$.
Copying uses the stopped prefix, not a convex function of $\Ex N$.

The selected-rate bound supplies \Cref{thm:ram27-clean-observer}'s selected-read
premise.
Lifetime positive credit and residual intensities pay autonomous reads at
$\O(1)\Ex\sum J_tB_*$, plus selected reads and sparse preparation;
releases may use earlier frames' credit.
\Cref{lem:ram-central-scheduler} pays central reports by signed displacement,
paid visits, and migrations.
Neither kind of report changes structural coefficients or creates genuine
events.

Packed assembly costs $\O(1)$ expected work per selected packet plus sparse
destination setup.
Use the one-uncapped-attempt padding analysis and same-tape exact sampling of
\Cref{sec:ram-phases}, before the actual-work cap and without redraw.
Inverse, field, header, timer-header, and proxy preparations have exponent at
most $1011/512<2$.
These bounds also pay the prospective genuine ledger.
Markov bounds genuine-ledger, sharp-token, and actual-work cap crossings on
their stopped references by small fixed probabilities before a primitive
failure; add the primitive-failure budget afterward.

\paragraph{Contract 4: failure accounting and finite execution.}
Each structural, proxy, solve, source, or fine-field call is fixed before its
independent tapes, permitting the call-horizon union bound.
Residual no-hit bounds cover adaptive futures from valid zero-target epoch
births; union over births conditional on the checkpoint, not intermediate
misses.

The finite arithmetic convention of \Cref{sec:ram27-parameter-hierarchy}
enforces finite execution and first-failure coupling, with ranges fixed before
sharp work caps.
Later failures kill nonnegative drift candidates; only the same-frame tracking
matrix event needs signed correction.
The separate frozen outer-crossing process retains its witness, as required in
the phase proof.

\paragraph{Endpoint hypotheses.}
\Cref{sec:ram27-endpoints} supplies the connected dense strictly feasible
midpoint, inner start, polynomial data and scale ratio, precision bounds, and
terminal feasible-gap premise.
\Cref{lem:ram27-hub}'s hub alternative and the terminal primal/dual checks
preserve and certify the original answer.
\Cref{lem:ram27-endpoint-caps} pays cold construction and $O(m\log n)$ terminal work
once, separately from interior work.
\end{proof}

\section{The explicit bound for dense work}
\label{sec:ram27-explicit-ledger}

We expand \Cref{prop:ram27-contract-closure}'s dense polynomial: weighted event
counts pay copies, and each copy pays scalar and handle work.
Write $p=512$; the exponent $P\ge512$ with $P=O(\log\log n)$ is the integer
fixed in \Cref{sec:ram27-logs}.
All constants may depend on the fixed input-range and confidence exponents.
The powers of $P,\log\log n$ displayed here do not.

\begin{lemma}
\label{prop:ram27-explicit-ledger}
The dense expected work of the stopped clean attempt in
\Cref{prop:ram27-contract-closure} is at most
\[
O\left(mP^{14}(\log\log n)^{19}\right)=\O(m).
\]
The looser polynomial $(P+1)^{16}(\log\log n+1)^{24}$ is therefore an admissible
choice for its $Q_{\rm led}(P,\log\log n)$.
The separately specified sparse rows, one-time cold construction, and one-time
terminal construction are unchanged.
\end{lemma} \begin{proof} \emph{Weighted event counts.} Put
$R_e=1+W_e+\ddeltabar_e^{-3}$, as in the weighted raw-rate lemma.
The current-density calculation and the fixed tracking moment give
\[
 \sum R_e^{12}=O(m(\log\log n)^{132}),\qquad
 \sum_{e,o}\phi_{eo}^{-12}=O(m(\log\log n)^{60}).
\]
For every fixed order $j$ used here, the central potential allowance gives $\sum
d_Z^{-j}\le m(CP^2)^{j/P}=O_j(m)$ uniformly in $P\ge512$.
Thus H\"older gives, explicitly,
\[
 \sum R^4\phi^{-4}d_Z^{-2}=O(m(\log\log n)^{64}),\qquad
 \sum R^2\phi^{-2}d_Z^{-2}=O(m(\log\log n)^{32}),
\]
where the second formula holds for either certified margin $d$.
These are population moments of the current owners, including their low-capacity
records; they are not historical update counts.

In the notation of the weighted raw-rate proof, the two factors in the curvature
bound are
\[
 S_R\le C_\gamma P^2(\log\log n)^{32}(m/s)(1+\zeta_*),\qquad
 B_\nu/V\le C_\gamma P(1+\zeta_*)^{-1/2}.
\]
Pairing $\sqrt{S_R}$ with $B_\nu/V$ cancels $\zeta_*$.
Since $k_P=CP^{12}$, the resulting curvature price is at most
$CP^{14}(\log\log n)^{16}B_*$.
The tracking-wall, central-wall, and forced source-wall rows are respectively
bounded by $C(\log\log n)^{16}B_*$, $CP^{12}(\log\log n)^{16}B_*$, and $CP^2(\log\log n)^{11}B_*$.
These prices cover rejected and duplicate raw hits, deletions, and fine reads at
their old margins.
As $\sum J_tB_*\le C_\gamma m$ in an attempt, the resulting dense weighted count
is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ram27-explicit-count}
 \Ex\sum_{\rm raw}R_e
                   =O(mP^{14}(\log\log n)^{16}),
\end{equation}
apart from the already separated sparse physical components.
The $P^{16}$ in the number of frames does not multiply this quantity: it has
canceled against $\beta_f$ in $TB_*$.

\paragraph{Cost of a visited label.}
A changed label updates a fixed number of handles, cache summands, keys, and
accounts.
Signed-key access and exponent comparisons cost $O(\log\log n)$.
Targets are fixed-depth rational or quadratic-field expressions; the separation
bounds of \Cref{sec:ram-finite-direction} and the scalar-update routine of
\Cref{sec:ram-local} give square-root and scalar-root evaluation in $O(\log\log n)$
stages, at most $O(\log\log n)$ work each.
Thus cheap point operations cost $O((\log\log n)^2)$.
The normalized root derivative bound is uniform at capacity walls.
Growing-$P$ powers use $O(\log P)$ rounded multiplications with polynomial
values and precision by \Cref{sec:ram27-parameter-hierarchy}.
Only sparse preparation scans all headers; touched labels use their own handles.

Packed fine reads cost $O((\log\log n)^2\ddeltabar_e^{-3})$ despite their
$\Theta(\log n\ddeltabar_e^{-2})$ confidence lanes.
Source coefficients have $O(\log\log n)$ mantissa bits, fitting the $O((\log\log n)^2)$ square
comparison allowance; their fixed number of packed lane blocks costs $O(\log\log n)$
conditional expected carry work.
Setup and transposition stay in the sparse $nT\log nn^{o(1)}$ row.
Exact gaps cost $O((\log\log n)^2)$ expected work per start and raw success, by
\Cref{sec:ram-exact-streams}.

\paragraph{Copies and central reports.}
Equation~\eqref{eq:ram27-explicit-count} also bounds the expected genuine
deletion ledger $Z$.
Thus the sharp prospective stop may use $N_{\rm
sharp}=C_{C,c_0}mP^{14}(\log\log n)^{16}$ with a sufficiently large fixed multiplier.
The pathwise certificate $N\le N_{\rm init}+81Z$ and the mixed-radix argument
give
\[
 1+\log(1+N_{\rm sharp}/T_0)=O(\log\log n),\qquad
 \Ex N_{\rm copies/moves}=O(mP^{14}(\log\log n)^{17}).
\]
Charging $O((\log\log n)^2)$ for the attached scalar and handle service
therefore costs
\[
O(mP^{14}(\log\log n)^{19}).
\]
The unweighted fixed-birth pruning work is already in $W_e$; there is no further
dynamic-tree factor on that dense row.

The central scheduler's radius satisfies $\rho(\vvsbar)^{-2}\le CP^{18}d_Z^{-2}$.
Its actual endpoint variation is therefore at most $CP^9(\log\log n)^5B_*$, by its
displayed fourth-moment estimate; the common positive-clock contribution is at
most $CP^9B_*$.
Its lifetime inspection count is bounded by
\[
 C\left(m+P^9(\log\log n)^5\sum J_tB_*+N_{\rm paid}+N_{\rm move}\right).
\]
Paid visits and moves enter additively, without the $P^9(\log\log n)^5$ multiplier.
An inspection changes list membership without creating another
occurrence-representation move.
Its $O((\log\log n)^2)$ service is dominated by the copy bound.

\paragraph{Observer and checkpoint rows.}
The autonomous observer supplies conditional new-credit and raw-mark expectation
$O(B_*)$.
Its residual multiplier obeys
\[
 \frac{\log nA_LR_0^3}{\sqrt{d_0}}
       \le n^{-8/p+o(1)}=O(1)
\]
for the observer's fixed sufficiently large logarithmic exponent $D_0$ and the
asymptotic cutoff.
The sharp true-energy trace estimate, not the crude $R_0$ comparison, pays
remaining positive credit.
Autonomous reads therefore cost $O(m(\log\log n)^2)$ per attempt, including initial
zero-credit conventions.
At a checkpoint, $\sum\ddeltabar^{-3}=O(m)$ gives $O(m(\log\log n)^2)$ for complete
packed refresh.
Saving physical/reference arrays costs $O(m)$; restoration and bulk
owner/metadata construction cost $O(m(\log\log n)^2)$, charging only records actually
built.
The cold lane scan remains a separate one-time cost.

\paragraph{Dense ledger.}
The resulting dense ledger is
\[
\begin{array}{@{}ll@{}}
\text{raw physical proposals, selected scalar work and fine reads}
   &O(mP^{14}(\log\log n)^{18}),\\
\text{conserved source preparation and expected packed additions}
   &O(mP^{14}(\log\log n)^{18}),\\
\text{genuine coefficient changes and unweighted pruning}
   &O(mP^{14}(\log\log n)^{16}),\\
\text{copies, migrations, and attached scalar/debt service}
   &O(mP^{14}(\log\log n)^{19}),\\
\text{autonomous central reports}
   &O(mP^9(\log\log n)^7),\\
\text{autonomous observer reads and complete checkpoint service}
   &O(m(\log\log n)^2).
\end{array}
\]
Central inspections paid by a prior visit or move are included in that visit or
move's scalar service.
All charges use their frozen old read prices.
Exact stream work is included in the conditional expected scalar price.
This proves the lemma.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Where the full logarithm enters.}
The terms $n^{15/8+o(1)}$ and $n^{1011/512+o(1)}$ cover the full
header population, solves, proxy/field lanes, timer-header scans, and sparse
exact-flow backend.
Their exponent gaps absorb fixed powers of $\log n$; this does not apply to dense
$m\log^a n$ terms.
Cold initialization costs $O(m\log n\log\log n+m(\log\log n)^3)$ and is paid once, with its own
confidence budget.
Terminal correction, integral rounding, and the exact dual construction have
dense cost $O((m+n)\log n)$ and are also paid once.
Finally, $O(\log n)$ attempts multiply the per-attempt work.
Since $P=O(\log\log n)$, the looser polynomial's two exponents add to $40$, a
conservative bound for all dense rows; in the notation of \Cref{sec:ram27-logs}
every dense row is $\O(m)$ and the total is $\O(m\log n)=\O(n^2\log n)$.

\section{Proof of the main result}
\label{sec:ram27-main-theorem}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \Cref{thm:ram27-main}]
Apply the exact preprocessing and augmentation of \Cref{sec:ram27-endpoints} and
\Cref{lem:ram27-hub}.
They preserve the original answer and give a connected working host with
$N=\Theta(n)$ vertices, $m=\Theta(n^2)$ labels, and $r=\Theta(n)$, represented
in a fixed number of simple layers.
Cold construction starts from its exactly feasible midpoint and gives an inner
checkpoint with zero residual.
The data and $\mu_0/\mu_{\rm end}$ have polynomial ranges; the dense price is
$O(m\log n\log\log n)+mn^{o(1)}$, paid once with polynomial confidence by
\Cref{lem:ram27-endpoint-caps}.

Fix constants and meshes by \Cref{lem:ram27-parameter-order}.
\Cref{prop:ram27-contract-closure} supplies all four phase contracts and the
endpoint premises.
\Cref{thm:ram-phase-composition} therefore gives $O(\log n)$ total attempts, with the
sharp-token, actual-work, and attempt-count caps specified there and separately
budgeted first-primitive failures.

\Cref{prop:ram27-explicit-ledger} permits $Q_{\rm led}(P,\log\log n)=(P+1)^{16}(\log\log n+1)^{24}$.
Since $P=O(\log\log n)$, the interior work is $O(m\log n(\log\log n)^{40})=\O(m\log n)$.
The two sparse rows in the closure lemma remain $o(n^2)$ after multiplication by
$O(\log n)$, by their strict exponent gaps.
The one-time endpoint prices also fit this bound; input range and confidence
affect constants, not the degree $40$.
The bounded-input branch covers the finitely many smaller dimensions.

At the terminal checkpoint, \Cref{sec:ram27-endpoints} supplies exact tree
correction, cost-nonincreasing integral rounding, and an integer dual, or the
verified hub cut when optimal hub flow is positive.
Its dense cost is $O(m\log n+n\log n)$, paid once under \Cref{lem:ram27-endpoint-caps}.

Finally verify the proposed answer on the original input in $O(m+n)$ exact word
operations.
For a flow, verify integrality, both capacity bounds, and all vertex balances.
For the supplied integer potential $\yy$, put $\cc_e^y=\cc_e-(Ay)_e$ and verify
\[
 \ff_e<\vvu_e\ \Longrightarrow\ \cc_e^y\ge0,
 \qquad
 \ff_e>l_e\ \Longrightarrow\ \cc_e^y\le0.
\]
These are exactly zero complementary slackness on every interval $[l_e,\vvu_e]$.
Together with primal feasibility they prove optimality by weak duality.
Self-loops and zero-width intervals satisfy the same direct tests.
Alternatively verify the stated bound, demand, or capacity-cut inconsistency
directly.
Reject any failed check with \textsc{Fail}.
These checks require only polynomial-size integer sums and a fixed number of
words per operand.

Allocate the original confidence exponent with a fixed reserve among cold
initialization, fresh primitives and epoch events, attempt-count exhaustion, and
terminal construction.
Their union has probability at most $n^{-c_0}$.
A global word-operation counter enforces the displayed bound on every tape,
including all malformed intermediate and rare exact-arithmetic branches.
All good executions finish below that cap.
Exact final verification makes every answer actually released correct, also on a
tape outside the good event.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \Cref{cor:ram27-matching}]
Orient every edge left to right, put lower capacity zero and upper capacity two
on each edge, and give each left vertex demand $-1$ and each right vertex demand
$1$.
A feasible integral flow has exactly one unit edge at each vertex, and hence is
a perfect matching.
Every edge of this integral flow is strictly below its upper bound.
The verified flow dual therefore has $\yy_j-\yy_i\le c_{ij}$ on every edge, with
equality on the selected edges.
Set $\alpha_i=-\yy_i$ on the left and $\beta_j=\yy_j$ on the right.
Then $\alpha_i+\beta_j\le c_{ij}$ and equality holds on the output matching,
giving the stated integer assignment dual.
\end{proof}


\bibliographystyle{alpha}
\bibliography{../DenseGraphs}

\appendix
\section{Construction and pruning of original-edge cores}
\label{sec:ram27-core-proof}
We prove \Cref{struct27:thm:main}, using the graph, reference, and label
conventions of the structural interface.

\subsection{A child guard and its fresh sparse sample}

The guard estimates degrees by probes and scans a full original row only when
removing its vertex.
Freeze root incidence arrays with exact positive degrees $w$, volume $W_0=2q_0$,
and minimum degree $d\ge1$.
Each child $A$, given by a vertex list and stamped membership, is fixed before
its fresh tapes; its failure budget is $\delta_c=n^{-O(1)}$.
Public removal excludes a row from the current original vertex set and scans its
root row; private removal changes auxiliary ghost-flow state.
These operations need not coincide.

\begin{lemma}\label{struct27:lem:guard}
For inverse-dyadic $e\le1/4096$, use $k=8 \log_2(8n/\delta_c) /e^2$
independent uniform root-neighbor membership probes at each listed vertex.
Let $\widetilde d_u$ be $w_u$ times the empirical membership fraction.
Repeatedly remove a vertex with $\widetilde d_u<w_u/3$, scan its root row, and
decrement every still-child neighbor's estimate by one.
Except with probability $\delta_c$, on the surviving child $C$ the known
integers
\begin{equation}\label{struct27:eq:guard}
 r_u= \widetilde d_u+3ew_u ,\qquad
 d_C(u)\le r_u\le2d_C(u),\qquad w_u/4\le r_u\le2w_u
\end{equation}
are valid.
Every removed vertex had current true child degree below $3w_u/8$.
Original degree estimates keep the same additive error under all subsequent
exact public row removals.
\end{lemma} \begin{proof} Hoeffding and a row union bound give $|\widetilde
d_u-d_A(u)|\le ew_u$.
Every decrement changes the estimate and truth by the same amount; adaptively
chosen removals do not change this error.
A survivor has $d_C\ge(1/3-e)w>w/4$.
Also $d_C+2ew\le r\le d_C+4ew \le d_C+16ed_C \le2d_C$,
using positive integral $d_C$ and $16e\le1$.
The remaining comparisons follow, and at removal $d_A<(1/3+e)w<3w/8$.
Only listed rows are initialized.
Uniform positions use exact integer rejection; its exponentially decaying extra
work is capped globally.
\end{proof}

An empty guarded child terminates immediately.
Otherwise write $v=|C|$, choose a known $d_*\le\min d_C$ and
$\rho\ge\max\{1,v/d_*\}$, and take a fresh sample after the guard.
For an inverse-dyadic probability choose
\begin{equation}\label{struct27:eq:samplep}
 \min\{1,C_0\log n/(\epsilon^2d_*)\}\le p
 \le\min\{1,2C_0\log n/(\epsilon^2d_*)\}.
\end{equation}
Run Bernoulli streams of rate $p$ on root rows.
Inspect every selected position, retaining only internal edges in their
smaller-endpoint orientation, with weight $1/p$.
Each internal edge thus has one independent eligible position; never search
until an internal edge appears.

The game uses the completed Laplacian comparison.
The later repair uses the raw adjacency bound to control degree error after
adaptive removals.

\begin{lemma}\label{struct27:lem:sample}
For a sufficiently large $C_0$, with the allocated confidence,
\begin{align}
 \norm{D_{d_C}^{-1/2}(\widehat A-A_C)D_{d_C}^{-1/2}}&\le\epsilon/4,
 &|\widehat d_u-d_C(u)|&\le(\epsilon/4)d_C(u).\label{struct27:eq:rawsample}
\end{align}
If $\epsilon\le e/4$, complete both adjacencies to known row sums $r$:
\[
 A^*=A_C+\diag(r-d_C),\quad
 \widehat A^*=\widehat A+\diag(r-\widehat d),\quad
 D=\diag(r),\quad V=r(C),\quad J_r=D-rr^T/V.
\]
Both loop completions are nonnegative, and
\begin{equation}\label{struct27:eq:laperror}
 -\epsilon J_r\preceq\widehat L-L_C\preceq\epsilon J_r,
 \qquad \norm{D^{-1/2}(\widehat A-A_C)D^{-1/2}}\le\epsilon.
\end{equation}
Sample generation costs $O((v+p\,w(C)+\log n)n^{o(1)})$ operations and that many or
fewer original position accesses, including every discarded position and
exhausted stream.
\end{lemma} \begin{proof} Each independent normalized adjacency summand has norm
at most $2/(pd_*)$, and its total variance parameter is $O(1/(pd_*))$.
The dimension in matrix Bernstein is at most $n$.
Scalar Bernstein and a union over rows give the degree assertion.
If $p=1$ both are exact.
The sample degree error is smaller than the guard's positive margin
$r-d_C\ge2ew$, so loops are nonnegative.
Adjacency error plus the degree diagonal gives completed error at most
$\epsilon$ in $D$ norm.
Both Laplacians annihilate constants: centering a test vector in $r$ replaces
$D$ by $J_r$.
A Chernoff bound caps selected positions by $O(pw(C)+\log n)$.
Finite geometric skipping with the same distribution up to a globally budgeted
coupling error is specified in \Cref{struct27:sec:skipping}.
A negative computed loop is reported as failure, rather than used as an edge.
\end{proof}

\subsection{A low-trace filter and sparse smoothing}

The filter approximately preserves low-energy cuts and reduces the initial
potential to $O(\rho)$, controlling the number of game rounds.

Let $P$ subtract the $r$-mean.
Define
\[
 \widehat W=D^{-1}\widehat A^*,\quad c_0=1+\epsilon,
 \quad B_0=(I+\widehat W/c_0)/2.
\]
Set $h=512 \log_2(64n) $, computed by integer bit-length comparisons.
It is a multiple of 16, satisfies $h\ge512\ln(64n)$, and is $O(\log n)$.
Put
\begin{equation}\label{struct27:eq:filter}
 p_h(t)=\sum_{j=13h/16}^h{h\choose j}t^j(1-t)^{h-j},
 \qquad F=Pp_h(B_0)P.
\end{equation}

\begin{lemma}\label{struct27:lem:filter}
For the above guard and sample parameters, $F$ is a centered, $D$-self-adjoint
contraction satisfying
\begin{equation}\label{struct27:eq:filterbounds}
 \norm{F}_{\HS,r}^2\le16\rho+1=:P_{\max},\qquad
 \norm{(I-F)x}_r\le\left(\frac1{16}+\sqrt{8\lambda_C(x)}\right)\norm{x}_r
\end{equation}
for every centered $x$, where $\lambda_C(x)=x^TL_Cx/\norm{x}_r^2$.
One application takes $O(M \log^2 n)$ sparse arithmetic operations at
inverse-polynomial output accuracy, where $M$ counts stored sample atoms and
vertices.
\end{lemma} \begin{proof} The binomial tail is at most $\xi=e^{-h/512}$ for
sampled adjacency eigenvalues at most $1/2$, and at least $1-\xi$ for
eigenvalues at least $3/4$.
Write the true normalized completed adjacency as $U+\diag(\ell)$ with
$U=D^{-1/2}A_CD^{-1/2}$.
Simplicity gives
\[
 \tr(U^2)=\sum_u\sum_{v:uv\in E(C)}\frac1{r_ur_v}
 \le v/d_*,\qquad 0\le\ell_u\le16e+1/d_*.
\]
If $d_*\ge8$, loop norm plus sample perturbation is below $1/4$.
At most $16v/d_*$ sampled eigenvalues can exceed $1/2$, by the trace bound on
true $U$ and eigenvalue monotonicity under a norm perturbation.
If $d_*<8$, dimension $v<8v/d_*$ suffices.
Compression by $P$ cannot increase Hilbert--Schmidt norm, and $v\xi^2<1$ proves
the first claim.
In particular we do not square all the completion loops.

On the centered subspace, let $E_0$ project to true normalized Laplacian
eigenvalues at most $1/8$, and let $Q_0$ project to sampled adjacency
eigenvalues below $3/4$.
True completed adjacency eigenvalues on $E_0$ are at least $7/8$.
The Sylvester equation for $Q_0E_0$ has spectral separation $1/8$.
Its integral solution bounds $\norm{Q_0E_0}\le8\epsilon$.
Thus $\norm{(I-F)E_0}\le\xi+8\epsilon\le1/16$.
The complementary component of $x$ has norm at most
$\sqrt{8\lambda_C(x)}\norm{x}_r$.
This proves the second claim.
De Casteljau evaluation of the Bernstein polynomial uses $O(h^2)$ sparse
products.
The amplification $2^{O(h)}$ is polynomial in $n$, permitting the common
$O(\log n)$-bit grid of \Cref{struct27:sec:finite}.
\end{proof}

To turn sample error into one-sided control of original cuts, join a hub to each
original vertex $u$ with weight $\epsilon r_u$.
Eliminating it gives
\begin{equation}\label{struct27:eq:hub}
 L_+=\widehat L+\epsilon J_r,\qquad
 L_C\preceq L_+\preceq L_C+2\epsilon J_r.
\end{equation}
For any active $A\subseteq C$, let $D_A=\diag(r|_A)$ and eliminate the hub and
inactive originals:
\[
 S_A=\Schur_A(L_+),\quad \gamma=(1+2\epsilon)^{-1},\quad
 K_A=I-\gamma D_A^{-1}S_A,\quad T_A=(K_A+K_A^2)/2.
\]
Route on the explicit sample plus hub; apply Schur operators by sparse solves
without storing them.

\begin{lemma}\label{struct27:lem:smoothing}
$K_A$ is a reversible nonnegative Markov kernel and a contraction.
If $X=T_AZ$, then
\begin{equation}\label{struct27:eq:smoothenergy}
 \tr(X^TS_AX)\le(\norm{Z}_r^2-\norm{X}_r^2)/(3\gamma).
\end{equation}
For every full original set $U\subseteq C$, extending kernels by the identity on
inactive coordinates gives
\begin{equation}\label{struct27:eq:smoothcut}
 \norm{(T_A-I)\oone_U}_{r,1}
 \le3[\partial_C(U)+2\epsilon\min\{r(U),V-r(U)\}].
\end{equation}
Each action uses two grounded solves on a sparse graph of size $O(M)$.
\end{lemma} \begin{proof} Schur signs and zero row sums, with
$(S_A)_{uu}\le(1+2\epsilon)r_u$, prove the Markov assertions.
Its spectrum lies in $[-1,1]$.
Equation~\eqref{struct27:eq:smoothenergy} follows by diagonalization from
$3(1-x)((x+x^2)/2)^2\le1-((x+x^2)/2)^2$ on that interval.
For an indicator, its $K$ displacement is twice $\gamma$ times its Schur cut.
The full original indicator is an admissible extension in the Schur variational
minimum, so this cut is bounded by \eqref{struct27:eq:hub}.
A second $K$ step has at most twice the first displacement by weighted $\ell_1$
contraction.
Averaging gives the factor three.
This argument applies to all original sets, including ones chosen only at the
end of the execution.

Here $L_{XY}$ are blocks of the explicit sample-plus-hub Laplacian.
A $K$ action solves on eliminated vertices $B$, including the hub, with
right-hand side $-L_{BA}f$.
Replace $B$--$A$ edges by ground edges preserving the diagonal and retain their
active values in this right-hand side.
The block is positive definite for nonempty $A$; \Cref{struct27:sec:finite}
supplies certified solves.
\end{proof}

\subsection{One projected candidate and a deficient exact flow}

Represent the embedding by actions on signs, retaining active rows and
original-child columns.
A projected field selects separated source and target demands; flow supplies
endpoint mixing and, when deficient, seed removal, the two loss terms below.
Equation~\eqref{struct27:eq:embedding} records smoothing, mixing, seed deletion,
and recentering.

Put $V_A=r(A)$.
Freeze a centered current embedding $Z$, set $X=T_AZ$, draw fresh Rademacher
column signs $g$, and compute a centered approximation $y$ to $Xg$.
Let $a$ be a weighted median of $y$; change the signs of both $y$ and the
analytical field $Xg$ if necessary so that $a\ge0$.
All projection events and quadratic estimates are invariant under this common
sign change.

The eligible sets are $y<0$ and $y\ge3a/2$, omitting zero coordinates.
At least $2/11$ of squared energy lies in their union: excluded energy is at
most $(9/4)r(A)a^2$, while zero mean and the median force negative energy at
least $r(A)a^2/2$.
Choose an eligible set containing at least $1/11$ of all energy and take its
largest-$|y|$ prefix of reference mass at most $r(A)/8$, splitting its final
vertex if necessary.
This retains at least $1/8$ of that eligible energy.
Use this prefix as source demand $l$, and the opposite median half as target
demand $t$.
Then
\begin{equation}\label{struct27:eq:demands}
 0\le l,t\le r,\quad\supp(l)\cap\supp(t)=\varnothing,
 \quad l(A)\le V_A/8,\quad t(A)=V_A/2,
 \quad\sum l_uy_u^2\ge\norm{y}_r^2/128.
\end{equation}
Every source differs from every target by at least $|y_u|/3$.
Demands have denominators dividing 8.

Write $\partial_+$ for cut weight in the explicit sample-plus-hub graph.
Give augmented routing edges capacity $\beta$ times their weight, where $\beta$
is a dyadic integer, and terminal arcs capacities $l,t$.
Compute an exact maximum flow after common dyadic scaling.
If all sources are saturated, put $U=S=\varnothing$.
Otherwise let $U$ be the residual reachable routing vertices from the
super-source, and $S=U\cap A$.
In the latter case, the minimum cut gives
\begin{equation}\label{struct27:eq:seed}
 \beta\partial_+(U)+t(U)<l(U),\qquad
 r(S)<5V_A/8,\qquad \partial_+(U)<r(S)/\beta.
\end{equation}
Indeed $t(U)\ge r(S)-V_A/2$ and $l(U)\le V_A/8$.

Cancel opposite flow and cycles, and retain source--target records whose source
lies outside $U$.
These sources are saturated and their paths avoid $U$: an entering positive arc
would have a residual reverse arc leaving $U$.
For retained amounts $A_{uv}$ set $R_{uv}=A_{uv}+A_{vu}$ off-diagonal and
complete row sums to $r$ with nonnegative loops, using disjoint demand supports
and \eqref{struct27:eq:demands}.
Then $M_R=(I+D_A^{-1}R)/2$ is reversible and fixes seed rows, and
$E_R(X)=\tr(X^T(D_A-R)X)$ equals routed endpoint squared energy.

\begin{lemma}\label{struct27:lem:candidate}
If $\Delta_X$ is squared-norm loss after $M_R$, deletion of $S$, and recentring,
then
\begin{align}
 \Delta_X&\ge\tfrac12 E_R(X)+\sum_{u\in S}r_u\norm{X_u}^2,\label{struct27:eq:candidateloss}\\
 E_R(y)+\sum_{u\in S}r_uy_u^2&\ge2^{-11}\norm{y}_r^2.\label{struct27:eq:projectedloss}
\end{align}
For every full original $W\subseteq C$,
\begin{equation}\label{struct27:eq:routecut}
 \norm{(M_R-I)\oone_W}_{r,1}
 \le\beta[\partial_C(W)+2\epsilon\min\{r(W),V-r(W)\}].
\end{equation}
The endpoint records can be constructed in $O(M\log(2M))$ work and $O(M)$
records.
\end{lemma} \begin{proof} For a reversible $P_R=D_A^{-1}R$ with eigenvalue
$x\in[-1,1]$, $1-((1+x)/2)^2\ge(1-x)/2$.
Seed rows are unchanged by $M_R$, and centering only reduces norm.
This proves \eqref{struct27:eq:candidateloss}.
Each source is either in $S$, whose energy is paid by deletion, or routed in
full to separated targets.
Thus the left side of \eqref{struct27:eq:projectedloss} is at least $\sum
l_uy_u^2/9\ge\norm{y}_r^2/1152$.
For \eqref{struct27:eq:routecut}, place the hub on the larger-reference side of
$W$.
The resulting augmented cut has weight at most the bracket by
\eqref{struct27:eq:laperror}; every endpoint-crossing route crosses it and has
congestion at most $\beta$.
The lazy displacement equals the endpoint cut weight; the bound uses the
explicit augmented cut, not a Schur cut.

\paragraph{Compressed routes.}
Maintain a forest with at most one selected positive outgoing arc per vertex.
The super-source has only outgoing arcs and the super-sink only incoming arcs.
Extend from the current root, purging zero arcs on both relevant root paths
before root/cycle tests.
Link distinct trees; on a cycle subtract its minimum flow.
At the sink subtract the path bottleneck and record endpoints and amount.

After source flow is exhausted, scan a fixed arc list to cancel circulations.
Query linked values from the dynamic tree.
Process each positive arc's tail until its outgoing flow vanishes, so that arc
has zero flow before the cursor advances.
Flows only decrease: every positive bottleneck zeros an arc, every linked arc is
cut once, and a renewed closing-arc attempt has a new zero-arc charge.
Current outgoing pointers therefore give $O(M)$ dynamic-tree events.
Check initial conservation once; path and cycle subtractions preserve it.
Finish with a sparse zero-residual scan.

For endpoint queries, synchronize two more forests with the same links and cuts.
Give source arcs $s\to u$ tag $u$ in one, sink arcs $w\to t$ tag $w$ in the
other, and all other arcs a larger sentinel.
Each $s$--$t$ path has exactly one tag of each kind.
Its two path minima return the endpoints in $O(\log(2M))$.
Only the flow forest receives path additions.
These are precisely the operations of \cite[Section~2]{ST83:journal}; no
represented path is traversed.
\end{proof}

\subsection{A uniform projection event and finite game chronology}

The flow response may depend on the projected field, so progress must hold for
every valid response to the same signs.
Put $b_\rho= \log_2(2+\rho) $, $\vartheta=2^{-60}/b_\rho$, and
$B_c=64b_\rho$.

\begin{lemma}\label{struct27:lem:uniform}
If $V_A\norm{y-Xg}_\infty^2\le2^{-20}\norm{Z}_r^2$, with probability at least
$1/16$ over fresh signs, every candidate response allowed above loses at least
$\vartheta\norm{Z}_r^2$, including smoothing loss.
\end{lemma} \begin{proof} Unmarked norms of fields and embeddings are
$r$-weighted; norms of individual row vectors are Euclidean.
If smoothing already loses at least $\vartheta\Phi$, where $\Phi=\norm{Z}_r^2$,
the claim holds.
Otherwise use the harmonic extension of $X$ through the fixed augmented graph.
As proof-only randomness, take the first $G=\min\{v, \rho\ln(1/\vartheta) \}$ vertices of a uniform permutation of the original
child.
At each active row, let $c_u$ be the harmonic-extension value at the first bank
vertex adjacent to it in $C$, or zero if none.
For each fixed active row, the probability of being uncovered is at most
$\vartheta$; conditional on coverage, the chosen neighbor is uniform among its
neighbors in $C$.
Since $r_u/d_C(u)\le2$, the harmonic energy bound
\eqref{struct27:eq:smoothenergy} and $\gamma^{-1}<3/2$ give
\[
 \Ex\delta_X\le2(\norm{Z}_r^2-\norm{X}_r^2)+\vartheta\Phi
 \le3\vartheta\Phi,
 \qquad\delta_X=\sum r_u\norm{X_u-c_u}^2.
\]
The factor two counts both orientations of each original edge; original energy
is bounded by augmented harmonic energy.
The bank has at most $2^{4b_\rho}$ centers including zero.
Rademacher tails and a union bound give simultaneously $|\langle
c_\star-c_\star',g\rangle|^2\le B_c\norm{c-c_\star'}^2$ for all pairs, except with probability
below $1/1000$.
Markov gives
\[
 \delta_X\le\Phi/(2^{24}B_c),\qquad
 \delta_g:=\sum r_u\langle X_u-c_u,g\rangle^2\le\Phi/2^{24}
\]
outside total probability below $1/48$, including the pair event: the two Markov
failure bounds are at most $3\vartheta2^{24}B_c$ and $3\vartheta2^{24}$.
Meanwhile $\Ex\norm{Xg}^4\le3(\Ex\norm{Xg}^2)^2$, so Paley--Zygmund gives
$\Pr\{\norm{Xg}^2\ge\norm{X}^2/2\}\ge1/12$.
The intersection has probability greater than $1/16$.

For any response define the positive form $\mathcal
F(Y)=E_R(Y)+\sum_Sr_u\norm{Y_u}^2$; its normalized norm is at most three.
Expand $X=c_\star+(X-c_\star)$ twice.
Pairwise center distortion, the inclusion of zero as a center, and
\eqref{struct27:eq:candidateloss} give, simultaneously for every $R,S$,
\begin{equation}\label{struct27:eq:uniformform}
 \mathcal F(Xg)\le4B_c\mathcal F(X)+12B_c\delta_X+6\delta_g
 \le8B_c\Delta_X+12B_c\delta_X+6\delta_g.
\end{equation}
Writing $y=Xg+e$ and using \eqref{struct27:eq:projectedloss} gives
\[
 \mathcal F(Xg)\ge2^{-13}\norm{Xg}^2-(3+2^{-12})\norm{e}_r^2.
\]
On the displayed events this is more than $2^{-15}\Phi$ before the two
center-error terms; those total at most $18\Phi/2^{24}$.
Thus $\Delta_X\ge\Phi/(2^{20}B_c)>\vartheta\Phi$.
Marginalizing the proof-only bank proves the stated sign probability; the bank
is never constructed and makes no original access.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Committed rounds and validation.}
Fix cut conductance $\chi$ and a volume-loss reserve $S\ge1$.
Set
\begin{align}
 \tau_0&=2^{-12},&
 t&= 16\vartheta^{-1} \log_2(P_{\max}/\tau_0)  +1,
 &\beta&=2^{ \log_2(2/\chi) },\label{struct27:eq:gameparam1}\\
 K&=t(\beta+3),&
 \phi&=2^{- \log_2\max\{2048,16K\} },
 &\eta&=\beta\phi/(128S),\quad \epsilon\le1/(4096K).
 \label{struct27:eq:gameparam2}
\end{align}
Let the diagonal frame be $F_0(u,u)=2^{- \log_4r_u }$, so
$1/(4r_u)\le F_0(u,u)^2\le1/r_u$.
Keep implicitly
\begin{equation}\label{struct27:eq:embedding}
 Z=P_AQFF_0,
\end{equation}
Here $P_A$ subtracts the $r$-weighted mean on active rows, and $Q$ contains
committed smoothers, couplings and deletion masks, retaining all original
columns.
Intermediate centerings may be omitted: Markov factors preserve the active
constant, deletion maps it to the next constant, and final $P_A$ kills it.
Thus \eqref{struct27:eq:embedding} equals centered replay, $Q$ is nonnegative
substochastic, and contraction gives $\Phi\le P_{\max}$.

Candidate signs find progress; independent validation signs measure
it before a commit.  Permit at most $t$ committed rounds and one
additional terminal validation block.  At each of these at most $t+1$
blocks perform the following operations in order.
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*]
\item Freeze the active state and $D_0=C\setminus A$.  Generate
$s=16 \log_2(16(t+1)/\delta_c) $ independent candidates from
this same state.
\item If some seed has $r(D_0\cup S_i)\ge\eta V$, return that
particular union as a cut.  Never accumulate rejected seeds.
\item Otherwise freeze every candidate map.  Independently draw a
shared validation bank in the smallest odd number $J$ of groups of
128 signs satisfying $J>\log_2(16(t+1)(s+1)/\delta_c)+1$.
Median group means estimate
$\Phi$ and each ideal candidate loss $\Delta_i$.
\item Put $a_{\rm stat}=\vartheta\tau_0/16$,
$U=2(\widehat\Phi+a_{\rm stat})$, and
$L_i=\max\{0,(2/3)(\widehat\Delta_i-a_{\rm stat})\}$.
Stop if $U\le4\tau_0$.  Otherwise report failure before any further
commit if $t$ rounds have already been committed.  If fewer have
been committed, select the largest $L_i$, reporting failure if it
is below $\vartheta U/16$, and commit only that candidate.
\end{enumerate}

Frozen candidates are contractions, so each ideal loss is a nonnegative
quadratic form with Rademacher variance at most twice its squared mean.
Chebyshev gives $[1/2,3/2]$ accuracy with failure at most $1/16$ per 128-sign
group, and the median fails with probability below $2^{-J}$.
Including \Cref{struct27:sec:finite}'s arithmetic error gives
\begin{equation}\label{struct27:eq:validation}
 \Phi\le U\le3\Phi+4a_{\rm stat},\qquad
 \Delta_i/3-4a_{\rm stat}/3\le L_i\le\Delta_i.
\end{equation}
