When $\Phi>\tau_0$, all $s$ candidates miss the uniform progress event with
probability at most $e^{-s/16}$.
If a candidate has that progress property, it passes the selection threshold,
and the committed round loses at least $\vartheta\Phi/16$.
The choice of $t$ forces $\Phi\le\tau_0$, where the stop test succeeds.
No relative accuracy is required in this small-potential case.

\begin{lemma}\label{struct27:lem:game}
Except with the allocated failure probability, the game returns either a cut of
true reference conductance at most $\chi$, whose two sides have reference volume
at least $\eta V$, or an active near-expander $A_1$ with
\begin{equation}\label{struct27:eq:near}
 r(A_1)>(1-\eta)V,\quad
 \partial_C(A_1)\le r(C\setminus A_1)/\beta,\quad
 \partial_C(U)\ge\phi r(U)\quad(r(U)\le r(A_1)/2).
\end{equation}
It uses no original row scans.
Its sparse cost is $M^{1+o(1)}$ when
$\chi^{-1},S,\epsilon^{-1},\log(2+\rho)$ are bounded by fixed powers of $\log n$, the
ambient quantities $v,V,\rho$ have the prescribed polynomial range, and
$\log(1/\delta_c)=O(\log n)$.
In particular, $\rho$ itself need not be polylogarithmic.
\end{lemma} \begin{proof} For a failed-flow seed use the full original set
$U\cap C$ in \eqref{struct27:eq:seed}.
Hub elimination and \eqref{struct27:eq:hub} show its true cut is at most the
augmented cut.
Inactive originals already belong to $D_0$.
Submodularity therefore increases the boundary of $D_0\cup S$ by at most
$r(S)/\beta$.
At a triggering union its volume is between $\eta V$ and $(5/8+3\eta/8)V$; the
complement is at least $3(1-\eta)V/8$.
Since $\eta\le1/9$, this complement is at least $\eta V$, and the volume ratio
is at most two.
Conductance is at most $2/\beta\le\chi$.

At potential stopping, put $r_0=r(C\setminus A_1)<\eta V$.
Boundary is at most $r_0/\beta$, hence $b:=\partial_C(A_1)/r(A_1)\le\phi/64$.
For a final set $U\subseteq A_1$, $s_0=r(U)\le r(A_1)/2$, let
$x=\oone_U-(s_0/r(A_1))\oone_{A_1}$.
It is centered on the full child, $\norm{x}_r^2\ge s_0/2$, and
$\lambda_C(x)\le2\partial_C(U)/s_0+b$ (expand its three edge classes).
If the cut ratio is at most $1/2048$, \Cref{struct27:lem:filter} and
$\Phi\le4\tau_0$ give
\[
 \norm{P_{A_1}Qx}_r
 \le(2\sqrt\Phi+1/16+\sqrt{8\lambda_C(x)})\norm{x}_r
 <\norm{x}_r/2.
\]
Here the frame gives $\norm{P_{A_1}QF}_{\HS,r}\le2\sqrt\Phi$.
For this comparison, view $Q$ on all rows of $C$, padding deleted output rows
with zeros.
Since $Q$ is substochastic and $x$ takes values in an interval containing zero
and of length one, $|(Q-I)x|\le1$.
Orthogonal centering on $A_1$ and $P_{A_1}x=x$ imply
$\norm{(Q-I)x}_r\ge\norm{x}_r/2$, hence $\norm{(Q-I)x}_{r,1}\ge s_0/8$.
Every deletion mask fixes this final-support vector.
Suffix telescoping of $Q-I$, followed by weighted $\ell_1$ contraction, tests
the same full original sets $U,A_1$ in every earlier factor.
Equations \eqref{struct27:eq:smoothcut} and \eqref{struct27:eq:routecut} give
\[
 s_0/8\le K[\partial_C(U)+(s_0/r(A_1))\partial_C(A_1)+4\epsilon s_0].
\]
Thus the ratio is at least $\min\{1/2048,1/(8K)-b-4\epsilon\}\ge\phi$.
This proves near-expansion.
For the work bound below, $\vartheta^{-1}=O(\log(2+\rho))$ and
\[
 t=O\bigl(\log(2+\rho)\log(P_{\max}/\tau_0)\bigr)
   =O\bigl(\log^2(2+\rho)\bigr).
\]
The proof-only bank and scalar $P_{\max}$ require no list of $\rho$ objects.
Other reciprocal parameters are polylogarithmic; vertex and volume factors
affect only $O(\log n)$-bit precision.
The static construction chooses admissible dyadic accuracies.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Finite arithmetic, sparse solvers, and work}\label{struct27:sec:finite}

\subsubsection{Finite Bernoulli skipping}\label{struct27:sec:skipping} For a row
suffix of length $N\le n$, invert the geometric CDF $\Pr(T>k)=(1-p)^k$ by binary
search on $1,\ldots,N+1$, where $N+1$ means exhaustion, using a uniform $b$-bit
dyadic $U$.
Repeated squaring on mesh $\zeta$ evaluates each power with error $O(n\zeta)$,
by telescoping products in $[0,1]$.
Coupling $U$ to a continuous uniform succeeds unless it lies within
$2^{-b}+O(n\zeta)$ of one of the $N$ CDF boundaries.
Thus per-call error is $O(n2^{-b}+n^2\zeta)$; choose $b=O(\log n)$ and
$\zeta=2^{-O(\log n)}$ for the polynomial horizon.
Each selected or exhausted position costs $O(\log^2 n)$ arithmetic and reads no
unselected label.
The coupling transfers the ideal Chernoff work cap to this finite sampler.

\subsubsection{Uniform replay error and centering} Every ideal replay of $F_0g$
has $D$-norm at most $\sqrt v$.
Choose final coordinate error
\begin{equation}\label{struct27:eq:astar}
 a_*\le\frac{\vartheta\tau_0}{2^{16}v(V+1)}.
\end{equation}
Budget $a_* /(4t+8)$ in $D$ norm per factor.
Contractions and exact centerings add these errors; $r_u\ge1$ gives coordinate
error at most $a_*$, meeting \Cref{struct27:lem:uniform} for $\Phi\ge\tau_0$.
Squared-norm error is at most $2\sqrt v\sqrt V a_*+Va_*^2$, small enough for
$a_{\rm stat}$ even when subtracting two norms.

After each sparse action round to a fixed dyadic grid, then center exactly.
This introduces only the integer volume denominator $V_A$, discarded at the next
rounding.
Round \emph{each coefficient--coordinate product before summing}, so
denominators never accumulate.
Polynomial magnitudes, $2^{O(h)}$ filter amplification and polynomially many
summands permit a common $O(\log n)$-bit grid fine enough for all errors.

\subsubsection{A finite grounded inverse} Use \Cref{lem:ram27-sparse-inverse}
and its grounded-block construction with ambient size $n+2$, including the
child, hub, and ground vertex.
The sample weights $1/p$, dyadic hub weights $\epsilon r_u$, and ground-edge
sums share an $O(\log n)$-bit dyadic mesh and polynomial positive ranges.
Together with the sparse count cap and the preceding common-grid rule for replay
right-hand sides, these satisfy the lemma's input hypotheses.

The lemma supplies fixed symmetric analytical inverse maps and finite
evaluations in $M^{1+o(1)}$ work with $O(\log n)$-bit words.
Choose its accuracy for the per-action allowance $a_*/(4t+8)$ in
\eqref{struct27:eq:astar}, and allocate factorization and random-work failure
budgets over the predetermined structural call horizon.
Its zero-pivot rule, operand guards, and work caps apply with the same
first-failure convention.
The lemma and its grounded-block reduction are proved independently of core
construction.

For the actual game block $L_{BB}$, a path to ground gives
$\lambda_{\min}(L_{BB})\ge w_{\min}/(v+1)^2$.
Its sparse exact residual certifies energy error using this bound.
If $C_0=-L_{AB}$, its row sums are at most $\gamma^{-1}r_A$.
Let $D_{B,A}$ be the diagonal matrix of total augmented edge weights from each
vertex of $B$ to $A$.
Weighted Cauchy gives
\[
 C_0^TD_A^{-1}C_0\preceq\gamma^{-1}D_{B,A}
 \preceq\gamma^{-1}L_{BB}.
\]
Thus squared $K_A$ output error is at most $\gamma$ times grounded solve energy
error.
Sparse residual checks certify replay precision.

\subsubsection{Exact flow on small sparse children} Common-scaled capacities
have $O(\log n)$ bits.
Give all network arcs cost zero and add a sink-to-source return arc of cost $-1$
and capacity $Q$, the sum of source-arc capacities, with zero demands.
Zero is feasible; adding or removing the return arc identifies minimum cost with
negative maximum-flow value.
Apply \cite[Theorem~1.1]{BCPKLPSS23:conf} with $U$ the maximum of two and all
capacities, including $Q$, and cost bound $C=2$.
Its $M^{1+o(1)}\log U\log C$ bound includes capacity scaling, so $\log U=O(\log n)$
gives $M^{1+o(1)}$ work even for tiny children.
Check capacity, conservation, integrality and the final residual cut by sparse
scans; handle empty and constant-size cases directly.

After local renumbering the input has $z=O(M\log n)$ bits.
Keep input/output integers in $O(\log n)$ bits and simulate the $O(\log^{O(1)}z)$-bit
fixed-point words of \cite[Section~2]{BCPKLPSS23:conf} with $n^{o(1)}$ ordinary
words and work per operation.
Store any wide addresses as multiword keys in a balanced dictionary pointing to
ordinary records.
There are at most as many keys as operations, so lookups cost $n^{o(1)}$ and
allocated memory is polynomial.
This preserves the work bound and the exact integral interface of
\cite[Theorem~1.1 and Lemma~4.1]{BCPKLPSS23:conf}.
\Cref{struct27:lem:candidate} supplies route decomposition.

\subsubsection{Total game work and failure probability} Keep at most $t+1$
active-set factors for prefix replay.
Candidates in each block share the current factor; their final seed deletion
needs no new factor.
Every rejected candidate pays one replay, smoothing, sort, exact flow, and
compressed route decomposition.
Independent validation replays the prefix and each frozen suffix.
A sufficient total bound is
\begin{equation}\label{struct27:eq:gamework}
 O((s+128J)(t+1)^2M^{1+o(1)}
       +s(t+1)M^{1+o(1)}).
\end{equation}
This includes filter application and every factor construction.
Predeclare a polynomial call horizon and abort before any work cap.
Allocate failure among probes, sample concentration and skipping coupling,
factors, candidate progress, validation, and random-integer tails.
New tapes follow a fixed input state; bound the first failed operation
conditionally on its past and union over the capped horizon.
No randomness is conditioned on future successful execution.

\subsection{A weighted fixed-birth pruner with atom-count work}
\label{struct27:sec:weighted-pruner}

Sample atoms can have large weight $1/p$, so work must depend on their count.
We first establish the mass and cut bounds, then implement them with a dynamic
forest.
Within a pruning theorem and its proof, $V$ denotes its birth vertex set.

\begin{theorem}\label{struct27:thm:weighted}
Let a weighted undirected birth graph have $m$ stored atoms with positive
integer weights, positive integer birth degrees $b$, volume $B$, and reference
conductance $\alpha\le1/2$, an inverse power of two.
An arbitrary adaptive sequence of distinct nonloop birth atoms may be fed as
genuine deletions.
Each feed deletes that entire atom at its full birth weight, at most once;
partial decrements of one atom are outside this interface.
With fed weight $k$, a deterministic structure removes birth volume at most
$16k/\alpha$.
Its surviving live graph satisfies
\[
 \partial_{\rm live}(X)\ge(\alpha/4)b(X)
 \quad\text{for }X\subseteq A,\ b(X)\le b(A)/2.
\]
For
\[
 H=2+ \ln(2B)/\ln(1+\alpha/8) ,
\]
total work is $O((m+|V|)H\log(2m+2|V|))$, independently of numeric weights
except their bit length and $H$.
No fed-weight budget is required for correctness or the volume bound.
\end{theorem}

Implement height by $2+ 16/\alpha  \log_2(2B) $, with
binary logarithms computed by bit lengths and comparison.
For the density-height bound below at $\theta=\phi/8$, use $2/\theta$ and
$\log_2\max\{1,M/(\theta d^2)\}$.

\subsubsection{State and the mass proof} Put $c_\star=2/\alpha$.
Initially $A=V$, $f=0$, and all levels are zero.
Private vertices are the survivors $A$; private edges include every birth edge
induced by $A$, including deleted ghosts.
Keep antisymmetric integral flow $|f(u,v)|\le cw_{uv}$, and
\begin{equation}\label{struct27:eq:weightedstate}
 \sigma_A(u)=c_\star\,w(E(u,V\setminus A))+2c_\star\deg_D(u),\quad
 m(u)=\sigma_A(u)+\sum_{v\in A}f(v,u),\quad
 e(u)=\max\{m(u)-b_u,0\}.
\end{equation}
Here $\deg_D$ counts all atoms fed so far at their birth weights, including
atoms whose other endpoint is no longer private.
Feeding weight $w$ adds $2cw$ at each endpoint still private; charge $4cw$ even
if one is gone.
Thus charged injection is $J=4ck=8k/\alpha$.
Maintain
\begin{equation}\label{struct27:eq:flowinvariants}
 m\ge0,\quad l(u)>0\Longrightarrow m(u)\ge b_u,\quad
 cw_{uv}-f(u,v)>0\Longrightarrow l(u)\le l(v)+1,
 \quad 0\le l\le H.
\end{equation}
Once absorbed, sink mass never leaves a surviving vertex.

Let $S_i=\{l\ge i\}$ and let $z_i$ be crossing weight between levels $i$ and
$i-1$.
If $S_H\ne\varnothing$, some nonempty $S_i$ has $z_i\le(\alpha/8)b(S_i)$:
otherwise successive volumes grow by $1+\alpha/8$ from at least one, exceeding
$B$.

Remove this upper set simultaneously, replacing crossing edge $u\to v$ by source
$cw$ at survivor $v$.
Its mass gain is $cw-f(u,v)\ge0$, zero across a level gap of at least two by
residual saturation.
Adjacent-level edges create at most $2cw$ each, totaling at most $b(S)/2$, while
removed positive-level sinks destroy at least $b(S)$.
For cumulative creation $C$ and removed birth volume $R$, every completed prefix
satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{struct27:eq:massbudget}
 C\le R/2,\qquad R\le J+C,\qquad R\le2J=16k/\alpha.
\end{equation}

At a zero-excess checkpoint, for smaller-reference $X\subseteq A$ let $x$ be its
private cut, $y$ its birth boundary outside $A$, and $z=\sum_{u\in X}\deg_D(u)$.
Birth expansion and feasible mass give
\[
 x+y\ge\alpha b(X),\qquad cy+2cz\le b(X)+cx.
\]
Since $1/c_\star=\alpha/2$, substitution gives $x-z\ge\alpha b(X)/4$.
Deleted crossing weight is at most $z$, proving the fixed-reference live
guarantee.

\subsubsection{The dynamic forest algorithm} Batch pushes along a
child-to-parent forest.
Each arc satisfies $l(u)=l(v)+1$ and stores residual capacity $cw_{uv}-f(u,v)$.
Lazy zeros may remain linked.
Before cutting, query and materialize its flow; unlinked flows are explicit.
Row queries test linkage in \emph{either} direction and read linked values from
the tree, never a stale explicit copy.

Each vertex has a row cursor reset only on relabel, incoming-child list, and one
active queue handle.
Validate private membership and excess before use; clear stale handles at
excess-interval end or removal.

Choose any active $v$.  Purge zero residual arcs on its root path,
repeating root finding after cuts, and let $w$ be its current root.
Process $v$ only while it has excess.
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*]
\item If $m(w)<b_w$, push
$a=\min\{e(v),b_w-m(w),\text{path residual minimum}\}$
along the root path, subtracting $a$ lazily from residuals.
Source and root masses change by $-a,+a$; internal masses stay fixed
and the root gains no excess.
The path is nonempty since $v$ has excess.
\item For a full root sink, scan its current row for positive residual
$w\to z$ with $l(w)=l(z)+1$.  If found, link it, leave the cursor there, and
repeat, purging extended-path zeros.  The trees are distinct: a
$z$--$w$ path would force $l(z)>l(w)$.
\item If no admissible arc remains, raise $l(w)$ by one, reset its cursor,
and materialize and cut \emph{all} incoming arcs.  Their children now
have the parent's level; advance their rejected slots.
Every residual neighbor had level at least old $l(w)$, so raising
preserves \eqref{struct27:eq:flowinvariants}.  This applies even to a full root
with zero excess.
\item On reaching height $H$, immediately prune a qualifying upper
set and restart the queue; otherwise continue with $v$.
\end{enumerate}

For pruning, mark the whole set and collect crossing atoms.
Query, materialize and cut \emph{all linked crossing atoms before any survivor
source update}.
Save their flows, then update sources, remove vertices and internal links, and
update handles.
No push interrupts this batch; only its completion is a checkpoint.
Strict descent prevents a surviving child from pointing into the removed upper
set.

\subsubsection{Cursor validity and the atom count} At a fixed sender level, a
rejected positive slot's neighbor is already as high and only rises.
Saturated slots reopen only by a reverse push, which also puts the neighbor too
high; parent relabels have the same effect.
Removal is permanent.
Thus each directed slot is scanned and selected $O(1)$ times per level: scans,
links and value queries total $O(mH)$, relabels $O(|V|H)$, and each link is cut
once.

Every positive push saturates a link, first fills a root sink, or ends its
source's excess interval; break ties arbitrarily.
A saturated link is purged before reuse.
Pushes leave internal masses unchanged and create no destination excess, so only
feeds and prune-boundary additions start excess intervals.
Each atom is fed and crosses a prune boundary at most once.
Hence there are $O(m+|V|)$ intervals and sink fills, and $O(mH+|V|)$ pushes.
Charge root queries to the next push, scan, link, cut, relabel or interval
completion.

Search levels downward to the first successful test, scanning only ultimately
removed rows: total row work is $O(m+|V|)$ and empty-level work $O(|V|H)$.
Materialize each crossing atom once.
Dynamic trees add one logarithm per operation, proving
\Cref{struct27:thm:weighted} and termination even behind full zero-excess roots.

\subsection{Adaptive degree-bias repair and the local induced core}

Return to the sampled child $C$, with $V=r(C)$.
Repair uses the global raw-adjacency bound to convert a sampled induced expander
into an original induced core.
We first prove this repair step; \Cref{struct27:lem:bootstrap} then obtains its
input by trimming the game's near-expander.

\begin{lemma}\label{struct27:lem:repair}
Let $0<\alpha\le1/2$ be an inverse power of two, with $\alpha^{-1}$ bounded by a
fixed power of $\log n$.
Let $C$ be simple, $d_C\le r\le2d_C$, and let a sample satisfy the raw error in
\eqref{struct27:eq:laperror} and $\widehat d_C\le r$.
A sample-adaptive $A_0$ has $r(A_0)\ge3V/4$, and its sampled induced graph
completed to degree $2r$ is an $\alpha$-expander.
Suppose original degree estimates have error at most $\delta_0r_u/4$ and are
maintained by exact public row decrements.
If
\[
 0<\delta_0\le\alpha/100,\quad \log n\ge \log_2(2v) ,\quad
 \epsilon\le\alpha\delta_0/(256S\log n),\quad S\ge1,
\]
then sparse work $M^{1+o(1)}$ plus scans of first publicly removed original rows
produces an original induced $\alpha/12$-core $R$, removing reference volume at
most $576\epsilon^2V/(\alpha\delta_0^2)$.
\end{lemma} \begin{proof} Give \Cref{struct27:thm:weighted} the fixed birth
graph on $A_0$, with sample edges and loops $2r_u-\widehat d_{A_0}(u)\ge r_u$.
Initialize public $R=A_0$ and keep it inside the private survivor set.
Removing a row from $R$ scans its original root row, independently of private
ghost-flow removal.

A public row is BAD if $|\widehat d_R(u)-\widetilde d_R(u)|>\delta_0r_u/2$.
Both degree counters exclude the reference-completion loops.
Choose one BAD row and feed every not-yet-fed birth nonloop edge incident to it.
Complete the pruner batch, then publicly remove that row and all privately
pruned rows.
A forced BAD row may remain private, with its state and loops untouched.
Finish all public updates before making the next BAD decision.

Let $V_B$ be reference volume of chosen BAD rows and $V_P=r(A_0\setminus R)$.
Fed weight $k\le V_B$ since birth nonloop degree is at most $r$.
Private pruning removes $r$-volume at most $8k/\alpha$, so
\begin{equation}\label{struct27:eq:repairvp}
 V_P\le(8/\alpha+1)V_B\le9V_B/\alpha.
\end{equation}
This holds for every completed prefix with no small-budget premise.

For the analysis of $V_B$, put $E=D^{-1/2}(\widehat A-A_C)D^{-1/2}$ and order
$P=A_0\setminus R$ by completed batches.
A chosen BAD row has true bias greater than $\delta_0r_u/4$, whose normalized
value is its coordinate of $ED^{1/2}\oone_{A_0}-ED^{1/2}\oone_{P_{\rm
earlier}}$; $P_{\rm earlier}$ contains only rows removed before its decision.
The second term on chosen rows is therefore a restriction of the strict
block-lower part $T(E)D^{1/2}\oone_P$.
Neither $E$ nor $T(E)$ is constructed.
For every block order,
\begin{equation}\label{struct27:eq:triangular}
 \norm{T(E)}\le \log n\norm{E}.
\end{equation}
To prove this, recursively bisect the ordered blocks.
At each depth, lower off-diagonal rectangles have disjoint rows and columns, so
their direct sum has norm at most $\norm{E}$.
Sum over at most $\log n$ depths.
This is valid for an order chosen after seeing $E$.

Consequently
\[
 (\delta_0^2/16)V_B\le2\epsilon^2V+2\log^2 n\epsilon^2V_P.
\]
Combine \eqref{struct27:eq:repairvp} and
$\epsilon^2\le\alpha\delta_0^2/(576\log^2 n)$ to absorb the last term:
\begin{equation}\label{struct27:eq:repairloss}
 V_B\le64\epsilon^2V/\delta_0^2,\qquad
 V_P\le576\epsilon^2V/(\alpha\delta_0^2).
\end{equation}

At termination every edge from $R$ to a BAD row still private has been fed.
For $U\subseteq R$, $r(U)\le r(R)/2$, both sides of the private cut have
$b$-volume at least $2r(U)$, where $b=2r$.
Hence the sampled live public cut is at least $(\alpha/2)r(U)$; use only
$(\alpha/6)r(U)$.
Final degree bias is $|\widehat d_R-d_R|\le\delta_0r$, and the raw norm gives
$|\oone_U^T(\widehat A-A_C)\oone_U|\le\epsilon r(U)$ for every adaptive $U$.
Degree sum minus internal adjacency yields
$|\widehat\partial_R(U)-\partial_R(U)|\le(\delta_0+\epsilon)r(U)$.
Since $\delta_0+\epsilon\le\alpha/12$, the original bound follows.

Sparse degree counters and a BAD queue update only affected rows.
Each sample edge is fed once.
Only first public removal scans an original row; later private removal cannot
scan it again.
Since $C$ is simple and $r\le2d_C$, the weighted birth volume is at most
$2V\le4n^2$.
Its height is therefore $n^{o(1)}$, and \Cref{struct27:thm:weighted} costs
$M^{1+o(1)}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}\label{struct27:lem:bootstrap}
In the near branch of \Cref{struct27:lem:game}, set
\begin{equation}\label{struct27:eq:bootstrapparam}
\begin{gathered}
 \alpha=\phi/8,\quad\delta_0=\alpha^2/256,\quad
 e\le\min\{1/(4096\log n),\delta_0/16\},\\
 \epsilon\le\min\{e/4,\alpha\delta_0/(256S\log n),1/(4096K)\},
\end{gathered}
\end{equation}
all inverse powers of two where appropriate.
An original induced core of reference conductance at least $\alpha/12$ remains
after removing less than $V/(16S)$ reference volume.
Removed root volume, and hence original row work, is less than $w(C)/(2S)$.
Other work is $M^{1+o(1)}$.
\end{lemma} \begin{proof} Complete the original-vertex rows of the
sample-plus-hub graph to $2r$.
To trim $A_1$, let $b_u$ now denote its augmented boundary supply and put
$b(S)=\sum_{u\in S}b_u$.
Use sparse maximum flow with sink capacity $\phi r_u/2$ and routing-edge
capacities equal to their sampled weights.
Route only on sampled edges induced by original vertices of $A_1$; the hub and
excluded originals are boundary sources, never transit.
Since $B_1=\partial_C(A_1)\le r(C\setminus A_1)/\beta$, total source is at most
$B_1+2\epsilon V$.
For the residual set $S_1$ reachable from unsaturated sources (empty if none),
the mincut gives
\[
 \widehat\partial_{A_1}(S_1)+(\phi/2)r(S_1)\le b(S_1),\qquad
 r(S_1)\le2(B_1+2\epsilon V)/\phi.
\]
Remove $S_1$ and replace its saturated outward arcs by boundary supply of the
same weights at their surviving endpoints.
The survivors' old sources were saturated, so the remaining flow routes all new
boundary supply to the same sinks.
Put $A_0=A_1\setminus S_1$.

For smaller-$r$ $U\subseteq A_0$, let $x$ be its sampled internal cut and $y$
its full augmented boundary outside $A_0$.
Near-expansion and augmented domination give $x+y\ge\phi r(U)$; the retained
flow gives $y\le(\phi/2)r(U)+x$.
Hence $x\ge(\phi/4)r(U)$, giving conductance $\alpha=\phi/8$ after completion to
$2r$.

Loss so far divided by $V$ is at most
\[
 \frac{\beta\phi}{128S}+\frac1{64S}+\frac{\delta_0}{512\log nS},
\]
so $r(A_0)\ge3V/4$.
Discard the hub and use the integral sample with completion loops $2r-\widehat
d_{A_0}\ge r$.
The guard estimate error is $ew\le4er\le\delta_0r/4$, and exact public
decrements preserve it.

Initialize $A_0$ estimates by scanning committed removed root rows in
$C\setminus A_0$ once and decrementing retained neighbors via membership flags.
The displayed loss pays game removals and trimming; rejected seeds trigger no
scan.
Stamp scanned rows to prevent scanning these original rows again.
\Cref{struct27:lem:repair} removes at most
\[
 576\epsilon^2V/(\alpha\delta_0^2)
 \le9\alpha V/(1024\log^2 nS^2).
\]
The sum is less than $V/(16S)$.
Each removed row costs $w_u\le4r_u$; $V\le2w(C)$ gives the root-volume and
row-work bound.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Delayed original-label classification and static cover}

The recursion first finds vertex sets.
Assigning original labels only after the tree is fixed keeps its depth out of
the dense scan cost.

For $q_j>\tau$, put $S_j=AD_j^2$ and $t_j=q_j/(nS_j)$.
Peel current degrees below $t_j$, exporting at most $nt_j=q_j/S_j$ labels in one
original scan.
The nonempty remainder has exact root degrees $w$, minimum $d\ge\max\{1,t_j\}$,
and frozen arrays.
Let $q_0$ be its edge count and $W_0=2q_0$ its volume.
Set common root parameters
\begin{equation}\label{struct27:eq:phaseparam}
 \begin{gathered}
 d_* =\max\{1,d/4\},\quad\rho=\max\{1,n/d_*\},\quad
 J_0=1+ \log_2\max\{1,8W_0/d^2\} ,\\
 \chi=2^{- \log_2(256S_jJ_0) },\qquad S=32S_j.
 \end{gathered}
\end{equation}
Choose each accuracy as the largest inverse power of two satisfying all its
upper bounds, hence within a factor two of their minimum.
Before child tapes, choose \eqref{struct27:eq:phaseparam}, then
$b_\rho,\vartheta,P_{\max}$ and
\eqref{struct27:eq:gameparam1}--\eqref{struct27:eq:gameparam2}, then
$\alpha=\phi/8$, $\delta_0=\alpha^2/256$, and finally $e$ and $\epsilon$ in that
order from \eqref{struct27:eq:bootstrapparam}.
Thus $\phi$ is independent of the later $\epsilon$, and reciprocal accuracies
have fixed polylogarithmic exponents.
Use these parameters and the same sampling probability at every node of the
binary refinement tree.
Stop before calls with fewer than $d/8$ vertices; otherwise guard, stopping if
empty, then run the game and bootstrap its near branch.
CUT recurses on both surviving sides; CORE exports removed vertices and
terminates.
Fresh tapes follow the fixed child, and the guard supplies the common $d_*$.

By \eqref{struct27:eq:guard}, each accepted cut has both root-volume sides at
least $\eta w(C)/8$, and true boundary at most twice $\chi$ times its smaller
root-volume side.
Enforce the stated volume-balance test even on a bad-sample execution.

\begin{lemma}\label{struct27:lem:covercharge}
The next explicit residual has $q_{j+1}\le9q_j/(8S_j)\le q_j/2$ for sufficiently
large $A$.
This phase uses $O(q_j+n)$ full original scan work and $n^{1+o(1)}$ probes and
sample positions.
Its sparse work is $n^{1+o(1)}$.
\end{lemma} \begin{proof} Let $P_g$ be all guard-removed vertices, $F_g$ the
distinct labels whose other endpoint is still in the current node when their
first guard endpoint is removed, and $B_g$ all accepted-cut labels.
Every label incident to $P_g$ belongs to $F_g\cup B_g$: a terminal CORE cannot
be an ancestor of a later recursive node.
The removal bound in \Cref{struct27:lem:guard} and endpoint counting give
\[
 |F_g|\le3w(P_g)/8,\quad w(P_g)\le2(|F_g|+|B_g|),\quad
 w(P_g)\le8|B_g|,\quad |F_g|\le3|B_g|.
\]
Charge each cut to its smaller root-volume side.
Each vertex's next charged parent has at most half the preceding volume.
Split parents have volume at least $d^2/8$, so the sum of smaller-side volumes
is at most $J_0W_0$ and $|B_g|\le2\chi J_0W_0$.
For a small leaf $U$, simplicity gives $e(U)\le w(U)/16$ and root boundary at
least $7w(U)/8$.
Summing those boundaries counts $B_g\cup F_g$ at most twice, so adding
small-leaf internal labels multiplies that charge by at most $8/7$.
Terminal CORE input sets are disjoint; their root-volume losses total at most
$W_0/(64S_j)$.
The full post-peel residual is therefore at most
\[
 \frac{4|B_g|}{1-1/8}+\frac{W_0}{64S_j}
 \le\left(\frac{8\chi J_0}{1-1/8}+\frac1{64S_j}\right)W_0
 <q_0/(8S_j).
\]
Including first-peel exports proves the size recurrence.

Uniform balance bounds depth by $H_0=2+ (8/\eta)\log_2(2W_0/d) =n^{o(1)}$, even with guards before cuts.
Consequently $\sum_i|A_i|\le nH_0$ and $\sum_iw(A_i)\le W_0H_0$.
Probes cost $k\sum_i|A_i|$.
Common sampling probability satisfies $p\le C \log n/(\epsilon^2d_*)$ even when
clamped to one, and $d_*\ge q_j/(4nS_j)$.
Together with the sample-cost bound in \Cref{struct27:lem:sample}, this gives
\[
 p\sum_iw(C_i)\le CnS_j\log nH_0/\epsilon^2,
 \qquad\sum_i M_i+\text{all probe and stream work}\le n^{1+o(1)}.
\]
This includes discarded sample positions and exhausted streams, not only
accepted sample edges.
Stamped local IDs avoid an ambient $n$ clearing per call.
Sum \eqref{struct27:eq:gamework} and the sparse bootstrap cost as
$(\sum_iM_i)^{1+o(1)}$.

Guard scans occur once and terminal export allowances are disjoint.
After fixing the tree, classify each root label \emph{once}: equal retained-core
endpoint flags install it, otherwise it enters the residual.
This builds degrees and direct handles in $O(q_j+n)$, without routing labels
through tree depth.
\end{proof}

Repeat on the residual until size $\tau$.
\Cref{struct27:lem:covercharge} gives $\sum_jq_j\le2q$ and $O(\log(2+q/\tau))$
phases.
Cores are vertex-disjoint within phases and globally edge-disjoint, induced in
their phase residual; total vertex population is $n^{1+o(1)}$.
The $n^{1+o(1)}$ probe and sample touches per phase are $O(q_j)$ for
$q_j>n^{9/8}$, by asymptotic absorption of any fixed logarithmic power.

From $\rho\le4n^2S_j/q_j$ and \eqref{struct27:eq:phaseparam},
$J_0,\log(2+\rho)=O(D_j)$.
The game has $t=O(\log^2(2+\rho))$, hence
\[
 h_j^{-1}=O(\phi^{-1})
 =O(S_jJ_0\log^2(2+\rho))=O(D_j^5).
\]
The guard and first peel give retained reference minimum $q_j/(4nS_j)$.
\Cref{struct27:sec:finite}'s first-failure bound covers adaptive refinement and
residual phases, completing the static portion of \Cref{struct27:thm:main}.

\subsection{Unweighted fixed-birth pruning at a density-dependent height}

Simplicity and minimum degree give installed cores a smaller pruning height and
a charge per genuine deletion.
In the next lemma, $\theta>0$ is the level-growth threshold; the pruner uses
$\theta=\phi/8$.

\begin{lemma}\label{struct27:lem:densityheight}
In a simple private graph whose degrees are bounded by positive integer
references $r$, with $\sum r\le M$ and $\min r\ge d$, give vertices levels
$0,\ldots,H$.
Put $S_i=\{l\ge i\}$ and let $z_i$ count edges between levels $i$ and $i-1$.
If $S_H\ne\varnothing$ and
\[
 H\ge2+ \ln\max\{1,M/(\theta d^2)\}/\ln(1+\theta) ,
\]
some nonempty $S_i$, $i\ge1$, has $z_i\le\theta r(S_i)$.
It can be found by scanning only rows ultimately removed.
\end{lemma} \begin{proof} If all tests fail, simplicity gives $\theta
d|S_H|\le\theta r(S_H)<z_H \le|S_H|\,|\{l=H-1\}|$.
Thus the next level has more than $\theta d$ vertices and $r(S_{H-1})>\theta
d^2$.
Each further failed level has $r(S_{i-1})>(1+\theta)r(S_i)$, contradicting total
$M$ at this height.
Scan levels downward and stop at the first success; only rows in its upper set
have been read.
Empty-level bookkeeping costs $O(H)$.
The $d^2$ argument requires simplicity; \Cref{struct27:sec:weighted-pruner} uses
a different work proof for weighted parallel atoms.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}\label{struct27:thm:unweighted}
Let a simple unweighted birth graph have actual degrees $r>0$, volume $M=2q_b$,
minimum degree $d$, and conductance $\phi\le1/2$, an inverse power of two.
Define
\[
 \Delta=1+\ln\left(1+\frac{M}{(\phi/8)d^2}\right),\qquad
 H=O(\Delta/\phi)
\]
using \Cref{struct27:lem:densityheight}.
For any adaptive sequence of genuine deletions there is a deterministic
structure with live reference conductance at least $\phi/4$, total work
\[
 O(q_b+|V|+H+U\Delta/\phi^2),
\]
and $O(U/\phi)$ exported live labels.
All prices and references are fixed at birth.
Retire at the first $U\ge \phi M/128 $, exporting every remaining
live label.
\end{theorem} \begin{proof} Use the ghost source and mass state
\eqref{struct27:eq:weightedstate} with $b=r$, unit edge weights, and $c_\star=2/\phi$.
A genuine deletion removes public handles but retains private flow and adds $2c_\star$
at each endpoint; its injection is $4c_\star=8/\phi$.
Preserve \eqref{struct27:eq:flowinvariants}.
Choose the minimum-level vertex with excess and level below $H$.
On an admissible arc $u\to v$ push $a=\min\{e(u),c_\star-f(u,v),r_v\}$.
Minimum-level choice makes the recipient nonexcessive, so its new excess is at
most $r_v$.
Sending only excess preserves filled sinks.
When no admissible arc remains, relabel and reset the cursor; the weighted
forest's rejection argument applies unchanged.

When only height-$H$ excess remains, apply \Cref{struct27:lem:densityheight}
with $\theta=\phi/8$ and mark its entire upper set before removal.
Replace each crossing private edge by source $c_\star$ at its surviving endpoint.
The source-and-mass calculation leading to \eqref{struct27:eq:massbudget} gives,
for injection $J=8U/\phi$, cumulative creation $C$, and removed birth volume
$R$, $C\le R/2$, $R\le J+C$, and $R\le2J$.
Scan each removed birth row once.
At most $R$ live incident labels are exported, each once; historical ghosts are
never exported.
The feasible-cut calculation following \eqref{struct27:eq:massbudget} proves the
$\phi/4$ live guarantee without subtracting deleted incidences twice.

Put $B=J+C\le2J$.
Each ever-active vertex absorbs $r_u$ until removal, so $\sum_{\rm ever\
active}r_u\le B$ and scans/relabels cost $O(HB)$.
A push of $a$ lowers $\Psi=\sum e(u)l(u)\ge0$ by at least $a$, since its
nonexcessive recipient is one level lower.
For cumulative direct injection/creation $I_u$, we have $e(u)\le r_u+I_u$:
received pushes start at zero excess and add at most $r_u$.
All relabels together increase $\Psi$ by at most $H\sum_{\rm ever\
active}r_u+H\sum I_u\le2HB$; direct additions raise $\Psi$ by at most $HB$, and
removals only lower it apart from charged creation.
Thus pushed integral mass and push count are at most $3HB$.

Use per-level active lists.
Pushes lower the minimum by at most one; positive integral additions lower it by
at most $H$, charged to their amount.
Pair upward scans with these decreases and initialization: queue work is
$O(HB+H)$.
At most $R\le2J$ upper-set removals cost $O(HJ)$ in empty levels.
Total update work is $O(HJ+H)$, pathwise under adaptive deletions.

At retirement $q_b=M/2\le64U/\phi$, paying the final scan and exports.
Charge a frozen batch in full as genuine input at old birth prices, even if it
triggers retirement; its exports are not new deletions in this birth.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Installing the static cores and closing the interface}

The construction reference was chosen before the core was known.
Installation replaces it by actual original birth degrees and freezes those
degrees for pruning.

In a phase-$j$ core, each retained reference is at least $m_j=q_j/(4nS_j)$.
In a core with at least two vertices, singleton cuts have reference at least
$m_j$ on both sides, so actual birth minimum degree is at least $h_jm_j$.
Actual degree is no larger than the static reference, so actual-degree
conductance is at least $h_j$.
Omit singleton cores with no original label.
Round $\phi$ down within a factor two of $h_j$ and freeze it.
Birth volume is at most $2q_j$; hence
\[
 \frac{M}{(\phi/8)d^2}
 \le C\frac{n^2S_j^2}{h_j^3q_j},\qquad
 \Delta=O(D_j).
\]
\Cref{struct27:thm:unweighted} now gives \Cref{struct27:thm:main}'s deletion and
export prices.


\clearpage

\label{sec:ram27-symbols}

\caption{Symbols, in order of first appearance.}\label{tab:ram27-symbols}\\
\toprule
Symbol & Meaning \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
Symbol & Meaning \\
\midrule
\endhead
\bottomrule
\endfoot
$n,m,r$ & Vertices, labels, and rank $r=n-1$ of the working graph \\
$P$ & Central potential exponent, the integer fixed in \Cref{sec:ram27-logs} \\
$C,c_0$ & Magnitude exponent of the input data and confidence exponent \\
$\BB$ & Incidence matrix \\
$s$ & Density $s=r/m$ \\
$\ff$ & Stored flow \\
$\llambda$ & Tracking weights \\
$\yy$ & Vertex potential \\
$\mu$ & Barrier parameter \\
$\cc,\vvu^{\max}$ & Cost vector and upper capacity vector \\
$\zz$ & Local barrier scale \\
$\ww$ & Regularized weight $\ww=2s+\llambda$ \\
$\vvr$ & Row scale $\vvr=\zz/\ww$ \\
$\XXibar$ & Barrier gradient scale $\zz(\ff^{-1}-(\vvu^{\max}-\ff)^{-1})$ \\
$\DD$ & Barrier curvature scale $\zz^3(\ff^{-3}-(\vvu^{\max}-\ff)^{-3})$ \\
$\HH$ & Ordinary coefficient Laplacian $\BB^T\diag(\vvr^2)\BB$ \\
$\ttau$ & Edge leverage scores of $\HH$ \\
$\UU,\PPi$ & Scaled incidence $\diag(\vvr)\BB\HH^{-1/2}$ and the projection $\UU\UU^T$ \\
$\LL$ & Physical metric for the direction solve and residual norm \\
$\vvp$ & Logarithmic tracking error \\
$\ddelta$ & Margin of the tracking error to the boundary \\
$\ddeltabar$ & Stored margin \\
$\vvpbar$ & Positive stored reference scale comparable to $s+\ttau$ \\
$\vvs$ & Central error \\
$\vvsbar$ & Stored representative of the central error \\
$\ssigma$ & Relative change of the squared row scale in a frame, $(\vvr_e'/\vvr_e)^2-1$ \\
$\bb$ & Demand vector \\
$d_Z$ & Margin $1-|\vvsbar|$ of the stored central representative \\
$p$ & Tracking potential exponent, $p=512$ \\
$\psi_q$ & Potential kernel $(1-x)^{-q}+(1+x)^{-q}$ \\
$\Psi_R$ & Tracking potential $\sum_e\psi_p(\vvp_e/\kappa)$ \\
$\Psi_Z$ & Centrality potential $\sum_e\psi_P(\vvs_e)$ \\
$M$ & Frame scale, $10^5n^{1/4}\le M\le2\cdot10^5n^{1/4}$ \\
$h$ & Central step size, within a factor two of $c_hn^{-3/p}P^{-16}$ \\
$\beta_f$ & Flow step scale $h/M$ \\
$\beta_s$ & Sampled step scale $\gamma\beta_f$ \\
$B_*$ & Per-frame variation scale $\beta_f\sqrt{m/s}$ \\
$W_e$ & Frozen structural update price of an edge \\
$a_{\rm jump}$ & Fixed scalar jump allowance of the owner rates \\
$k_P$ & Coefficient of the central correction \\
$G_{Z,e}$ & Certified central gradient bound of an edge \\
$c_Z$ & Constant of the central correction cap \\
$\FF$ & Central correction \\
$\KK_0$ & Root-update coefficient $(C+q)/(\theta(1+b_{\ww}))$ \\
$T_0$ & Target of the root update \\
$C_{R,\rm out}$ & Threshold of the tracking potential at a valid pre-state \\
$\Delta_D$ & Direction mesh \\
$\mu_{\rm low}$ & Supplied lower bound on $\mu$ over an attempt range \\
$c_{\rm cen}$ & Rational constant of the central scheduler \\
$\rho_{\min}$ & Dyadic floor of the scheduler radii \\
$\tau_0$ & Residual cutoff of the owner, $\tau_0\asymp n^{9/8}$ \\
$\phi_{eo}$ & Owner gate weight of a label occurrence \\
$N_{\rm init}$ & Number of initial tokens, $O(m)$ \\
$N_{\rm sharp}$ & Sharp token cap $C_0mB(P,\log\log n)$ \\
$T_P,T_H$ & Approximate inverses of $P$ and of $\HH$ \\
$\XXi$ & Central ratio $\zz(\cc-\BB\yy)/(\mu\ww)=\XXibar+\varepsilon\vvs$ \\
$\XXi_0,\DD_0$ & Central ratio and curvature scale at the initial point \\
$\RR$ & Reduced-cost ratio $(\cc-\BB\yy)/(\mu\ww)$ \\
$\SS$ & Third-order barrier scale $\ff^{-3}-(\vvu^{\max}-\ff)^{-3}$ \\
$\ZZ$ & Squared barrier scale $\zz^2$ \\
$\YY$ & Weight ratio $3\llambda/\ww$ \\
$\kk$ & Step denominator $\DD\XXi+\YY(1-\DD\XXibar)$ \\
$\gg,\gg_0$ & Step right-hand side and its leading term \\
$\qq$ & Potential part of the step, $\zz\BB\xxdir/(\mu\ww)$ \\
$\xxdir$ & Potential direction of the solve \\
$\UUps,\LLam$ & Implemented and exact flow directions \\
$\dd$ & Movement scale $|\UUps|+|\pphi|$ \\
$\nnu$ & Central noise coefficient $(\gg_0-\qq)/(\varepsilon\theta)$ \\
$\pphi$ & Source of the central correction \\
$\iiota$ & Leverage correction $1-s/\llambda-\ttau/\widehat\ttau$ \\
$\TT$ & Selection scaling, $\TT_e=I_e/p_e$ \\
$\xxi$ & Selection noise $(\TT-1)\nnu$ \\
$\GG$ & Gradient of the tracking potential \\
$\EE$ & Scaled change $\UU^T\diag(\ssigma)\UU$ \\
$\QQ,\KK_0$ & Root-update operators of the local step \\
$\XX$ & Per-edge variation scale \\
$\II$ & Identity \\
$\MM,\QQ_\omega,\QQ_\mu$ & Observer matrices $\BB^T\diag(\omega)\BB$, $L_C(\omega)+D_{R_E}(\omega)$, $L_C(\mu)+D_{R_E}(\mu)$ \\
$\WW$ & Price diagonal $\diag(W_e)$ of the exact output \\
$\xx$ & Generic vector; iterates $\xx_i$ of the observer solves \\
$\varkappa$ & Scalar step argument of $\Phi$ and $\RR$ \\
$\epsilon_0$ & Prefix threshold of the upper-stopped drift argument \\
$\Delta_C$ & Common-grid gauge mesh of a core occurrence \\
$U_R,U_Z$ & Computable upper bounds of the two potentials \\
$d_0$ & Endpoint list depth $n^{64/p}\log^{D_0}n$ \\
$L_C,D_{R_E},A_{R_E}$ & Weighted Laplacian of the endpoint union and residual degree and adjacency \\
$Q_0$ & Reference operator $L_C(\omega)+D_{R_E}(\omega)$ \\
$\xxrand_j$ & Random vertex vectors of the observer \\
$P_M$ & Proxy $(1\pm\epsilon)M$ of the observer \\
$\mu_0,\mu_{\rm end}$ & Initial and final barrier parameters \\
$C_T$ & Phase constant of the attempt length \\
$T$ & Attempt length $C_T\sqrt r/\beta_s$ \\
$W_0$ & Root volume $2q_0$ of the core construction \\
$d_C,d_*$ & Degree in the core $C$ and a known lower bound on $\min d_C$ \\
$r_u$ & Guard reference degree of a vertex \\
$V_A$ & Reference volume $r(A)$ \\
$A_0,A_1$ & Sample-adaptive set and active near-expander of the pruning game \\
$\delta_0$ & Degree-estimate error parameter of the repair lemma \\
$S_1$ & Residual set reachable from unsaturated sources \\
\end{longtable}
